Diagonal cycles and anticyclotomic Iwasawa theory of modular forms
Francesc Castella, Kim Tuan Do

Abstract
. We construct a new anticyclotomic Euler system (in the sense of Jetchev–Nekovář–Skinner) for the Galois representation V f , χ V_{f,\chi} attached to a newform f f of weight k ≥ 2 k\geq 2 twisted by an anticyclotomic Hecke character χ \chi . We then show some arithmetic applications of the constructed Euler system, including new results on the Bloch–Kato conjecture in ranks zero and one, and a divisibility towards the Iwasawa–Greenberg main conjecture for V f , χ V_{f,\chi} . In particular, in the case where the base-change of f f to our imaginary quadratic field has root number + 1 +1 and χ \chi has higher weight (which implies that the complex L L -function L ⁡ ( V f , χ , s ) L(V_{f,\chi},s) vanishes at the center), our results show that the Bloch–Kato Selmer group of V f , χ V_{f,\chi} is nonzero, and if a certain distinguished class κ f , χ \kappa_{f,\chi} is nonzero, then the Selmer group is one-dimensional. Such applications to the Bloch–Kato conjecture for V f , χ V_{f,\chi} were left wide open by the earlier approaches using Heegner cycles and/or Beilinson–Flach classes. Our construction is based instead on a generalisation of the Gross–Kudla–Schoen diagonal cycles.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11G05; Secondary 11G40

Introduction

Let
f=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}q^{n}\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N_{f}))
be an elliptic newform of even weight
k\geq 2
, and let
p\nmid 6N_{f}
be a prime. Let
K/\mathbf{Q}
be an imaginary quadratic field in which
p
splits. Let
L
be a number field containing
K
and the Fourier coefficients of
f
, and let
\mathfrak{P}
be a prime of
L
above
p
at which
f
is ordinary, i.e.
v_{\mathfrak{P}}(a_{p})=0
. Let
\chi
be an anticyclotomic Hecke character of
K
, and consider the conjugate self-dual
G_{K}={\rm Gal}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}/K)
-representation

V_{f,\chi}:=V_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\chi^{-1},

where
V_{f}^{\vee}
is the contragredient of Deligne’s
\mathfrak{P}
-adic Galois representation associated to
f
.

We prove, among other results, the following applications to the Bloch–Kato conjecture for
V_{f,\chi}
: Under mild hypothesis on
f
and
\chi
, the nonvanishing of the Rankin–Selberg
L
-function
L(f/K,\chi,s)
at the center
s=k/2
implies that the dimension of the associated Bloch–Kato Selmer group is
0
; and when this central
L
-value vanishes, the nonvanishing of a distinguished class
\kappa_{f,\chi}
implies that the dimension of the associated Bloch–Kato Selmer group is
1
. These results are applications of the main contribution of this paper, which is the construction of a new anticyclotomic Euler system for
V_{f,\chi}
.

Our construction is based on a generalisation of the diagonal cycles introduced by Gross–Kudla
[
GK92
]
and Gross–Schoen
[
GS95
]
, and studied more recently by Darmon–Rotger and Bertolini–Seveso–Venerucci (see
[
BDR+22
]
).

0.1.
Main results

Assume that the discriminant
D_{K}
of
K
satisfies
(D_{K},N_{f})=1
.
Write
N_{f}=N^{+}N^{-}
with
N^{+}
(resp.
N^{-}
) divisible only by primes that are split (resp. inert) in
K
, and assume that

(0.1)

N^{-}
is squarefree.

Denote by
\nu(N^{-})
the number of prime factors of
N^{-}
, and assume also that

(0.2)

\chi
has conductor
c\mathcal{O}_{K}
with
(c,pN_{f})=1
.

Under hypotheses (
0.1
) and (
0.2
), it is known that the sign
\epsilon(f,\chi)
in the functional equation for
L(f/K,\chi,s)
(relating its values at
s
and
k-s
) depends only on the global root number of the base-change of
f
to
K
, given by

\epsilon(f/K)=-(-1)^{\nu(N^{-})},

and the infinity type
(-j,j)
of
\chi
. According to these, the values of
\epsilon(f,\chi)
are as in the following table
1
1

1

Because
L(f/K,\chi,s)=L(f/K,\chi^{\mathbf{c}},s)
, where
\chi^{\mathbf{c}}
is the composition of
\chi
with the action of complex conjugation, without loss of generality we may assume
j\geq 0
.
:

\epsilon(f/K)=-1

\epsilon(f/K)=+1

0\leq j<k/2

-1

+1

+1

0.1.1.
The Euler system

Throughout the remainder of this Introduction,
assume that
f
and
K
satisfy the following hypotheses:

(h1)

f
is ordinary and non-Eisenstein at
\mathfrak{P}
;

(h2)

p=\mathfrak{p}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}
splits in
K
;

(h3)

p\nmid h_{K}
, where
h_{K}
is the class number of
K
.

For every positive integer
n
, let
K[n]
be the maximal
p
-subextension of the ring class field of
K
of conductor
n
. Denote by
\mathcal{N}
the set of squarefree products of primes
\mathfrak{l}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
split in
K
with
\ell=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{l})
prime to
p
.

Theorem A

(Theorem
2.3.2
)
.

There exists a family of cohomology classes

z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}\in H^{1}(K[mp^{r}],T_{f,\chi})

indexed by the ideals
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{N}
with
m=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{m})
coprime to
p
and
r\geq 0
, where
T_{f,\chi}
is a certain
G_{K}
-stable
\mathcal{O}
-lattice inside
V_{f,\chi}
, such that

{\rm cor}_{K[mp^{r+1}]/K[mp^{r}]}(z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r+1})=z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}

for all
r\geq 0
, and for every split prime
\mathfrak{l}
of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
of norm
\ell
with
(\ell,mp)=1
we have the tame norm relation

{\rm cor}_{K[m\ell p^{r}]/K[mp^{r}]}(z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{l},r})=P_{\mathfrak{l}}({\rm Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}})\,z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r},

where
P_{\mathfrak{l}}(X)=\det(1-{\rm Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}}X\,|\,V_{f,\chi}^{\vee}(1))
, and
{\rm Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}}
is the geometric Frobenius.

The system of classes of Theorem
A
defines an
anticyclotomic Euler system
in the sense of Jetchev–Nekovář–Skinner
[
JNS
]
for the conjugate self-dual representation
V_{f,\chi}
. Significantly extending Kolyvagin’s methods, the general theory developed in
op. cit.
provides a machinery that bounds Selmer groups for conjugate self-dual representations
V
from the input of a non-trivial anticyclotomic Euler system for
V
. The Selmer group being bounded depends on the local condition at
p
satisfied by the Euler system classes, and by varying certain elements in the construction of
z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}
, we produce in fact two different anticyclotomic Euler systems for
V_{f,\chi}
, differing by their local conditions at the primes above
p
.

To describe this, recall that by
\mathfrak{P}
-ordinarity of
f
, the Galois representation
V_{f}^{\vee}
restricted to a decomposition group
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}\subset G_{\mathbf{Q}}
fits into a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow V_{f}^{\vee,+}\rightarrow V_{f}^{\vee}\rightarrow V_{f}^{\vee,-}\rightarrow 0,

where
V_{f}^{\vee,\pm}\simeq L_{\mathfrak{P}}
, with the
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
-action on
V_{f}^{\vee,-}
given by the unramified character sending the arithmetic Frobenius
{\rm Frob}^{-1}_{p}
to
\alpha_{p}
, the unit root of
x^{2}-a_{p}x+p^{k-1}
. Put

V_{f,\chi}^{\pm}:=V_{f}^{\vee,\pm}(1-k/2)\otimes\chi^{-1}.

Then more generally, we construct:

•

An anticyclotomic Euler system
\{z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}^{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}\}_{\mathfrak{m},r}

with the local condition at the primes
w|p
defined by

H^{1}_{\rm ord}(K[mp^{r}]_{w},V_{f,\chi}):={\rm ker}\bigl(H^{1}(K[mp^{r}]_{w},V_{f,\chi})\rightarrow H^{1}(K[mp^{r}]_{w},V_{f,\chi}^{-})\bigr).

•

An anticyclotomic Euler system
\{z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}^{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}\}_{\mathfrak{m},r}
for the local condition at the primes
w|p
defined by

\begin{cases}H^{1}(K[mp^{r}]_{w},V_{f,\chi})&\textrm{if $w|\mathfrak{p}$,}\\[1.99997pt]
0&\textrm{if $w|\bar{\mathfrak{p}}$.}\end{cases}

Using the Panchiskin condition, it can be shown that at least one of these classes land in the Bloch–Kato Selmer group
{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K[mp^{r}],V_{f,\chi})
, namely the class

\kappa_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}:=\begin{cases}z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}^{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}&\textrm{if
$j\geq k/2$,}\\[3.00003pt]
z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}^{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}&\textrm{if $0\leq j<k/2$.}\end{cases}

0.1.2.
Applications to the Bloch–Kato conjecture in rank
1

Put

\kappa_{f,\chi}:={\rm cor}_{K[1]/K}(\kappa_{f,\chi,(1),0})\in{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})

From the general Euler system machinery of
[
JNS
]
applied to the construction of Theorem
A
we deduce in particular the following result. Let
\mathcal{O}
be the ring of integers of
L_{\mathfrak{P}}
. We say that
f
has
big image
if for a certain Galois stable
\mathcal{O}
-lattice
T_{f}^{\vee}\subset V_{f}^{\vee}
, the image of
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
in
{\rm Aut}_{\mathcal{O}}(T_{f}^{\vee})
contains a conjugate of
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
.

Theorem B

(Theorem
5.7.1
)
.

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N_{f}))
be a newform and
\chi
an anticyclotomic Hecke character of
K
as above of infinity type
(-j,j)
. Assume that

\epsilon(f/K)=+1\quad\textrm{and}\quad j\geq k/2,

which implies
L(f/K,\chi,k/2)=0
. Assume also that
p>k-2
,
\bar{\rho}_{f}
is
p
-distinguished, and
f
has big image.
Then

\dim_{L_{\mathfrak{P}}}{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})\geq 1.

Moreover, if the class
\kappa_{f,\chi}

is nonzero, then

{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})=L_{\mathfrak{P}}\cdot\kappa_{f,\chi}.

By the Gross–Zagier formula for the modified diagonal cycles introduced in
[
GK92
,
GS95
]

(a special case of the arithmetic Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture for
{\rm SO}(3)\times{\rm SO}(4)
) proved by Yuan–Zhang–Zhang
[
YZZ
]
in certain cases, the non-triviality of
\kappa_{f,\chi}
is expected to be governed by the nonvanishing of
L^{\prime}(f/K,\chi,k/2)
, and hence Theorem
B
provides strong evidence towards the Bloch–Kato conjecture for
V_{f,\chi}
in analytic rank
1
.

Our methods also yield an analogue of Theorem
B
in the
“indefinite case”
\epsilon(f/K)=-1
and
0\leq j<k/2
(indeed, for
N^{-}=1
this follows immediately from Theorem
6.6.1
),
but in this case such result can also be obtained from the Euler system of (generalised) Heegner cycles
[
Nek92
,
CH18a
]
.

0.1.3.
Applications to the Bloch–Kato conjecture in rank
0

We now turn our attention to the cases where
\epsilon(f,\chi)=+1
, so the central value
L(f/K,\chi,k/2)
is expected to be generically nonzero. Put

\kappa^{\prime}_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}:=\begin{cases}z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}^{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}&\textrm{if $j\geq k/2$,}\\[3.00003pt]
z_{f,\chi,\mathfrak{m},r}^{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}&\textrm{if $0\leq j<k/2$,}\end{cases}

and
\kappa_{f,\chi}^{\prime}:={\rm cor}_{K[1]/K}(\kappa^{\prime}_{f,\chi,(1),0})
. Building on the reciprocity law for diagonal cycles by Bertolini–Seveso–Venerucci
[
BSV22
]
,
we show that the class
\kappa_{f,\chi}^{\prime}
is non-crystalline at
p

precisely when
L(f/K,\chi,k/2)\neq 0
.
Together with the machinery of
[
JNS
]
applied to the anticyclotomic Euler system of Theorem
A
extending
\kappa_{f,\chi}^{\prime}
, we thus deduce in particular the following cases of the Bloch–Kato conjecture in analytic rank
0
.

Theorem C

(Theorem
5.5.1
)
.

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N_{f}))
be a newform and
\chi
an anticyclotomic Hecke character of
K
as above. Assume that
\epsilon(f/K)=+1
and
p>k-2
. Then

L(f/K,\chi,k/2)\neq 0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})=0,

and hence the Bloch–Kato conjecture holds in this case.

Note that the nonvanishing of
L(f/K,\chi,k/2)
implies that
\epsilon(f,\chi)=+1
, and so without loss of generality, the character
\chi
in Theorem
C
may be assumed to have infinity type
(-j,j)
with
0\leq j<k/2
. Similarly as in the rank
1
case, our methods also yield an analogue of Theorem
C
in the indefinite case (see Theorem
6.5.1
).

Finally, we note
that results also include the proof of a divisibility towards the anticyclotomic Iwasawa Main Conjecture for
V_{f,\chi}
, giving in particular a new proof of the main result of
[
BD05
]
(see Theorem
5.6.1
).

0.2.
Relation to previous works

Starting with the landmark results by Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin
[
GZ86
,
Kol88
]
(see also
[
BD90
]
), and followed by their vast generalisations by Zhang
[
Zha97
]
, Tian
[
Tia03
]
, Nekovář
[
Nek07
]
, Yuan–Zhang–Zhang
[
YZZ13
]
and others, the Euler system of Heegner points and Heegner cycles has been a key ingredient in the study of the arithmetic of
V_{f,\chi}
under the Heegner hypothesis

\epsilon(f/K)=-1.

Classical Heegner cycles account for the cases where the anticyclotomic character
\chi
has finite order (i.e.,
j=0
), but using their new variant by Bertolini–Darmon–Prasanna
[
BDP13
]
, one obtains classes controlling the arithmetic of
{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})
in the following cases:

(1
st
quadrant)

\epsilon(f/K)=-1,\quad 0\leq j<k/2.

In another major advance, Bertolini–Darmon
[
BD05
]
exploited congruences between modular forms on different quaternion algebras and the Cerednik–Drinfeld theory of interchange of invariants to realise the Galois representation (on finite quotients of)
T_{f,\chi}
in the torsion of the Jacobian of certain Shimura curves. This allowed them to still use the Heegner point construction in situations where
\epsilon(f/K)=+1
. Together with the extension to higher weights by Chida–Hsieh
[
CH15
]
, these methods yielded a proof of many cases of the Bloch–Kato conjecture in analytic rank
0
when

(2
nd
quadrant)

\epsilon(f/K)=+1,\quad 0\leq j<k/2.

under a certain “level-raising” hypothesis. More recently, the Euler system of Beilinson–Flach classes constructed by Lei–Loeffler–Zerbes
[
LLZ14
,
LLZ15
]
and Kings–Loeffler–Zerbes
[
KLZ17
,
KLZ20
]
(inspired in part by earlier results of Bertolini–Darmon–Rotger
[
BDR15a
,
BDR15b
]
) provided an alternative approach to similar rank
0
results under some hypotheses (among other applications).

On the other hand, exploiting the variation of (generalised) Heegner cycles in
p
-adic families, the first author and Hsieh
[
CH18a
,
Cas20
]
, and more recently Kobayashi
[
Kob
]
, obtained results on the Bloch–Kato conjecture for
V_{f,\chi}
in rank
0
in the cases

(3
rd
quadrant)

\epsilon(f/K)=-1,\quad j\geq k/2.

Contrastingly, in the cases where

(4
th
quadrant)

\epsilon(f/K)=+1,\quad j\geq k/2,

the conjectures of Beilinson–Bloch and Bloch–Kato predict the existence of non-trivial classes in
{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})
coming from geometry (since
\epsilon(f,\chi)=-1
and therefore
L(f/K,\chi,k/2)=0
), but the construction of such classes seems to fall outside of all the aforementioned methods.

The anticyclotomic Euler system constructed in this paper allows us to fill this gap, while also providing a new approach
to the aforementioned results in other cases:

\epsilon(f/K)=-1

\epsilon(f/K)=+1

1
st
quadrant

2
nd
quadrant

0\leq j<k/2

[
Kol88
]
,
[
Tia03
]
,
[
Nek07
]
, etc.

[
BD05
]
,
[
CH15
]
,
[
KLZ17
]
, etc.

Theorem
6.6.1

Theorem
5.5.1

3
rd
quadrant

4
th
quadrant

j\geq k/2

[
CH18a
]
,
[
Cas20
]
,
[
Kob
]
, etc.

-

Theorem
6.5.1

Theorem
5.7.1

In future work, we intend to generalise our construction to totally real fields, a setting in which one finds even more cases where the arithmetic of Rankin–Selberg convolutions falls outside the scope of Heegner cycles and/or Beilinson–Flach classes.

Part I
The Euler system

1.
Preliminaries

1.1.
Modular curves and Hecke operators

We give a precise description of the modular curves and Hecke operators
that will appear in our construction. The main references for this section are
[
Kat04
, §2]
,
[
BSV22
, §2]
, and
[
ACR21
, §2]
, where more details can be found.

1.1.1.
Modular curves

Let
M,N,u,v
be positive integers such that
M+N\geq 5
. Define
Y(M,N)
to be the affine modular curve over
\mathbf{Z}[1/MN]
representing the functor

S\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{isomorphism classes of triples }(E,P,Q)\text{ where $E$ is an elliptic curve over $S$,}\\
\text{$P$, $Q$ are sections of $E$ over $S$ such that $M\cdot P=N\cdot Q=0$; and the map}\\
\mathbf{Z}/M\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}/N\mathbf{Z}\rightarrow E,\text{ sending }(a,b)\mapsto a\cdot P+b\cdot Q\text{ is injective}\end{array}\right\}

for
\mathbf{Z}[1/MN]
-schemes
S
. More generally, define the affine modular curve
Y(M(u),N(v))
over
\mathbf{Z}[1/MNuv]
representing the functor

S\mapsto\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{isomorphism classes of quintuples }(E,P,Q,C,D)\text{ where $(E,P,Q)$ is as above,}\\
\text{$P\in C$ is a cyclic subgroup of $E$ of order $Mu$,}\\
\text{$Q\in D$ is a cyclic subgroup of $E$ of order $Nv$ such that}\\
\text{$C$ is complementary to $Q$ and $D$ is complementary to $P$}\end{array}\right\}

for
\mathbf{Z}[1/MNuv]
-schemes
S
. When either
u=1
or
v=1
, we drop them from the notation.

Let
\mathbf{H}
be the Poincaré upper half-plane and define the modular group:

\Gamma(M(u),N(v))=\left\{\gamma\in\mathop{\rm SL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z})\text{ such that }\gamma\equiv\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\text{ mod }\begin{pmatrix}M&Mu\\
Nv&N\end{pmatrix}\right\}.

The Riemann surface
Y(M,N)(\mathbf{C})
admits a complex uniformisation:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(\mathbf{Z}/M\mathbf{Z})^{\times}\times\Gamma(M,N)\backslash\mathbf{H}&\xrightarrow{\sim}&Y(M,N)(\mathbf{C})\\
(m,z)&\mapsto&(\mathbf{C}/\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{Z}z,mz/M,1/N),\end{array}

and similarly for
Y(M(u),N(v))(\mathbf{C})
.

Let
\ell
be a prime. There is an isomorphism of
\mathbf{Z}[1/\ell MN]
-schemes:

\begin{array}[]{ccl}\varphi_{\ell}:Y(M,N(\ell))&\rightarrow&Y(M(\ell),N)\\
(E,P,Q,C)&\mapsto&(E/NC,P+NC,\ell^{-1}(Q)\cap C+NC,(\ell^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}\cdot P+NC)/NC)),\end{array}

which under the complex uniformisation is induced by the map
(m,z)\mapsto(m,\ell\cdot z)
.

1.1.2.
Degeneracy maps

We have the natural degeneracy maps

\textstyle{Y(M,N\ell)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{\ell}}
\textstyle{Y(M,N(\ell))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{\ell}}
\textstyle{Y(M,N)}
\textstyle{Y(M\ell,N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\check{\mu}_{\ell}}
\textstyle{Y(M(\ell),N)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\check{\nu}_{\ell}}
\textstyle{Y(M,N),}

where
\mu_{\ell}(E,P,Q)=(E,P,\ell\cdot Q,\mathbf{Z}\cdot Q)
,
\nu_{\ell}(E,P,Q,C)=(E,P,Q)
, and
\check{\mu}_{\ell}
,
\check{\nu_{\ell}}
are defined similarly. Put

\begin{split}\text{pr}_{1}:=\nu_{\ell}\circ\mu_{\ell}:Y(M,N\ell)&\rightarrow Y(M,N),\\
(E,P,Q)&\mapsto(E,P,\ell\cdot Q)\end{split}

and

\displaystyle\text{pr}_{\ell}:=\check{\nu}_{\ell}\circ\varphi_{\ell}\circ\mu_{\ell}:Y(M,N\ell)

\displaystyle\rightarrow Y(M,N)

\displaystyle(E,P,Q)

\displaystyle\mapsto(E/N\mathbf{Z}\cdot Q,P+N\mathbf{Z}\cdot Q,Q+N\mathbf{Z}\cdot Q).

On the complex upper half plane
\mathbf{H}
, the map
\text{pr}_{1}
(resp.
\text{pr}_{\ell}
) is induced by the identity (resp. multiplication by
\ell
). Moreover,
\mu_{\ell},\check{\mu}_{\ell},\nu_{\ell},\check{\nu}_{\ell},\text{pr}_{1},\text{pr}_{\ell}
are all finite étale morphisms of
\mathbf{Z}[1/MN\ell]
-schemes.

1.1.3.
Relative Tate modules and Hecke operators

Let
S
be a
\mathbf{Z}[1/MN\ell p]
-scheme where
p
is a fixed prime. For each
\mathbf{Z}[1/MN\ell p]
-scheme
X
, denote the base change
X_{S}=X\times_{\mathbf{Z}[1/MN\ell p]}S
. Notate
A=A_{X}
to be either the locally constant sheaf
\mathbf{Z}/p^{m}\mathbf{Z}(j)
or the locally constant
p
-adic sheaf
\mathbf{Z}_{p}(j)
on
X_{\et}
for some fixed
j\in\mathbf{Z}
and
m\geq 1
.

For the ease of notation, we may write
\cdot
for
M(u),N(v)
(i.e.
Y(\cdot)=Y(M(u),N(v))
). Denote by
E(\cdot)
the universal elliptic curve over
Y(\cdot)
. Then one obtains a natural degree
\ell
isogeny of universal elliptic curves under the base change by
\varphi_{\ell}^{*}E(M(\ell),N)\rightarrow Y(M,N(\ell))
:

\lambda_{\ell}:E(M,N(\ell))\rightarrow\varphi_{\ell}^{*}(E(M(\ell),N).

Denote by
v_{\cdot}:E(\cdot)_{S}\rightarrow Y(\cdot)_{S}
the structure map. We also use
\nu_{\ell},\check{\nu}_{\ell}
and
\lambda_{\ell}
for the base change to
S
of the corresponding degeneracy maps. Set:

\mathscr{T}_{\cdot}(A)=R^{1}v_{\cdot*}\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1)\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}A\text{ and }\mathscr{T}_{\cdot}^{*}(A)=\text{Hom}_{A}(\mathscr{T}_{\cdot}(A),A)

where
R^{q}v_{\cdot*}
is the
q
-th right derivative of
v_{\cdot*}:E(\cdot)_{\et}\rightarrow Y(\cdot)_{\et}
. When
A=\mathbf{Z}_{p}
, this gives the relative Tate module of the universal elliptic curve, in which case we will drop the
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
from the notation.

Fix an integer
r\geq 0
. The (perfect) cup product pairing combined with the relative trace

\mathscr{T}_{\cdot}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathscr{T}_{\cdot}\rightarrow R^{2}v_{\cdot*}\mathbf{Z}_{p}(2)\cong\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1)

allows us to identify
\mathscr{T}_{\cdot}(-1)
with
\mathscr{T}_{\cdot}^{*}
.
Put

\mathscr{L}_{\cdot,r}(A)=\text{Tsym}^{r}_{A}\mathscr{T}_{\cdot}(A),\quad\mathscr{S}_{\cdot,r}(A)=\text{Symm}^{r}_{A}\mathscr{T}_{\cdot}^{*}(A),

where
\text{Tsym}^{r}_{R}M
is the
R
-submodule of the symmetric tensors in
M^{\otimes r}
,
\text{Symm}^{r}_{R}M
is the maximal symmetric quotient of
M^{\otimes r}
, and
M
is any finite free module over a profinite
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-algebra
R
. When the level is clear, we shall simplify the notations, e.g. writing:

(1.1)

\mathscr{L}_{r}(A)=\mathscr{L}_{M(u),N(v),r}(A),\quad\mathscr{L}_{r}=\mathscr{L}_{r}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}),\quad\mathscr{S}_{r}(A)=\mathscr{S}_{M(u),N(v),r}(A),\quad\mathscr{S}_{r}=\mathscr{S}_{r}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}).

Let
\F_{\cdot}^{r}
be either
\mathscr{L}_{\cdot,r}(A)
or
\mathscr{S}_{\cdot,r}(A)
. Then there are natural isomorphisms of sheaves

\nu_{\ell}^{*}(\F_{M,N}^{r})\cong\F_{M,N(\ell)}^{r},\quad\check{\nu}_{\ell}^{*}(\F_{M,N}^{r}),\cong\F_{M(\ell),N}^{r},

and these induce pullback maps

H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M,N(\ell))_{S},\F_{M,N(\ell)}^{r})\xleftarrow{\nu_{\ell}^{*}}H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M,N)_{S},\F_{M,N}^{r})\xrightarrow{\check{\nu}_{\ell}^{*}}H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M(\ell),N)_{S},\F_{M(\ell),N}^{r})

and traces

H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M,N(\ell))_{S},\F_{M,N(\ell)}^{r})\xrightarrow{\nu_{\ell*}}H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M,N)_{S},\F_{M,N}^{r})\xleftarrow{\check{\nu}_{\ell*}}H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M(\ell),N)_{S},\F_{M(\ell),N}^{r}).

The finite étale isogeny
\lambda_{\ell}
induces morphisms

\lambda_{\ell*}:\F_{M,N(\ell)}^{r}\rightarrow\varphi_{\ell}^{*}(\F_{M(\ell),N}^{r}),\quad\lambda_{\ell}^{*}:\varphi_{\ell}^{*}(\F_{M(\ell),N}^{r})\rightarrow\F_{M,N(\ell)}^{r}

and this allows us to define a pushforward

\Phi_{\ell*}:=\varphi_{\ell*}\circ\lambda_{\ell*}:H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M,N(\ell))_{S},\F_{M,N(\ell)}^{r})\rightarrow H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M(\ell),N)_{S},\F_{M(\ell),N}^{r})

and a pullback

\Phi_{\ell}^{*}:=\lambda_{\ell}^{*}\circ\varphi_{\ell}^{*}:H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M(\ell),N)_{S},\F_{M(\ell),N}^{r})\rightarrow H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M,N(\ell))_{S},\F_{M,N(\ell)}^{r}).

The Hecke operator
T_{\ell}
and the dual Hecke operator
T_{\ell}^{\prime}
acting on
H^{i}_{\et}(Y(M,N)_{S},\F_{M,N}^{r})
are defined by

T_{\ell}:=\check{\nu}_{\ell*}\circ\Phi_{\ell*}\circ\nu_{\ell}^{*},\quad T_{\ell}^{\prime}:=\nu_{\ell*}\circ\Phi_{\ell}^{*}\circ\check{\nu}_{\ell}^{*}.

Remark 1.1.1
.

Note the relations

\text{deg}(\mu_{\ell})T_{\ell}=\text{pr}_{\ell*}\circ\text{pr}_{1}^{*},\quad\text{deg}(\mu_{\ell})T_{\ell}^{\prime}=\text{pr}_{1*}\circ\text{pr}_{\ell}^{*},

as follow immediately from the definitions.

For
d\in(\mathbf{Z}/MN\mathbf{Z})^{*}
, the diamond operator
\langle d\rangle
on
Y(\cdot)
is defined in terms of moduli by

(E,P,Q,C,D)\mapsto(E,d^{-1}\cdot P,d\cdot Q,C,D).

This is also a unique diamond operator
\langle d\rangle
on the universal elliptic curve making the following diagram cartesian:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.18822pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.15349pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E(\cdot)_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.04288pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v_{\cdot}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.21788pt\raise 8.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\langle d\rangle}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.37643pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v_{\cdot}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.37643pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-15.18822pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y(\cdot)_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.21788pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\langle d\rangle}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.18822pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.18822pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y(\cdot)_{S}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

and this induces automorphisms
\langle d\rangle=\langle d\rangle^{*}
and
\langle d\rangle^{\prime}=\langle d\rangle_{*}
on
H^{i}_{\et}(Y(\cdot)_{S},\mathscr{F^{r}_{\cdot}})
.

For any profinite
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-algebra
R
and finite free
R
-module
M
, the evaluation map induces a perfect pairing

\text{Tsym}^{r}_{R}M\otimes_{R}\text{Symm}^{r}_{R}M^{*}\rightarrow R,

where
M^{*}=\text{Hom}_{R}(M,\mathbf{Z}_{p})
. This gives a perfect pairing
\mathscr{L}_{r}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathscr{S}_{r}\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}_{p}
, and therefore a cup product

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:H^{1}_{\et}(Y(\cdot)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ll_{r}(1))\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}H^{1}_{\et,c}(Y(\cdot)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ss_{r})\rightarrow H^{2}_{\et}(Y(\cdot)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\cong\mathbf{Z}_{p},

which is perfect after inverting
p
. Moreover, the Hecke operators
T_{\ell}
,
T_{\ell}^{\prime}
,
\langle d\rangle
,
\langle d\rangle^{\prime}
induce endomorphisms on the compactly supported cohomology groups
H^{1}_{\et,c}(Y(\cdot)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ss_{r})
, and by construction,
(T_{\ell},T_{\ell}^{\prime})
and
(\langle d\rangle,\langle d\rangle^{\prime})
are adjoint pairs under
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
. The Eichler–Shimura isomorphism
[
Shi94
]

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ll_{r})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathbf{C}\cong M_{r+2}(N,\mathbf{C})\oplus\overline{S_{r+2}(N,\mathbf{C})}

commutes with the action of the Hecke operators on both sides.

1.2.
Galois representations associated to newforms

Let
f=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}q^{n}
be a normalised newform of weight
k\geq 2
, level
\Gamma_{1}(N_{f})
, and nebentype
\chi_{f}
. Let
p\nmid N_{f}
be a prime. Fix embeddings
i_{\infty}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{C}
and
i_{p}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. Let
L/\mathbf{Q}
be a finite extension containing all values
i_{\infty}^{-1}(a_{n})
and
i_{\infty}^{-1}\circ\chi_{f}
. Let
\mathfrak{P}
be the prime of
L
above
p
with respect to
i_{p}
. Then Eichler–Shimura and Deligne construct a
p
-adic Galois representation associated to
f
:

\rho_{f,\mathfrak{P}}:G_{\mathbf{Q}}\rightarrow\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(L_{\mathfrak{P}})

which is unramified outside
pN_{f}
, and characterised by the property for all finite primes
\ell\nmid pN_{f}
,

{\rm trace}(\rho_{f,\mathfrak{P}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}))=i_{p}(a_{\ell}),\quad{\rm det}(\rho_{f,\mathfrak{P}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{\ell}))=i_{p}(\chi_{f}(\ell)l^{k-1}),

where
{\rm Frob}_{\ell}
is the geometric Frobenius. Moreover,
\rho_{f,\mathfrak{P}}
is known to be irreducible
[
Rib77
]
, hence absolutely irreducible since the image of the complex conjugation has eigenvalues
1
and
-1
.

1.2.1.
Geometric realisations

The representation
\rho_{f,\mathfrak{P}}
can be realised geometrically as the largest subspace
V_{f}
of

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{f})_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ss_{k-2})\otimes L_{\mathfrak{P}}

on which
T_{\ell}
acts as multiplication by
a_{\ell}
for all
\ell\nmid N_{f}p
and
\langle d\rangle^{\prime}=\langle d\rangle_{*}
acts as multiplication by
\chi_{f}(d)
for all
d\in(\mathbf{Z}/N_{f}\mathbf{Z})^{\times}
. If
N
is any multiple of
N_{f}
, then the above subspace with
N_{f}
replaced by
N
gives rise to a representation
V_{f}(N)
isomorphic (non-canonically) to a finite number of copies of
V_{f}
.

The dual
V_{f}^{\vee}={\rm Hom}(V_{f},L_{\mathfrak{P}})

can be interpreted as the maximal quotient of

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{f})_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ll_{k-2}(1))\otimes L_{\mathfrak{P}}

on which the dual Hecke operator
T_{\ell}^{\prime}
acts as multiplication by
a_{\ell}
for all
\ell\nmid N_{f}p
and
\langle d\rangle=\langle d\rangle^{*}
acts as multiplication by
\chi_{f}(d)
for all
d\in(\mathbf{Z}/N_{f}\mathbf{Z})^{\times}
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be the ring of integers of
L_{\mathfrak{P}}
. In this paper we shall be mostly working with
V_{f}^{\vee}
and the
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
-stable
\mathcal{O}
-lattice
T_{f}^{\vee}\subset V_{f}^{\vee}
defined as the image of

H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{f})_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ll_{k-2}(1))\otimes\mathcal{O}
in
V_{f}^{\vee}
.

1.2.2.
The
p
-ordinary case

If
f
is ordinary at
p
, i.e.
i_{p}(a_{p})\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
, then the restriction of
V_{f}
to
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}\subset G_{\mathbf{Q}}
is reducible, fitting into and exact sequence of
L_{\mathfrak{P}}[G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}]
-modules

0\rightarrow V_{f}^{+}\rightarrow V_{f}\rightarrow V_{f}^{-}\rightarrow 0

with
{\rm dim}_{L_{\mathfrak{P}}}V_{f}^{\pm}=1
, and which the
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
-action on the subspace
V_{f}^{+}

given by the unramified character sending
\mathrm{Frob}_{p}
to
\alpha_{p}
, the unit root of
x^{2}-a_{p}x+\chi_{f}(p)p^{k-1}
.
By duality, we also obtain an exact sequence for
V_{f}^{\vee}
restricted to
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}

(1.2)

0\rightarrow V_{f}^{\vee,+}\rightarrow V_{f}^{\vee}\rightarrow V_{f}^{\vee,-}\rightarrow 0

with
V_{f}^{\vee,+}\simeq(V_{f}^{-})^{\vee}(1-k)(\chi_{f}^{-1})
, and with the
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
-action on the quotient
V_{f}^{\vee,-}
given by the unramified character sending arithmetic Frobenius
{\rm Frob}_{p}^{-1}
to
\alpha_{p}
.

1.3.
Patched CM Hecke modules

In this section, after explaining our conventions on Hecke characters, we recall the construction of certain patched CM Hecke modules from
[
LLZ15
]
.

1.3.1.
Hecke characters and theta series

Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field
in which

\textrm{$p=\mathfrak{p}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}$ splits},

with
\mathfrak{p}
the prime of
K
above
p
induced by
i_{p}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. We say that a Hecke character
\psi:\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\times}/K^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
has infinity type
(a,b)\in\mathbf{Z}^{2}
if
\psi_{\infty}(x_{\infty})=x_{\infty}^{a}\bar{x}_{\infty}^{b}
.
Then
\psi(x)x_{\infty}^{-a}\bar{x}_{\infty}^{-b}
is a ray class character, hence it takes value in a finite extension
L/K
. For
\mathfrak{P}|\mathfrak{p}
the prime of
L
above
p
induced by
i_{p}
, we define the
p
-adic avatar
\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}
of
\psi
as follows. Denote by
\text{rec}_{K}:\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\times}\rightarrow G_{K}^{\text{ab}}
the geometrically normalised Artin reciprocity map. For
g\in G_{K}
,
we take
x\in\mathbb{A}_{K}^{\times}
such that
\text{rec}_{K}(x)=g|_{K^{\rm ab}}
and define

\psi_{\p}(g)=i_{p}\circ i_{\infty}^{-1}(\psi(x)x_{\infty}^{-a}\bar{x}_{\infty}^{-b})x_{\pa}^{a}x_{\bar{\pa}}^{b}.

Since there should be no confusion, in the following we shall also use
\psi
to denote its
p
-adic avatar.

Let
\psi
be a Hecke character of
K
of infinity type
(-1,0)
, conductor
\mathfrak{f}
, taking values in a finite extension
L/K
. The theta series attached to
\psi
is

\theta_{\psi}=\sum_{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{f})=1}\psi(\mathfrak{a})q^{N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})}\in S_{2}(\Gamma_{1}(N_{\psi}),\chi_{\psi}\epsilon_{K})

where
N_{\psi}=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{f}){\rm disc}(K/\mathbf{Q})
,
\chi_{\psi}
is the unique Dirichlet character modulo
N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{f})
such that

\psi((n))=n\chi_{\psi}(n)
for all
n\in\mathbf{Z}
with
(n,N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{f}))=1
, and
\epsilon_{K}
is the quadratic Dirichlet character attached to
K
.
The cuspform
\theta_{\psi}
is new of level
N_{\psi}
if
\mathfrak{f}
is the conductor of
\psi
, and its
\mathfrak{P}
-adic representation satisfies

V_{\theta_{\psi}}\cong{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}L_{\mathfrak{P}}(\psi),\quad V_{\theta_{\psi}}^{\vee}\cong{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}L_{\mathfrak{P}}(\psi^{-1}).

1.3.2.
Hecke algebras and norm maps

Let
\mathfrak{n}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
be an ideal divisible by
\mathfrak{f}
. Put
N=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{n}){\rm disc}(K/\mathbf{Q})
. Let
K[\mathfrak{n}]
be the ray class field of
K
with conductor
\mathfrak{n}
, and let
H_{\mathfrak{n}}
be the ray class group of
K
modulo
\mathfrak{n}
. Let
K(\mathfrak{n})
be the largest
p
-subextension of
K
contained in
K[\mathfrak{n}]
, so
{\rm Gal}(K(\mathfrak{n})/K)\cong H_{\mathfrak{n}}^{(p)}
is the largest
p
-power quotient of
H_{\mathfrak{n}}
. For an ideal
\mathfrak{k}
of
K
coprime to
\mathfrak{n}
, let
[\mathfrak{k}]
be the class of
\mathfrak{k}
in
H_{\mathfrak{n}}
.

Proposition 1.3.1

(
[
LLZ15
, Prop. 3.2.1]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N)
be the subalgebra of
{\rm End}_{\mathbf{Z}}(H^{1}(Y_{1}(N)(\mathbf{C}),\mathbf{Z}))
generated by
\langle d\rangle^{\prime}
and
T_{\ell}^{\prime}
for all primes
\ell
. There exists a homomorphism
\phi_{\mathfrak{n}}:\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{n}}]
defined by

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathfrak{n}}(T_{\ell}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathfrak{l}}[\mathfrak{l}]\psi(\mathfrak{l}),

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathfrak{n}}(\langle d\rangle^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\chi_{\psi}(d)\epsilon_{K}(d)[(d)],

where the sum is over the ideals
\mathfrak{l}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
with
\mathfrak{l}\nmid\mathfrak{n}
and
N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{l})=\ell
.

For
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{n}\mathfrak{l}
, with
\mathfrak{l}
a prime ideal and
(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime},p)=1
, put
N^{\prime}=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime})\text{disc}(K/\mathbf{Q})
. Following
[
LLZ15
, §3.3]
, we consider the norm maps

\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}^{(p)}]\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N^{\prime})\otimes\mathbf{Z}_{p},\phi_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N^{\prime})_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\rightarrow\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{n}}^{(p)}]\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N)\otimes\mathbf{Z}_{p},\phi_{\mathfrak{n}}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))

defined by the following formulae:

•

If
\mathfrak{l}\mid\mathfrak{n}
then

\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}=1\otimes\text{pr}_{1*};

•

If
\mathfrak{l}\nmid\mathfrak{n}
is split or ramified in
K
, then

\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}=1\otimes\text{pr}_{1*}-\frac{\psi(\mathfrak{l)}[\mathfrak{l}]}{\ell}\otimes\text{pr}_{\ell*};

•

If
\mathfrak{l}\nmid\mathfrak{n}
is inert in
K
, say
\mathfrak{l}=(\ell)
, then

\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}=1\otimes\text{pr}_{1*}-\frac{\psi(\mathfrak{l})[\mathfrak{l}]}{\ell^{2}}\otimes\text{pr}_{\ell\ell*};

and we extend the definition of
\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}
to any pair of ideals
\mathfrak{n}\mid\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
by composition.

Notation 1.3.2
.

Let
\psi
be a character character of
K
of infinity type
(-1,0)
and conductor
\mathfrak{f}
. We say that
\psi
satisfies
Condition
\spadesuit
if either
(p,\mathfrak{f})=1
, or
\mathfrak{p}\mid\mathfrak{f}
,
\bar{\mathfrak{p}}\nmid\mathfrak{f}
, and

\psi|_{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}}\not\equiv\omega\;({\rm mod}\,\mathfrak{P}),

where
\omega
is the Teichmüller character.

Since we assume that
p
splits in
K
, by Proposition 5.1.2 and Remark 5.1.3 in
[
LLZ15
]
, if Condition
\spadesuit
holds, then for any ideal
\mathfrak{n}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
divisible by
\mathfrak{f}
and with
(\mathfrak{n},\bar{\mathfrak{p}})=1
, the maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N)
defined by the kernel of the composite map

\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N)\overset{\phi_{\mathfrak{n}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{n}}]\overset{{\rm aug}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{P},

where
\phi_{\mathfrak{n}}
is the map from Proposition
1.3.1
, is non-Eisenstein,
p
-ordinary, and
p
-distinguished.

Theorem 1.3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be the set of ideals
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
with
(\mathfrak{m},\bar{\mathfrak{p}})=1
, and put
\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}=\{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}\colon\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{A}\}
. Suppose
\psi
satisfies Condition
\spadesuit
. Then there is a family of
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
-equivariant isomorphisms of
\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{n}}^{(p)}]
-modules

\nu_{\mathfrak{n}}:\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{n}}^{(p)}]\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N)\otimes\mathbf{Z}_{p},\phi_{\mathfrak{n}}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K(\mathfrak{n})}\mathcal{O}(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1})

indexed by
\mathfrak{n}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
, such that for any
\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
with
\mathfrak{n}\mid\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}
the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}^{(p)}]\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N^{\prime})\otimes\mathbf{Z}_{p},\phi_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N^{\prime})_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime})}\mathcal{O}(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Norm}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{n}}^{(p)}]\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N)\otimes\mathbf{Z}_{p},\phi_{\mathfrak{n}}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{\mathfrak{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K(\mathfrak{n})}\mathcal{O}(\psi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{-1}),}

where
\mathrm{Norm}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}
is the natural norm map.

Proof.

This is a reformulation of Corollary 5.2.6 in
[
LLZ15
]
in the case where
p
splits in
K
.
∎

1.4.
Diagonal classes

We sketch the construction of the diagonal classes in the triple product of modular curves
Y_{1}(N)
using classical invariant theory, following Section
3
in
[
BSV22
]
.

We recall some notation used in Section
1.1.3
. Here,
Y_{1}(N)=Y_{1}(N)_{\mathbf{Q}}
,
E_{1}(N)=E_{1}(N)_{\mathbf{Q}}
the universal elliptic curve over
Y_{1}(N)
together with the structural map
v:E_{1}(N)\rightarrow Y_{1}(N)
. The relative Tate module of the universal elliptic curve is
\mathscr{T}=R^{1}v_{*}\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1)
, and its dual is
\mathscr{T}^{*}=\text{Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(\mathscr{T},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
. The cup product pairing combined with the relative trace

\mathscr{T}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathscr{T}\rightarrow R^{2}v_{*}\mathbf{Z}_{p}(2)\cong\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1)

gives a perfect relative Weil pairing

\langle,\rangle_{E_{1}(N)_{p^{\infty}}}:\mathscr{T}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathscr{T}\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1),

which allows
\mathscr{T}(-1)
to be identified with
\mathscr{T}^{*}
.

For
A
either the locally constant sheaf
\mathbf{Z}/p^{m}\mathbf{Z}(j)
or the locally constant
p
-adic sheaf
\mathbf{Z}_{p}(j)
on
X_{\et}
for some fixed
m\geq 1
and
m,j\in\mathbf{Z}
, recall that

\mathscr{L}_{r}(A)=\text{Tsym}^{r}_{A}\mathscr{T}(A),\quad\mathscr{S}_{r}(A)=\text{Symm}^{r}_{A}\mathscr{T}^{*}(A),

where given any finite free module
M
over a profinite
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-algebra
R
,
\text{Tsym}^{r}_{R}M
is the
R
- submodule of the symmetric tensors in
M^{\otimes r}
, and
\text{Symm}^{r}_{R}M
is the maximal symmetric quotient of
M^{\otimes r}
.

For a fixed geometric point
\eta:\text{Spec}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}})\rightarrow Y_{1}(N)
, denote by
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}=\pi_{1}^{\et}(Y_{1}(N),\eta)
the fundamental group of
Y_{1}(N)
with base point
\eta
. The stalk of
\mathscr{T}
at
\eta
, denoted
\mathscr{T}_{\eta}
, is a free
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-module of rank
2
, equipped with a continuous action of
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}
. Fix a choice of
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-module isomorphism
\zeta:\mathscr{T}_{\eta}\cong\mathbf{Z}_{p}\oplus\mathbf{Z}_{p}
such that
\langle x,y\rangle_{E_{1}(N)_{p^{\infty}}}=\zeta(x)\wedge\zeta(y)
(where we identify
\bigwedge^{2}\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{2}
with
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
via
(1,0)\wedge(0,1)=1
). One then obtains a continuous group homomorphism:

\rho_{\eta}:\mathcal{G}_{\eta}\rightarrow\text{Aut}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(\mathscr{T}_{\eta})\cong\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}).

By
[
FK88
, Prop A I.8]
, the category of locally constant
p
-adic sheaves on
Y_{1}(N)_{\et}
is equivalent to the category of
p
-adic representations of
\mathcal{G}_{\eta}
via the map
\mathscr{F}\mapsto\mathscr{F}_{\eta}
. Using
\rho_{\eta}
, one can associate with every continuous representation of
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
over a free finite
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-module
M
a smooth sheaf
M^{\et}
on
Y_{1}(N)
such that
M^{\et}_{\eta}=M
.

Let
S_{i}(A)
be the set of
2
-variable homogeneous polynomials of degree
i
in
A[x_{1},x_{2}]
equipped with the action of
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
by
gP(x_{1},x_{2})=P((x_{1},x_{2})\cdot g)
for all
g\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
and
P\in S_{i}(A)
. Its
A
-linear dual
L_{i}(A)
is also equipped with a
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
-action by
g\tau(P(x_{1},x_{2}))=\tau(g^{-1}P(x_{1},x_{2}))
for all
g\in\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
,
P\in S_{i}(A)
, and
\tau\in L_{i}(A)
. As sheaves on
Y_{1}(N)_{\mathbf{Q}}
, one has

L_{i}(A)^{\et}=\Ll_{i}(A)\quad\text{and}\quad S_{i}(A)^{\et}=\Ss_{i}(A).

Hence
\mathscr{T}_{\eta}\cong L_{1}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
and
\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1)_{\eta}\cong\bigwedge^{2}\mathscr{T}_{\eta}\cong\det^{-1}
. This implies that for any
j\in\mathbf{Z}
, and any
p
-adic representation
M
of
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
:

(1.3)

H^{0}(\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}),M\otimes\text{det}^{-j})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{G}_{\eta},M\otimes\text{det}^{-j})\cong H^{0}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N),M^{\et}(j)).

Assumption 1.1
.

Let
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
such that
r_{i}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
(r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3})/2=r\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and
r_{i}+r_{j}\geq r_{k}
for all permutation
(i,j,k)
of
(1,2,3)
. We call this the
balanced condition
.

Under the Assumption
1.1
, let

S_{\mathbf{r}}=S_{r_{1}}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}S_{r_{2}}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}S_{r_{3}}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})

a
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
-representation, and let

\Ss_{\mathbf{r}}=S_{\mathbf{r}}^{\et}=\Ss_{r_{1}}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\Ss_{r_{2}}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\Ss_{r_{3}}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}).

We identify
S_{\mathbf{r}}
with the module of
6
-variable polynomials
\mathbf{Z}_{p}[x_{1},x_{2},y_{1},y_{2},z_{1},z_{2}]
which is homogeneous of degree
r_{1}
,
r_{2}
, and
r_{3}
in the variables
(x_{1},x_{2})
,
(y_{1},y_{2})
, and
(z_{1},z_{2})
respectively. By the Clebsch–Gordan decomposition of classical invariant theory, the following is a
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
-invariant of
S_{\mathbf{r}}\otimes\det^{-r}
:

\text{Det}_{N}^{\mathbf{r}}:=\det\begin{pmatrix}x_{1}&x_{2}\\
y_{1}&y_{2}\end{pmatrix}^{r-r_{3}}\det\begin{pmatrix}x_{1}&x_{2}\\
z_{1}&z_{2}\end{pmatrix}^{r-r_{2}}\det\begin{pmatrix}y_{1}&y_{2}\\
z_{1}&z_{2}\end{pmatrix}^{r-r_{1}},

i.e.
\text{Det}_{N}^{\mathbf{r}}\in H^{0}(\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}),S_{\mathbf{r}}\otimes\det^{-r})
and denote its image under (
1.3
) as

(1.4)

\mathtt{Det}^{\mathbf{r}}_{N}\in H^{0}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N),\Ss_{\mathbf{r}}(r)).

Let
p_{j}:Y_{1}(N)^{3}\rightarrow Y_{1}(N)
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
be the natural projections and denote

\displaystyle\Ss_{[\mathbf{r}]}:=p_{1}^{*}\Ss_{r_{1}}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}p_{2}^{*}\Ss_{r_{2}}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}p_{3}^{*}\Ss_{r_{3}}(\mathbf{Z}_{p}),

\displaystyle\mathtt{W}_{N,\mathbf{r}}:=H^{3}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)^{3}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ss_{[\mathbf{r}]}(r+2)).

Because
Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}
is a smooth affine curve over
\overline{\mathbf{Q}}
, we have
H^{4}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)^{3}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ss_{[\mathbf{r}]}(r+2))=0
. Hence by the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence,

H^{p}(\mathbf{Q},H^{q}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)^{3}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ss_{[\mathbf{r}]}(r+2)))\Longrightarrow H^{p+q}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)^{3}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ss_{[\mathbf{r}]}(r+2))

one obtains

\mathtt{HS}:H^{4}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)^{3},\Ss_{[\mathbf{r}]}(r+2))\rightarrow H^{1}(\mathbf{Q},\mathtt{W}_{N,\mathbf{r}}).

If we let
d:Y_{1}(N)\rightarrow Y_{1}(N)^{3}
be the diagonal embedding, then there is a natural isomorphism
d^{*}\Ss_{[\mathbf{r}]}\cong\Ss_{\mathbf{r}}
of smooth sheaves on
Y_{1}(N)_{\et}
. As
d
is an embedding of codimension
2
, there is a pushforward map

d_{*}:H^{0}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N),\Ss_{\mathbf{r}}(r))\rightarrow H^{4}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)^{3},\Ss_{\mathbf{r}}(r+2)),

and we define the class

(\mathtt{HS}\circ d_{*})(\mathtt{Det}^{\mathbf{r}}_{N})\in H^{1}(\mathbf{Q},\mathtt{W}_{N,\mathbf{r}}).

Dually, by the bilinear form
\det^{*}:L_{i}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}L_{i}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}_{p}\otimes\det^{-i}
defined by
\det^{*}(\tau\otimes\sigma)=\tau\otimes\sigma((x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1})^{i})
that becomes perfect after inverting
p
, we can define an isomorphism of
\mathop{\rm GL}\nolimits_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
-modules

\mathbf{s}_{i}:S_{i}(\mathbf{Q}_{p})\cong L_{i}(\mathbf{Q}_{p})\otimes\text{det}^{i},

and so
\mathbf{s}_{i}:\Ss_{i}(\mathbf{Q}_{p})\cong\Ll_{i}(\mathbf{Q}_{p})\otimes\text{det}^{i}
by the above equivalence of categories. We then similarly define the sheaves
\Ll_{\mathbf{r}}
on
Y_{1}(N)
and
\Ll_{[\mathbf{r}]}
on
Y_{1}(N)^{3}
. Set

\mathtt{V}_{N,\mathbf{r}}:=H^{3}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N)^{3}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\Ll_{[\mathbf{r}]}(2-r)),\quad V_{N,\mathbf{r}}=\mathtt{V}_{N,\mathbf{r}}\otimes\mathbf{Q}_{p}.

Let
\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{r}}=\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{r}_{1}}\otimes\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{r}_{2}}\otimes\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{r}_{3}}
, which gives an isomorphism
W_{N,\mathbf{r}}\rightarrow V_{N,\mathbf{r}}
, and finally as in
[
BSV22
]
put

(1.5)

\kappa_{N,\mathbf{r}}:=(\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{r}*}\circ\mathtt{HS}\circ d_{*})(\mathtt{Det}^{\mathbf{r}}_{N})\in H^{1}(\mathbf{Q},V_{N,\mathbf{r}}).

As explained in detail in [
loc. cit.
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.2], the class
\kappa_{N,\mathbf{r}}
is closely related to the
p
-adic étale Abel–Jacobi image of the generalised Gross–Kudla–Schoen diagonal cycles on Kuga–Sato varieties studied in
[
DR14
]
.

Proposition 1.4.1
.

For a prime number
\ell
and a positive integer
m
, if
(m\ell,pN)=1
then

({\rm pr}_{i*},{\rm pr}_{j*},{\rm pr}_{k*})\kappa_{m\ell,\textbf{r}}=(\bigstar)\kappa_{m,\textbf{r}}

where

(i,j,k)

\bigstar

(\ell,1,1)

(\ell-1)(T_{\ell},1,1)

(1,\ell,1)

(\ell-1)(1,T_{\ell},1)

(1,1,\ell)

(\ell-1)(1,1,T_{\ell})

(1,\ell,\ell)

\ell^{r-r_{1}}(\ell-1)(T_{\ell}^{\prime},1,1)

(\ell,1,\ell)

\ell^{r-r_{2}}(\ell-1)(1,T_{\ell}^{\prime},1)

(\ell,\ell,1)

\ell^{r-r_{3}}(\ell-1)(1,1,T_{\ell}^{\prime})

If
(\ell,m)=1
then we also have

(i,j,k)

\bigstar

(1,1,1)

(\ell^{2}-1)

(\ell,\ell,\ell)

(\ell^{2}-1)\ell^{r}

Proof.

See equations (174) and (176) in
[
BSV22
]
.
∎

2.
Main theorems

In this section, for a newform
f
and two Hecke characters
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
of an imaginary quadratic field
K
, using the results from
[
BSV22
]
and
[
LLZ15
]
recalled in the preceding section, we construct a family of cohomology classes for
f\otimes\psi_{1}\psi_{2}
defined over ring class field extensions of
K
, and prove that they satisfy the norm relations of an anticyclotomic Euler system.

The construction is in two steps: we first give the construction and the proof of the tame norm relations in the case where
(f,\theta_{\psi_{1}},\theta_{\psi_{2}})
have weights
(2,2,2)
; then using the variation of diagonal cycle classes in Hida families we extend the construction to more general weights and deduce the proof of the wild norm relations.

Throughout this section we consider the following set-up. We let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N_{f}))
be a newform of weight
k\geq 2
,
K/\mathbf{Q}
an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant prime to
N_{f}
, and
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
(not necessarily primitive)
Hecke characters of
K
of infinity type
(1-k_{1},0),(1-k_{2},0)
, with
k_{1},k_{2}\geq 1
, and modulus
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2}
, respectively. Denote by

\theta_{\psi_{i}}\in S_{k_{i}}(N_{\psi_{i}},\chi_{\psi_{i}})

the associated theta series, where
N_{\psi_{i}}=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{f}_{i})\cdot{\rm disc}(K/\mathbf{Q})
and
\chi_{\phi_{i}}
is the Dirichlet character modulo
N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{f}_{i})
defined by
\psi_{i}((n))=n^{k_{i}-1}\chi_{\psi_{i}}(n)
for all integers
n
prime to
N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{f}_{i})
(
i=1,2
). We assume the self-duality condition

(2.1)

\chi_{\psi_{1}}\chi_{\psi_{2}}=1.

In particular, since
k
is even by hypothesis, condition (
2.1
) implies that
k_{1}\equiv k_{2}\,({\rm mod}\,2)
.

Let
L/K
be a finite extension containing the Fourier coefficients of
f
,
\theta_{\psi_{1}}
, and
\theta_{\psi_{2}}
. Let
p\geq 5
be a prime that splits in
K
and such that
(p,N_{f}N_{\psi_{1}}N_{\psi_{2}})=1
, and let
\mathfrak{P}|\mathfrak{p}
be the primes of
L/K
above
p
determined by a fixed embedding
i_{p}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
. Finally, let
L_{\mathfrak{P}}
be the completion of
L
at
\mathfrak{P}
, and denote by
\mathcal{O}
the ring of integers of
L_{\mathfrak{P}}
.

2.1.
Construction in weight
(2,2,2)

Suppose in this subsection that
k=k_{1}=k_{2}=2
. Let
N=\text{lcm}(N_{f},N_{\psi_{1}},N_{\psi_{2}})
and for every positive integer
m
put

{Y(m):=Y(1,Nm)=Y_{1}(Nm)}.

When
m=1
, we drop it from the notation, so
Y:=Y_{1}(N)
. We begin with the cohomology class

(2.2)

\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}:=\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{r}*}\circ\mathtt{HS}\circ d_{*}(\mathtt{Det}_{Nm}^{\mathbf{r}})\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{3}_{\et}(Y(m)^{3}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(2)\bigr)

in the notations of Section
1.4
, where
\mathbf{r}=(0,0,0)
, and put

\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(2)}=({\rm pr}_{m*},1,1)\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{3}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times Y(m)^{2}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(2)\bigr),

where, writing
m=\prod_{i}\ell_{i}
as a product of (not necessarily distinct) primes,
\text{pr}_{m*}
is the composition of the pushforward by the degeneracy maps
\text{pr}_{\ell_{i}}
.

Applying the Künneth decomposition theorem (see e.g.
[
Mil80
, Thm. 22.4]
) together with the natural degeneracy maps
Y(m)\rightarrow Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{i}}m)

(i=1,2)
, the class
\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(2)}
is projected to

(2.3)

\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(3)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\otimes H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{1}}m)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\otimes H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{2}}m)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))(-1)\bigr).

Now we fix a test vector
\breve{f}\in S_{2}(N)[f]
. The maps used in the construction
\tilde{\kappa}^{(3)}_{m}
are compatible with correspondences, and so after tensoring with
\mathcal{O}
the above process gives rise to a class

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(4)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},T_{f}^{\vee}

\displaystyle\otimes H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{1}}m)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N_{\psi_{1}}m)}\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}]

\displaystyle\quad\otimes H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{2}}m)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N_{\psi_{2}}m)}\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}]\bigr),

where the labeled tensor products are with respect to the Hecke algebra homomorphisms

\phi_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}}:\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N_{\psi_{1}}m)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}],\quad\phi_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}:\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N_{\psi_{2}}m)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}]

of Proposition
1.3.1
, and we used our chosen
\breve{f}
to take the image under the projection
H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\rightarrow T_{f}^{\vee}
in the first factor.

Since
p
splits in
K
and is coprime to
\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{f}_{2}
, Condition
\spadesuit
is satisfied for both
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
. Therefore by the isomorphisms from Proposition
1.3.3
:

\displaystyle\nu_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}}:H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{1}}m)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N_{\psi_{1}}m)}\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K(\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m})}\mathcal{O}(\psi_{1}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\nu_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}:H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{2}}m)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N_{\psi_{2}}m)}\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K(\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}})}\mathcal{O}(\psi_{2}^{-1}),

the class
\tilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(4)}
defines an element in

H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\text{Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K(\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m})}\mathcal{O}(\psi_{1}^{-1})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\text{Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K(\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}})}\mathcal{O}(\psi_{2}^{-1})(-1)\bigr),

which under the maps induced by
H_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}}\twoheadrightarrow H_{\mathfrak{m}}
and
H_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}\twoheadrightarrow H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}
is naturally projected to a class

\tilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(5)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{1}^{-1}}[H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{2}^{-1}}[H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}](-1)\bigr).

2.1.1.
Projection to ring class groups

Directly from the definitions of the class groups involved, we deduce the commutative diagram with exact rows

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\times\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{m})^{\times}\times(\mathcal{O}_{K}/\overline{\mathfrak{m}})^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{\mathfrak{m}}\times H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{1}\times H_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{O}_{K}/m\mathcal{O}_{K})^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{H_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{1,}

where the unlabelled vertical arrow is given by the restriction map

\sigma\mapsto(\sigma|_{K_{\mathfrak{m}}},\sigma|_{K_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}}).

In particular, when
p\nmid 6h_{K}
, where
h_{K}:=|H_{1}|
is the class number of
K
, taking
p
-primary parts this map induces an isomorphism

(2.4)

H_{m}^{(p)}\xrightarrow{\simeq}H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}\times H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}.

Given an integer
n>0
, let
H[n]
be the ring class group of
K
of conductor
n
, so

H[n]\simeq{\rm Pic}(\mathcal{O}_{n})
under the Artin reciprocity map, where
\mathcal{O}_{n}=\mathbf{Z}+n\mathcal{O}_{K}
is the order of
K
of conductor
n
. Let
H[n]^{(p)}
be the maximal
p
-power quotient of
H[n]
, and denote by
K[n]
be the maximal
p
-extension inside the ring class field of
K
of conductor
n
, so
H[n]^{(p)}={\rm Gal}(K[n]/K)
.

Proposition 2.1.1
.

Suppose
p\nmid 6h_{K}
and
\mathfrak{m}
is an ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
of norm
m
divisible only by primes that are split in
K
. Then, identifying
H_{m}^{(p)}
with
H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}\times H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}
as in (
2.4
), we have an exact sequence

1\longrightarrow(\mathbf{Z}/m\mathbf{Z})^{\times,(p)}\overset{\Delta}{\longrightarrow}H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}\times H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}\overset{\pi_{\Delta}}{\longrightarrow}H[m]^{(p)}\longrightarrow 1,

where the map
\Delta
sends
a\mapsto([a],[a])
for every integer
a
coprime to
m
. Moreover, if
(\ell)=\mathfrak{l}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}
is a prime that splits in
K
and is coprime to
m
, the projection
\pi_{\Delta}
sends

\displaystyle\quad[\mathfrak{l}]\times[\mathfrak{l}]

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathrm{Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}}

where
\mathrm{Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}}
is the Frobenius element of
\mathfrak{l}
in
H[m]^{(p)}
.

Proof.

The first part is clear from the above discussion together with the commutative diagram with exact rows

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{O}_{K}/m\mathcal{O}_{K})^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}/\mathbf{Z}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{O}_{K}/m\mathcal{O}_{K})^{\times}/(\mathbf{Z}/m\mathbf{Z})^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H[m]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{1,}

where the vertical arrows are given by the natural projections. The second part follows from the functoriality properties of Frobenii.
∎

Under the hypotheses in Proposition
2.1.1
, we can consider the image of
\tilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(5)}
under the composite map

(2.5)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern where the horizontal arrow is the map determined by
\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2}\mapsto\xi(\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2})
with
\xi(\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2})(g)=\phi_{1}(g_{1})\otimes\phi_{2}(g_{2})
if

, resulting in the class

\kappa_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(6)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\text{Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}}[H[m]^{(p)}](-1)\bigr).

Definition 2.1.2
.

Suppose
p\nmid 6h_{K}
splits in
K
and
\mathfrak{m}
is an ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
of norm
m
divisible only by primes that split in
K
. Then we define

\widetilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{1}\bigl(K[m],T_{f}^{\vee}(\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1})(-1)\bigr)

to be the image of
\kappa_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(6)}
under the isomorphism

H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\text{Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}}[H[m]^{(p)}](-1)\bigr)\simeq H^{1}\bigl(K[m],T_{f}^{\vee}(\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1})(-1)\bigr)

given by Shapiro’s lemma.

We finish this section by recording the following observation for our later use.

Lemma 2.1.3
.

The following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{1}^{-1}}[H_{\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{l}}^{(p)}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{2}^{-1}}[H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}}^{(p)}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}}[H[ml]^{(p)}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Norm}^{ml}_{m}}
\textstyle{{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{1}^{-1}}[H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{2}^{-1}}[H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Ind}^{\mathbf{Q}}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}}[H[m]^{(p)}],}

where the horizonal arrows are given by the composition
\xi_{\Delta}
in (
2.5
).

Proof.

This is clear from the explicit description of the maps involved.
∎

2.2.
Proof of the tame norm relations

Let
\mathfrak{m}
be an ideal of norm
m
for which we have the class
\tilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
as in Definition
2.1.2
.

Proposition 2.2.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{l}
be a prime split in
K
of norm
\ell
coprime to
mp
. Then

\displaystyle{\rm Norm}_{K[m]}^{K[m\ell]}(\tilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{l}})=(\ell-1)\bigg(

\displaystyle a_{\ell}(f)-\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\mathfrak{l})}{\ell}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\mathfrak{l}])-\frac{\psi_{1}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell}([\overline{\mathfrak{l}}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])

\displaystyle+(1-\ell)\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell^{2}}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])\bigg)(\tilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}).

In the notations of Theorem
1.3.3
, for any
\mathfrak{n}=\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{f}}
put

H^{1}(\psi,\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}):=H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi}m)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(N_{\psi}m)}\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}].

Then from Theorem
1.3.3
and Lemma
2.1.3
we have the following commutative diagram:

(2.6)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 91.85242pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-91.85242pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise where the horizontal arrows are given by the maps induced by the composition

H^{1}(\psi_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\nu_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}}{\rm Ind}_{K(\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}^{\prime})}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathcal{O}(\psi_{1}^{-1})\simeq{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{1}^{-1}}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}^{(p)}]\longrightarrow{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{1}^{-1}}[H_{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}^{(p)}]

and likewise for
H^{1}(\psi_{2},\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{\prime})
, together with the composition
\xi_{\Delta}
in (
2.5
).

Now, tracing through the definitions we compute:

\displaystyle(1\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathfrak{ml}}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}})(\tilde{\kappa}^{(2)}_{m\ell})

\displaystyle=(1\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathfrak{ml}}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}})(\tpr_{m\ell*},1,1)(\tilde{\kappa}^{(1)}_{m\ell})

\displaystyle=(\tpr_{m*},1,1)(\text{pr}_{\ell*}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathfrak{ml}}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}})(\tilde{\kappa}^{(1)}_{m\ell})

\displaystyle=(\tpr_{m*},1,1)\biggl(\tpr_{\ell*}\times(1\otimes\text{pr}_{1*}-\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})[\mathfrak{l}]}{\ell}\otimes\text{pr}_{\ell*})\times(1\otimes\text{pr}_{1*}-\frac{\psi_{2}(\bar{\mathfrak{l}})[\bar{\mathfrak{l}}]}{\ell}\otimes\text{pr}_{\ell*})\biggr)(\tilde{\kappa}^{(1)}_{m\ell})

\displaystyle=(\tpr_{m*},1,1)\biggl((\text{pr}_{\ell*},{\rm pr}_{1*},{\rm pr}_{1*})-\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})[\mathfrak{l}]}{\ell}(\text{pr}_{\ell*},\text{pr}_{\ell*},{\rm pr}_{1*})-\frac{\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}]}{\ell}(\text{pr}_{\ell*},{\rm pr}_{1*},\text{pr}_{\ell*})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell^{2}}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])(\text{pr}_{\ell*},\text{pr}_{\ell*},\text{pr}_{\ell*})\biggr)(\tilde{\kappa}^{(1)}_{m\ell}).

Together with Proposition
1.4.1
, we thus obtain

\displaystyle(1\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathfrak{ml}}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}})(\tilde{\kappa}^{(2)}_{m\ell})

\displaystyle=(\ell-1)(\tpr_{m*},1,1)\bigg((T_{\ell},1,1)-\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})[\mathfrak{l}]}{\ell}(1,1,T_{\ell}^{\prime})-(1,T_{\ell}^{\prime},1)\frac{\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}]}{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell^{2}}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])(\ell+1)\bigg)(\tilde{\kappa}^{(1)}_{m})

\displaystyle=(\ell-1)\bigg((T_{\ell},1,1)-\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})[\mathfrak{l}]}{\ell}(1,1,T_{\ell}^{\prime})-(1,T_{\ell}^{\prime},1)\frac{\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}]}{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell^{2}}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])(\ell+1)\bigg)(\tilde{\kappa}^{(2)}_{m}),

and from this it follows that

\displaystyle(1\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathfrak{ml}}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\overline{\mathfrak{l}}})(\tilde{\kappa}^{(4)}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{l}})

\displaystyle=(\ell-1)\bigg(a_{\ell}(f)-\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})[\mathfrak{l}]}{\ell}(\psi_{2}(\mathfrak{l})[\mathfrak{l}]+\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])-(\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})[\mathfrak{l}]+\psi_{1}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])\frac{\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}]}{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell^{2}}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])(\ell+1)\bigg)(\tilde{\kappa}^{(4)}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}})

\displaystyle=(\ell-1)\bigg(a_{\ell}(f)-\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\mathfrak{l})}{\ell}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\mathfrak{l}])-\frac{\psi_{1}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell}([\overline{\mathfrak{l}}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])

\displaystyle\quad+(1-\ell)\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell^{2}}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])\bigg)(\tilde{\kappa}^{(4)}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}).

In light of the commutative diagram (
2.6
), this yields the result.
∎

Remark 2.2.2
.

The appearance of the factor
(\ell-1)
in Proposition
2.2.1
can be traced back to the relations
\text{deg}(\mu_{\ell})T_{\ell}=\text{pr}_{\ell*}\circ\text{pr}_{1}^{*}
and
\text{deg}(\mu_{\ell})T_{\ell}^{\prime}=\text{pr}_{1*}\circ\text{pr}_{\ell}^{*}
,
i.e., it is caused by the degeneracy map
\mu_{\ell}
. In the next subsection we shall get rid of this extra factor.

Remark 2.2.3
.

We want to emphasize that Proposition
2.2.1
is the key result for the construction of our anticyclotomic Euler system for
T_{f}^{\vee}(\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1})(-1)
.
Indeed, with the factor
(\ell-1)
stripped out, the term in the right-hand side of Proposition
2.2.1
can be massaged to agree with the local Euler factor at
\mathfrak{l}
of the Galois representation
[T_{f}^{\vee}(\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1})(-1)]^{\vee}(1)=T_{f}(\psi_{1}\psi_{2})(2)
, giving the correct norm relations.

2.2.1.
Removing the extra factor
(\ell-1)

Adapting some ideas from
[
DR17
,
\lx@sectionsign{1.4}
]
, we now introduce a modification of the classes
\widetilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
for which we can prove an analogue of Proposition
2.2.1
without the extra factor
(\ell-1)
.

We begin by noting that for any prime
\ell\nmid N
the degeneracy maps
{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm pr}_{\ell}:Y_{1}(N\ell)\rightarrow Y_{1}(N)
can be factored as

\textstyle{Y_{1}(N\ell)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{\ell}}
\textstyle{Y(1,N(\ell))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\textstyle{Y_{1}(N)}

\textstyle{Y_{1}(N\ell)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\text{pr}_{\ell}}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{\ell}}
\textstyle{Y(1,N(\ell))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\ell}}
\textstyle{Y_{1}(N),}

where

\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{\ell}
are a non-Galois coverings of degree
\ell+1
, and we recall that
\mu_{\ell}
is a cyclic Galois covering of degree
\ell-1
.

Denote by

D_{m}=\{(\langle d\rangle,\langle d\rangle):d\in(\mathbf{Z}/Nm\mathbf{Z})^{\times},\;d\equiv 1\;({\rm mod}\,{N})\}

the set of diamond operators acting diagonally on
Y_{1}(Nm)^{2}
. Set

W_{1}(Nm)=(Y_{1}(Nm)\times Y_{1}(Nm))/D_{m},

and denote by
d_{m}:Y_{1}(Nm)^{2}\rightarrow W_{1}(Nm)
the natural projection map, which is an étale morphism of degree
\phi(m)=|(\mathbf{Z}/m\mathbf{Z})^{\times}|
.

Let
\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}
be as in (
2.2
), and denote by

\kappa_{m}^{(1)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{3}_{\et}(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times W_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})(2)\bigr)

the image of
(\mu_{m*},1,1)(\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)})
under the natural map
induced by
d_{m}
; thus the class
\kappa_{m}^{(1)}
is defined by the relation

(2.7)

(\mu_{m*},d_{m*})\tilde{\kappa}^{(1)}_{m}=\phi(m)\kappa^{(1)}_{m}.

Proposition 2.2.4
.

For a prime number
\ell
and a positive integer
m
such that
(m,\ell)=1
and
(m\ell,pN)=1
, we have

(\pi_{i*},{\rm pr}_{j*},{\rm pr}_{k*})\kappa_{m\ell}^{(1)}=(\bigstar)\kappa_{m}^{(1)},

where

(i,j,k)

\bigstar

(i,j,k)

\bigstar

(\ell,1,1)

(T_{\ell},1,1)

(\ell,1,\ell)

(1,T_{\ell}^{\prime},1)

(1,\ell,1)

(1,T_{\ell},1)

(\ell,\ell,1)

(1,1,T_{\ell}^{\prime})

(1,1,\ell)

(1,1,T_{\ell})

(1,1,1)

(\ell+1)

(1,\ell,\ell)

(T_{\ell}^{\prime},1,1)

(\ell,\ell,\ell)

(\ell+1)

Proof.

Directly from the definitions we find

\displaystyle(\mu_{m*},d_{m*})({\rm pr}_{\ell*},{\rm pr}_{1*},{\rm pr}_{1*})\tilde{\kappa}_{m\ell}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=(\pi_{\ell*},{\rm pr}_{1*},{\rm pr}_{1*})(\mu_{m\ell*},d_{m\ell*})\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\phi(m\ell)(\pi_{\ell*},{\rm pr}_{1*},{\rm pr}_{1*})\kappa_{m}^{(1)},

while on the other hand, from Proposition
1.4.1
and (
2.7
) we have

\displaystyle(\mu_{m*},d_{m*})({\rm pr}_{\ell*},{\rm pr}_{1*},{\rm pr}_{1*})\tilde{\kappa}_{m\ell}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=(\mu_{m*},d_{m*})(\ell-1)(T_{\ell},1,1)\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\phi(m)(\ell-1)(T_{\ell},1,1)\kappa_{m}^{(1)}.

Since
\phi(m\ell)=(\ell-1)\phi(m)
under our assumptions, this shows the result in the case
(i,j,k)=(\ell,1,1)
and the other cases are shown in the same manner.
∎

Now we want to proceed as above to obtain from the new
\kappa_{m}^{(1)}
a construction of classes satisfying the correct norm relations (i.e., without the factor
\ell-1
). This requires a careful study of the étale cohomology of the quotient
Y(1,N(m))\times W_{1}(Nm)
.

We begin with the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence:

E_{2}^{p,q}=H^{p}\bigl(D_{m},H^{q}_{\et,c}(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times Y_{1}(Nm)^{2}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\bigr)\Rightarrow H^{p+q}_{\et,c}\bigl(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times W_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}\bigr).

This yields the exact sequence

(2.8)

E\longrightarrow H^{3}_{\et,c}\bigl(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times W_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}\bigr)\xrightarrow{(1,d_{m}^{*})}E_{2}^{0,3}\xrightarrow{d_{2}^{0,3}}E_{2}^{2,2},

where
E
is naturally identified with a subquotient of
E_{2}^{1,2}\oplus E_{2}^{2,1}
. Thus we see that the difference between the two middle pieces are classes coming from
H^{q}_{\et,c}(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times Y_{1}(Nm)^{2}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
with
0\leq q\leq 2
. From the Künneth decomposition, each of these classes will have a factor from either
H^{0}_{\et,c}(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
or
H^{0}_{\et,c}(Y_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})
, and so they will be annihilated after localisation at a non-Eisenstein ideal
\mathcal{I}
of
\mathbb{T}_{Nm}^{\prime}
, therefore obtaining an isomorphism

H^{3}_{\et,c}\bigl(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times W_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}\bigr)_{\mathcal{I}}\xrightarrow{(1,d_{m}^{*})}H^{3}_{\et,c}\bigl(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times Y_{1}(Nm)^{2}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\bigr)^{D_{m}}_{\mathcal{I}}

after localizing (
2.8
) at
\mathcal{I}
. By Poincaré duality, from (
2.8
) we obtain a map

(2.9)

H^{3}_{\et}\bigl(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times W_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}\bigr)\xleftarrow{(1,d_{m*})}H^{3}_{\et}\bigl(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times Y_{1}(Nm)^{2}_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})\bigr)_{D_{m}}

whose kernel and cokernel will also be annihilated by localization at a non-Eisenstein ideal
\mathcal{I}
. Additionally, we recall the following lemma:

Lemma 2.2.5
.

For
\mathcal{I}
a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal of
\mathbb{T}_{N}^{\prime}
, we have natural isomorphisms

H^{1}_{c}(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})_{\mathcal{I}}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(X_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})_{\mathcal{I}}\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{1}(Y_{1}(N)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})_{\mathcal{I}}.

Proof.

Immediate from the Manin–Drinfeld theorem (see e.g.
[
LLZ14
, Prop. 4.1.3]
).
∎

Hence from (
2.9
), Lemma
2.2.5
, and the Künneth decomposition, it follows that after localization at a non-Eisenstein maximal ideal
\mathcal{I}
we get a natural map

(2.10)

H^{3}_{\et}(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}\times W_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})_{\mathcal{I}}\xrightarrow{(1,d_{m*}^{-1})}H^{1}_{\et}(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})_{\mathcal{I}}\otimes\\
H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})_{\mathcal{I}}\otimes_{D_{m}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}))_{\mathcal{I}}.

Next, for
\psi
a Hecke character of
K
of conductor
\mathfrak{f}
and
\mathfrak{m}
an ideal of
K
of norm
m
coprime to
p
divisible only by primes that split in
K
, we let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}
be the kernel of the composite map

\mathbb{T}_{Nm}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}}\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}]\xrightarrow{\rm aug}\mathcal{O}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{P}.

By
[
LLZ15
, Prop 5.1.2]
, the maximal ideal
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}
is non-Eisenstein,
p
-ordinary and
p
-distinguished. At a later step, we shall look at the module

\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}]\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\prime}(Nm)\otimes\mathbf{Z}_{p},\phi_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1)),

and it is clear that the map from
H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(Nm)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))
to this module factors through completion at
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}
. Moreover, assuming that
f
is non-Eisenstein modulo
\mathfrak{P}
(i.e.
T_{f}^{\vee}
is residually irreducible), we can choose an auxiliary prime
q\nmid Nmp
with
1+q-a_{q}(f)\in\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and
\frac{1+q-T_{q}}{1+q-a_{q}(f)}\notin\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}
,
hence defining an invertible element after localization at
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}
that fixes the
f
-isotypic component we are interested in.

Now we put
\kappa_{m}^{(2)}:=(\pi_{m*},1,1)\kappa_{m}^{(1)}
, and define

\kappa_{m}^{(3)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{1}_{\et}(Y(1,N(m))_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})_{\mathcal{I}}\otimes H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{1}}m)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})_{\mathcal{I}}\otimes_{D_{m}}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{2}}m)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p})_{\mathcal{I}}(-1)\bigr),

with
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{f}\mathfrak{m}}
, to be the image of
\kappa_{m}^{(2)}
under the map (
2.10
) composed with the natural degeneracy maps
Y_{1}(Nm)\rightarrow Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{i}}m)
(
i=1,2
).

Note that taking
D_{m}^{(p)}
-coinvariants (where
D_{m}^{(p)}
denotes the
p
-part of
D_{m}
) is compatible with the diagonal map
\xi_{\Delta}
in (
2.5
), since
by Theorem
1.3.1

for
(\langle d\rangle,\langle d\rangle)\in D_{m}^{(p)}
we have
\phi_{\mathfrak{m}}(\langle d\rangle^{\prime})\times\phi_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}(\langle d\rangle^{\prime})=[d]\times[d]\in H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}\times H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}
, and this is in the kernel of
\pi_{\Delta}
. Thus applying to
\kappa_{m}^{(3)}
the same process we used above to go from
\tilde{\kappa}_{m}^{(3)}
to the class
\tilde{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
of Definition
2.1.2
we obtain

{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{1}\bigl(K[m],T_{f}^{\vee}(\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1})(-1)\bigr).

Proposition 2.2.6
.

Suppose
f
is non-Eisenstein modulo
\mathfrak{P}
.
Let
\mathfrak{m}
be an ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
of norm
m
divisible only by primes split in
K
, and let
\mathfrak{l}
be a prime split in
K
of norm
\ell
coprime to
mp
. Then

\displaystyle{\rm Norm}_{K[m]}^{K[m\ell]}({\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{l}})=\bigg(

\displaystyle a_{\ell}(f)-\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\mathfrak{l})}{\ell}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\mathfrak{l}])-\frac{\psi_{1}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell}([\overline{\mathfrak{l}}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])

\displaystyle+(1-\ell)\frac{\psi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})\psi_{2}(\overline{\mathfrak{l}})}{\ell^{2}}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\overline{\mathfrak{l}}])\bigg)({\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}).

Proof.

After the above discussion, the same calculation as in the proof of Proposition
2.2.1
applies, replacing the use of Proposition
1.4.1
by Proposition
2.2.4
.
∎

Thus we arrive at the following theorem:

Theorem 2.2.7
.

Suppose
p\nmid 6h_{K}
and
f
is non-Eisenstein modulo
\mathfrak{P}
. Let
\mathfrak{m}
run over the ideals of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
divisible only by primes that are split in
K
with
m=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{m})
coprime to
p
.
Then there exists a collection of cohomology classes

z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{1}\bigl(K[m],T_{f}^{\vee}(\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1})(-1)\bigr)

such that for every split prime
\mathfrak{l}
of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
of norm
\ell
with
