(\ell,mp)=1
we have the norm relation

\mathrm{Norm}_{K[m]}^{K[m\ell]}(z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{ml}})=P_{\mathfrak{l}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}})(z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}),

where
P_{\mathfrak{l}}(X)=\det(1-X\cdot\mathrm{Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}}\,|\,T_{f}(\psi_{1}\psi_{2})(2))
.

Proof.

Denote by
Q_{\mathfrak{l}}
the factor appearing in the right-hand side of Proposition
2.2.6
. Recalling that
[\mathfrak{l}]\times[\mathfrak{l}]
corresponds to
{\rm Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}}\in H[m]^{(p)}
under the map
\pi_{\Delta}
of Proposition
2.1.1
, we find the following congruences as endomorphisms of
H^{1}(K[m],T_{f}^{\vee}(\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1})(-1))
:

\displaystyle-\psi_{1}\psi_{2}(\mathfrak{l})([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\mathfrak{l}])\cdot Q_{\mathfrak{l}}

\displaystyle\equiv-a_{\ell}(f)\psi_{1}\psi_{2}(\mathfrak{l})([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\mathfrak{l}])+\frac{\psi_{1}\psi_{2}(\mathfrak{l})^{2}}{\ell}([\mathfrak{l}]\times[\mathfrak{l}])^{2}+\frac{\psi_{1}\psi_{2}((\ell))}{\ell}([\ell]\times[\ell])

\displaystyle\equiv P_{\mathfrak{l}}({\rm Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}})\pmod{\ell-1},

using the relation
\psi_{1}\psi_{2}((\ell))=\chi_{\psi_{1}}\chi_{\psi_{2}}(\ell)\ell^{2}=\ell^{2}
and the fact that
[\ell]\times[\ell]
is in the kernel of
\pi_{\Delta}
for the second congruence. Therefore, by Lemma 9.6.1 and 9.6.3 in
[
Rub00
]
, the existence of classes
z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
satisfying the stated norm relations follows from Proposition
2.2.6
.
∎

Remark 2.2.8
.

Similar to what we did for
\mathfrak{l}
a split prime of
K
, when
\mathfrak{l}=(\ell)
is inert in
K
, we also obtain such a norm relation like in Theorem
2.2.7
. Remember that in this case, we push forward from level
N\ell^{2}
to level
N
. First, note that the norm map from Proposition
1.3.1
is then given by

\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\mathfrak{n}(\ell)}=1\otimes\text{pr}_{1*}-\frac{\psi(\ell)[(\ell)]}{\ell^{2}}\otimes\text{pr}_{\ell\ell*}

Second, to calculate
(1\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\mathfrak{m\ell}}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\ell})(\kappa_{m\ell}^{(2)})
, just like in Proposition
2.2.1
, we use the table in Proposition
1.4.1
together with

\displaystyle(\text{pr}_{\ell*},\pr 1,\pr 1)(T_{\ell},1,1)\kappa^{(2)}_{m\ell}

\displaystyle=\{(T_{\ell}^{2},1,1)-(\ell+1)(\langle\ell\rangle,1,1)\}\kappa^{(2)}_{m},

\displaystyle(\text{pr}_{\ell*},\text{pr}_{\ell*},\pr 1)(1,1,T_{\ell}^{\prime})\kappa^{(2)}_{m\ell}

\displaystyle=\{(1,1,T_{\ell}^{\prime 2})-(\ell+1)(1,1,\langle\ell\rangle^{\prime})\}\kappa^{(2)}_{m},

and arrive at

\mathrm{Norm}_{K[m]}^{K[m\ell]}(\kappa^{(6)}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{ml}})=(\ell-1)\left(a_{\ell}(f)^{2}-(\ell+1)-\frac{2(\ell+1)}{\ell}[\ell]\times[\ell]+(\ell+1)[\ell]\times[\ell]\right)(\kappa^{(6)}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}).

Instead of Proposition
2.1.1
, we use the following exact sequence

1\longrightarrow H_{m}\overset{\Delta}{\longrightarrow}H_{m}\times H_{m}\longrightarrow H_{m}\longrightarrow 1,

combining with the quotient
H_{m}\rightarrow H[m]
, which makes
[\ell]\times[\ell]
acting trivially on the cohomology class. After removing the extra factor
(\ell-1)
and multiplying with
-1
on the RHS factor, we obtain the correct Euler factor modulo
\ell^{2}-1
:

P_{\mathfrak{l}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}})=2+2\ell-a_{\ell}(f)^{2}.

Note that
(\ell+1)/\ell\equiv(\ell+1)\ell=\ell^{2}+\ell\equiv 1+\ell\pmod{\ell^{2}-1}
and the twist
\psi_{1}(\ell)\psi_{2}(\ell)/\ell^{2}=1
.

2.3.
Construction for general weights and wild norm relations

Keeping the setting introduced at the beginning of
\lx@sectionsign
2
, we now extend the construction of the preceding subsection to arbitrary weights
k\geq 1
and
k_{1},k_{2}\geq 1
, and prove that the resulting classes
\kappa_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
also satisfy the wild norm relations (i.e., they are universal norms for the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
). In this subsection, we assume in addition that

(2.11)

p=\mathfrak{p}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}
splits in
K
,

with
\mathfrak{p}
the prime of
K
above
p
induced by our fixed embedding
i_{p}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
,
and that

(2.12)

p\nmid h_{K}
,

where
h_{K}
is the class number of
K
.

Let
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
the Galois group of the unique
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
unramified outside
\mathfrak{p}
,
and let
\psi_{0}
be the unique Hecke character of
K
of infinity type
(-1,0)
, conductor
\mathfrak{p}
, and whose
\mathfrak{P}
-adic avatar factors through
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
. (As noted in
[
BL18
, §3.2.1]
, the uniqueness of
\psi_{0}
is a consequence of our assumption (
2.12
).) The characters
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
fixed at the beginning of this section can be uniquely written as

\psi_{i}=\xi_{i}\psi_{0}^{k_{i}-1},

where
\xi_{i}
is a ray class character of
K
of conductor dividing
\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{p}
. Viewing
\psi_{0}
and
\xi_{i}
as characters on
H_{\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}
(noting that
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a quotient the latter group), we consider the formal
q
-expansion

\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{i}}(q)=\sum_{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{p})=1}\xi_{i}\psi_{0}(\mathfrak{a})[\mathfrak{a}]q^{N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})}\in\Lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}[\![q]\!],

where
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathcal{O}[\![\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]\!]
. Identifying
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
\Gamma=1+p\mathbf{Z}_{p}
via the (geometrically normalised) local Artin map, inducing an identification
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}\simeq\mathcal{O}\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket
, then
\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{i}}
is the Hida family passing through
\theta_{\psi_{i}}
, in the sense that the specialisation of
\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{i}}
at weight
k_{i}
and trivial character recovers the ordinary
p
-stabilization of
\theta_{\psi_{i}}
(see
\lx@sectionsign
4.1.1
below for our conventions regarding Hida families).

In the following, we let
{\bm{f}}
be the Hida family associated to
f
, and

({\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})=(\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{2}})

be the CM Hida families associated to
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
, respectively. We also use
\kappa_{f}
,
\kappa_{g}
, and
\kappa_{h}
to denote the Dirichlet characters modulo
p
giving the
p
-part of the tame characters of
{\bm{f}}
,
{\bm{g}}
, and
{\bm{h}}
, respectively.

Let
\Lambda=\mathbf{Z}_{p}\llbracket{\Gamma}\rrbracket
. For each
i\in\mathbf{Z}/(p-1)\mathbf{Z}
denote by
\kappa_{i}:\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
the character

z\mapsto\omega^{i}(z)[\langle z\rangle]
, where
\omega:\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
is the reduction map composed with the Teichmüller lift, and
\langle z\rangle=z\omega^{-1}(z)\in 1+p\mathbf{Z}_{p}
.

Set
\mathrm{T}=\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}\times\mathbf{Z}_{p}
,
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}=p\mathbf{Z}_{p}\times\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. Let
\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}
be the maximal ideal of
\Lambda
, denote by
{\rm Cont}(\mathbf{Z}_{p},\Lambda)
the
\Lambda
-module of continuous functions on
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
with values in
\Lambda
, and put
\kappa=\kappa_{i}
for some
i\in\mathbf{Z}/(p-1)\mathbf{Z}
. We consider the
\Lambda
-modules

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\left\{f:\mathrm{T}\rightarrow\Lambda\;|\;f(1,z)\in\text{Cont}(\mathbf{Z}_{p},\Lambda)\text{ and }f(a\cdot t)=\kappa(a)\cdot f(t)\text{ for all }a\in\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times},\,t\in\mathrm{T}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left\{f:\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\rightarrow\Lambda\;|\;f(pz,1)\in\text{Cont}(\mathbf{Z}_{p},\Lambda)\text{ and }f(a\cdot t)=\kappa(a)\cdot f(t)\text{ for all }a\in\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times},\,t\in\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\right\}

equipped with the
\mathfrak{m}_{\Lambda}
-adic topology, and set

\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\text{cont},\Lambda}(\mathcal{A}_{\kappa},\Lambda),\quad\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime}=\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\text{cont},\Lambda}(\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\prime},\Lambda)

equipped with the weak-
\ast
topology.

As in
[
BSV22
, Eq. (81))]
, the evaluation
\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\cdot}\otimes_{\Lambda}\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\cdot}\rightarrow\Lambda
gives rise to a
\Lambda
-module homomorphism

\xi_{i}:H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{\kappa})\rightarrow{\rm Hom}_{\Lambda}(H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}),\Lambda).

Similarly, the determinant map
\det:\mathrm{T}\times\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
defined by
\det((x_{1},x_{2}),(y_{1},y_{2}))=x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1}
, composed with
\kappa_{i}:\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\times}
gives rise to

\zeta_{i}:\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits_{\Lambda}(H^{1}_{c}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}),\Lambda)\rightarrow H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})(-\kappa_{i}).

Then for any weight
k=r+2\geq 2
with
k\equiv i\pmod{p-1}
we have specialization maps

\rho_{r}:H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{\kappa})\rightarrow H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathscr{S}_{r}),\quad\rho_{r}:H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})\rightarrow H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathscr{L}_{r})

fitting into the commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{A}_{\kappa})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{i}\circ\xi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{r}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(\Gamma,\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\prime})(-\kappa_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{r}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathscr{S}_{r})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathtt{s}_{r}}
\textstyle{H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathscr{L}_{r})(-r).}

To ease notation, set
Y(m,p)=Y(1,Nm(p))
and denote by
\Gamma(m,p)
the associated congruence subgroup. Adopting the notations from
[
BSV22
, §8.1]
(but working the modules of continuous functions
\mathcal{A}_{\kappa}^{\cdot}
and their duals
\mathcal{D}_{\kappa}^{\cdot}
as above, rather than the analogous spaces of locally analytic functions considered in
[
BSV22
]
), we denote by

(2.13)

\widetilde{\bm{\kappa}}_{m}^{(1)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{f}})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{g}})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{h}})(2-\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*})\bigr)

the image of the element
\textbf{Det}_{Nm,p}^{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}\in H^{0}_{\et}(Y(m,p),\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa_{f}}^{\prime}\otimes\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa_{g}}\otimes\bm{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa_{h}}(-\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*}))
defined in
[
BSV22
, §8.1]
under the composition

\displaystyle H^{0}_{\et}\bigl(Y(m,p),\bm{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\kappa_{f}}^{\prime}\otimes\bm{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\kappa_{g}}\otimes\bm{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\kappa_{h}}(-\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*})\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\overset{d_{*}}{\longrightarrow}H_{\et}^{4}\bigl(Y(m,p)^{3},\bm{{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{f}}\boxtimes\bm{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\kappa_{g}}\boxtimes\bm{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\kappa_{h}}(-\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*})\otimes\mathbf{Z}_{p}(2)\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathtt{HS}}{\longrightarrow}H^{1}(\mathbf{Q},H^{3}_{\et}(Y(m,p)_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}}^{3},\bm{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\kappa_{f}}^{\prime}\boxtimes\bm{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\kappa_{g}}\boxtimes\bm{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\kappa_{h}})(2+\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*}))

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathtt{K}}{\longrightarrow}H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa_{f}}^{\prime})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa_{g}})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa_{h}})(2+\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*})\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\xrightarrow{(w_{p}\otimes 1\otimes 1)_{*}}H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa_{f}})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa_{g}})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),{\mathcal{A}}_{\kappa_{h}})(2+\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*})\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\xrightarrow{\mathtt{s}_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}}H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{f}})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{g}})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{h}})(2-\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*})\bigr),

where
\mathtt{s}_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}
is the tensor of the compositions
\zeta_{i}\circ\xi_{i}
for
i=\kappa_{f},\kappa_{g},\kappa_{h}
.

As we did in
\lx@sectionsign
2.2.1
, replacing the second and third copies of
Y(m,p)
in the above construction by the quotient
(Y(m,p)\times Y(m,p))/D_{m}
, where
D_{m}
is the group of diamond operators, we obtain the class

(2.14)

\bm{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{1}(\tilde{\Gamma}(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{f}})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{g}})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}[D_{m}]}H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{h}})(2-\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*})\bigr)

determined by the relation
\phi(m)\bm{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}=(\mu_{m*},d_{m*})\tilde{\bm{\kappa}}_{m}^{(1)}
, where
\tilde{\Gamma}(m,p)=\Gamma(1,N(mp))
, and we put

(2.15)

\bm{\kappa}_{m}^{(2)}:=(\pi_{m*},1,1)\bm{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}.

Proposition 2.3.1
.

For a prime number
\ell
and a positive integer
m
with
(m\ell,pN)=1
we have

(\pi_{i*},{\rm pr}_{j*},{\rm pr}_{k*})\bm{\kappa}_{m\ell}^{(1)}=(\bigstar)\bm{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)},

where

(i,j,k)

\bigstar

(\ell,1,1)

(T_{\ell},1,1)

(1,\ell,1)

(1,T_{\ell},1)

(1,1,\ell)

(1,1,T_{\ell})

(1,\ell,\ell)

\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*}(\ell)\kappa_{\bm{f}}^{*}(\ell)^{-1}(T_{\ell}^{\prime},1,1)

(\ell,1,\ell)

\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*}(\ell)\kappa_{\bm{g}}^{*}(\ell)^{-1}(1,T_{\ell}^{\prime},1)

(\ell,\ell,1)

\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*}(\ell)\kappa_{\bm{h}}^{*}(\ell)^{-1}(1,1,T_{\ell}^{\prime})

If we also have that
(\ell,m)=1
then

(i,j,k)

\bigstar

(1,1,1)

(\ell+1)

(\ell,\ell,\ell)

(\ell+1)\kappa_{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*}(\ell)

Proof.

With
\pi_{i}
replaced by
{\rm pr}_{i}
and the classes
\bm{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}
replaced by
\tilde{\bm{\kappa}}_{m}^{(1)}
, the stated relations with an extra factor of
\ell-1
follow immediately from equations (174) and (176) in
[
BSV22
]
(adding the prime
\ell
to the level, rather than
p
). The stated relations for
\bm{\kappa}_{m}^{(1)}
then follow in the same way as in Proposition
2.2.4
.
∎

Assume that

\xi_{i}\psi_{0}\not\equiv\omega\;\;({\rm mod}\,{\mathfrak{P}})

for
i=1,2
. Then for every
r\geq 0
Condition
\spadesuit
holds, and so by Theorem
1.3.3
, for every ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of norm
m
coprime to
p
the Hecke algebra homomorphism

\phi_{\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{r}}:\mathbb{T}(1,N_{\psi_{i}}mp^{r})^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{r}}]

associated to
\xi_{i}\psi_{0}
induces an isomorphism

\nu_{\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{r}}:H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{i}}mp^{r})_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1))\otimes\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{r}}^{(p)}]\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\rm Ind}_{K(\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{r})}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathcal{O}((\xi_{i}\psi_{0})^{-1})

safisfying the natural compatibility as
r
varies. On the other hand, as noted in
[
BSV22
, p. 38]
, from a slight variant of Lemma 6.8 in
[
GS93
]
we obtain a
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
-module isomorphisms

(2.16)

H^{1}(\Gamma(1,N_{\psi_{i}}m(p)),\mathcal{D}_{\kappa_{i}}^{\prime})(1)\simeq e_{i}\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}_{\et}(Y_{1}(N_{\psi_{i}}mp^{r})_{\overline{\mathbf{Q}}},\mathbf{Z}_{p}(1)).

Therefore combining (
2.16
) with the inverse limit of the isomorphisms
\nu_{\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{r}}
we obtain the
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
-equivariant isomorphisms

\nu_{\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}:H^{1}(\Gamma(1,N_{\psi_{i}}m(p)),\mathcal{D}_{\kappa_{i}}^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}[\![H_{\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}^{(p)}]\!]\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\rm Ind}_{K(\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m})}^{\mathbf{Q}}\Lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}((\xi_{i}\psi_{0})^{-1}).

Composing these with the natural degeneracy maps from level
\Gamma(m,p)
to level
\Gamma(1,N_{\psi_{i}}m(p))
and the projection
H_{\mathfrak{f}_{i}\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}\rightarrow H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}
, we then obtain
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
-equivariant maps

(2.17)

\displaystyle H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{1}})\otimes\mathcal{O}[\![H_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}^{(p)}]\!]

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathcal{O}_{(\xi_{1}\psi_{0})^{-1}}[H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}][\![\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]\!],

\displaystyle H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{2}})\otimes\mathcal{O}[\![H_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}^{(p)}]\!]

\displaystyle\rightarrow{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathcal{O}_{(\xi_{2}\psi_{0})^{-1}}[H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}][\![\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]\!].

Similarly as in
\lx@sectionsign
2.1
, the maps used in the construction of the class
\bm{\kappa}_{m}^{(2)}
in (
2.15
) are compatible under correspondences. Hence after tensoring with
\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{r}}^{(p)}]
and
\mathcal{O}[H_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\mathfrak{p}^{r}}^{(p)}]
using the maps
\phi_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{r}}
and
\phi_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{r}}
, respectively, and letting
r\rightarrow\infty
, the same construction gives rise to a class

\displaystyle\bm{\kappa}_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(3)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},H^{1}(\Gamma(1,N(p)),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{f}})

\displaystyle\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}(H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{g}})\otimes\mathcal{O}[\![H_{\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}^{(p)}]\!])

\displaystyle\quad\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}[D_{m}]}(H^{1}(\Gamma(m,p),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{\kappa_{h}})\otimes\mathcal{O}[\![H_{\mathfrak{f}_{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}^{(p)}]\!])(2-\kappa_{f{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*})\bigr).

Now let
\breve{f}
be a level-
N
test vector for
f
, and consider the associated specialization map

(2.18)

\pi_{f}:H^{1}(\Gamma(1,N(p),\mathcal{D}_{\kappa_{f}}^{\prime})(1)\rightarrow T_{f}^{\vee}.

Then taking the image of
\bm{\kappa}_{\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(3)}
under the natural maps induced by (
2.17
) and (
2.18
) we obtain

\bm{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(4)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}({\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathcal{O}_{(\xi_{1}\psi_{0})^{-1}}[H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}][\![\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]\!])\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}[D_{m}]}({\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\mathcal{O}_{(\xi_{2}\psi_{0})^{-1}}[H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}][\![\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]\!])(-1-\kappa_{f{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}^{*})\bigr),

which after specializing the third factor to weight
k_{2}
, i.e. taking the projection

\mathcal{O}_{(\xi_{2}^{-1}\psi_{0})^{-1}}[H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}][\![\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}]\!]\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\psi_{2}^{-1}}[H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}],

and applying the diagonal map
\xi_{\Delta}
in (
2.5
) finally gives rise to the class

(2.19)

\bm{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(5)}\in H^{1}\bigl(\mathbf{Q},T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\rm Ind}_{K[m]}^{\mathbf{Q}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}\kappa_{\rm ac}^{(k_{1}+k_{2}-4)/2}\bm{\kappa}_{\rm ac}^{-1})(1-(k_{1}+k_{2})/2)\bigr).

Here, we identify
\Ga^{-}=\text{Gal}(K_{\infty}^{-}/K)
with the anti-diagonal in
(1+p\mathbf{Z}_{p})\times(1+p\mathbf{Z}_{p})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathfrak{p}}^{(1)}\times\mathcal{O}_{K,\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{(1)}
via the geometric normalised Artin map, and define

\displaystyle\kappa_{ac}:\Ga^{-}\rightarrow\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times},\quad

\displaystyle\quad((1+p)^{-1/2},(1+p)^{1/2})\mapsto(1+p),

\displaystyle\bm{\kappa}_{ac}:\Ga^{-}\rightarrow\La^{\times},\quad

\displaystyle\quad((1+p)^{-1/2},(1+p)^{1/2})\mapsto[(1+p)].

Then, for
T
an
\mathcal{O}
-lattice inside a
G_{K}
-representation
V
, by Shapiro’s lemma we have

H^{1}\bigl(K,T\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{O}}(\bm{\kappa}_{\rm ac}^{-1})\bigr)\simeq H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(K[p^{\infty}],T),

where
H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(K[p^{\infty}],T):=\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}(K[p^{r}],T)
with limit under the corestriction maps. Thus in the following we shall view the class
\bm{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}^{(5)}
in (
2.19
) as an Iwasawa cohomology class

(2.20)

\bm{\kappa}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{1}_{\rm Iw}\bigl(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}\kappa_{\rm ac}^{(k_{1}+k_{2}-4)/2}(1-(k_{1}+k_{2})/2)\bigr)

for the self-dual representation
T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)
twisted by the Hecke character
\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}\mathbf{N}^{1-(k_{1}+k_{2})/2}
, which is anticyclotomic and of infinity type
((k_{1}+k_{2})/2-1,-(k_{1}+k_{2})/2+1)
. For the ease of notation, put

(2.21)

T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}=T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}(1-(k_{1}+k_{2})/2).

Thus we arrive at the following key result.

Theorem 2.3.2
.

Suppose
p\nmid 6h_{K}
and
f
is non-Eisenstein modulo
\mathfrak{P}
. Let
\mathfrak{m}
run over the ideals of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
divisible only by primes that are split in
K
with
m=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{m})
coprime to
p
.
Then there exists a collection of Iwasawa cohomology classes

\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{1}_{\rm Iw}\bigl(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}\bigr)

such that for every split prime
\mathfrak{l}
of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
of norm
\ell
with
(\ell,mp)=1
we have the norm relation

\mathrm{Norm}_{K[m]}^{K[m\ell]}(\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{ml}})=P_{\mathfrak{l}}(\mathrm{Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}})(\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}),

where
P_{\mathfrak{l}}(X)=\det(1-X\cdot\mathrm{Frob}_{\mathfrak{l}}\,|\,(T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})^{\vee}(1))
.

Proof.

This follows from a direct adaptation of the proof of Theorem
2.2.7
. The only difference is that this time we also invoke
[
Rub00
, Thm 6.3.5]
to go from a collection of Iwasawa cohomology classes for the twist

T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}\kappa_{\rm ac}^{(k_{1}+k_{2}-4)/2}(1-(k_{1}+k_{2})/2)=T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}\otimes\kappa_{\rm ac}^{(k_{1}+k_{2}-4)/2}

with the stated norm relations, to a similar collection of cohomology classes for
T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}
.
∎

3.
Anticyclotomic Euler systems

In this section we show that the system of classes constructed in Theorem
2.3.2
(and a variant thereof) land in certain Selmer groups defined in the style of Greenberg
[
Gre94
]
. As a result, our classes form an anticyclotomic Euler system in the sense of Jetchev–Nekovář–Skinner
[
JNS
]
. We then record the bounds on different Selmer groups that follow by applying their machinery to our construction.

Throughout we let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N_{f}))
be a
p
-ordinary newform of weight
k\geq 2
with
p\nmid N_{f}
, and
K/\mathbf{Q}
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant prime to
N_{f}
in which
p=\mathfrak{p}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}
splits.

3.1.
Selmer groups

Let
\chi
be an anticyclotomic Hecke character of
K
of infinity type
(-j,j)
for some
j\geq 0
, and consider the conjugate self-dual
G_{K}
-representation

V_{f,\chi}:=V_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\chi^{-1}.

Given a prime
v
of
K
above
p
and a
G_{K_{v}}
-stable subspace
\mathscr{F}_{v}^{+}(V_{f,\chi})\subset V_{f,\chi}
, we put
\mathscr{F}_{v}^{-}(V_{f,\chi})=V_{f,\chi}/\mathscr{F}_{v}^{+}(V_{f,\chi})
.

Definition 3.1.1
.

Let
L
be a finite extension of
K
, and fix
\mathscr{F}=\{\mathscr{F}_{v}^{+}(V_{f,\chi})\}_{v|p}
. The associated
Greenberg Selmer group

{\rm Sel}_{\mathscr{F}}(L,V_{f,\chi})
is defined by

{\rm Sel}_{\mathscr{F}}(L,V_{f,\chi}):={\rm ker}\biggl\{H^{1}(L,V_{f,\chi})\rightarrow\prod_{w}\frac{H^{1}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})}{H^{1}_{\mathscr{F}}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})}\biggr\},

where
w
runs over the finite primes of
L
, and the local conditions are given by

H^{1}_{\mathscr{F}}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})=\begin{cases}{\rm ker}\bigl\{H^{1}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{w}^{\rm ur},V_{f,\chi})\bigr\}&\textrm{if $w\nmid p$},\\[1.99997pt]
{\rm ker}\bigl\{H^{1}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{w},\mathscr{F}_{v}^{-}(V_{f,\chi}))\bigr\}&\textrm{if $w\mid v\mid p$}.\end{cases}

Given any lattice
T_{f,\chi}\subset V_{f,\chi}
, we let
H^{1}_{\mathscr{F}}(L_{w},T_{f,\chi})
be the inverse image of
H^{1}_{\mathscr{F}}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})
under the natural map
H^{1}(L_{w},T_{f,\chi})\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})
, and define
{\rm Sel}_{\mathscr{F}}(L,T_{f,\chi})
in the same manner; and given any
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
L_{\infty}=\bigcup_{n}L_{n}
of
L
, we put

{\rm Sel}_{\mathscr{F}}(L_{\infty},T_{f,\chi}):=\varprojlim_{n}{\rm Sel}_{\mathscr{F}}(L_{n},T_{f,\chi}),

with limit with respect to corestriction, and also put
{\rm Sel}_{\mathscr{F}}(L_{\infty},V_{f,\chi}):={\rm Sel}_{\mathscr{F}}(L_{\infty},T_{f,\chi})\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathbf{Q}_{p}
(which is independent of the chosen
T_{f,\chi}
).

We shall be particularly interested in the following two instances of these definitions:

•

The
relaxed-strict Selmer group

{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(L,V_{f,\chi})
obtained by taking

\mathscr{F}_{v}^{+}(V_{f,\chi})=\begin{cases}V_{f,\chi}&\textrm{if $v=\mathfrak{p}$,}\\[1.99997pt]
0&\textrm{if $v=\bar{\mathfrak{p}}$.}\end{cases}

•

The
ordinary Selmer group

{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(L,V_{f,\chi})
. Since
f
is
p
-ordinary, upon restriction to
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}\subset G_{\mathbf{Q}}
the Galois representation
V_{f}^{\vee}
fits into a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow V_{f}^{\vee,+}\rightarrow V_{f}^{\vee}\rightarrow V_{f}^{\vee,-}\rightarrow 0

with
V_{f}^{\vee,\pm}
one-dimensional, and with the
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
-action on
V_{f}^{\vee,-}
being unramified (see
\lx@sectionsign
1.2.2
). Then
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(L,V_{f,\chi})
is the Greenberg Selmer group defined by

(3.1)

\mathscr{F}_{v}^{+}(V_{f,\chi})=V_{f,\chi}^{+}:=V_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-k/2)\otimes\chi^{-1}

for all
v\mid p
.

Following
[
BK90
]
, we also define the Selmer group
{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(L,V_{f,\chi})
by

{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(L,V_{f,\chi}):={\rm ker}\biggl\{H^{1}(L,V_{f,\chi})\rightarrow\prod_{w}\frac{H^{1}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})}{H^{1}_{f}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})}\biggr\},

where as before
w
runs over the finite primes of
L
, and the local conditions are given by

H^{1}_{f}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})=\begin{cases}{\rm ker}\bigl\{H^{1}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{w}^{\rm ur},V_{f,\chi})\bigr\}&\textrm{if $w\nmid p$},\\[1.99997pt]
{\rm ker}\bigl\{H^{1}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi}\otimes\mathbf{B}_{\rm cris})\bigr\}&\textrm{if $w\mid p$},\end{cases}

with
\mathbf{B}_{\rm cris}
being Fontaine’s crystalline period ring. The local conditions
H^{1}_{f}(L_{w},T_{f,\chi})\subset H^{1}(L_{w},T_{f,\chi})
are then defined by propagation.

For our later convenience, we now recall the well-known relation between these different Selmer groups. Here we shall adopt the convention that the
p
-adic cyclotomic character has Hodge–Tate weight
-1
. Thus, since
\chi
has infinity type
(-j,j)
(see
\lx@sectionsign
1.3.1
for our convention regarding infinity types), the
p
-adic avatar of
\chi
has Hodge–Tate weight
j
at
\mathfrak{p}
and
-j
at
\bar{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Lemma 3.1.2
.

For any finite extension
L
of
K
we have

{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(L,V_{f,\chi})=\begin{cases}{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(L,V_{f,\chi})&\textrm{if $j\geq k/2$},\\[1.99997pt]
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(L,V_{f,\chi})&\textrm{if $0\leq j<k/2$}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Combining the results of
[
Nek00
, (3.1)-(3.2)]
and
[
Fla90
, Lem. 2, p. 125]
, for every prime
w|v|p
of
L/K/\mathbf{Q}
we have

H^{1}_{f}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})={\rm im}\bigl\{H^{1}(L_{w},{\rm Fil}_{v}^{1}(V_{f,\chi}))\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{w},V_{f,\chi})\bigr\},

where
{\rm Fil}_{v}^{1}(V_{f,\chi})\subset V_{f,\chi}
is a
G_{K_{v}}
-stable subspace (assuming it exists) such that the Hodge–Tate weights of
{\rm Fil}_{v}^{1}(V_{f,\chi})
(resp.
V_{f,\chi}/{\rm Fil}_{v}^{1}(V_{f,\chi})
) are all
<0
(resp.
\geq 0
).

Now, the Hodge–Tate weights of
V_{f,\chi}^{+}
and
V_{f,\chi}^{-}:=V_{f,\chi}/V_{f,\chi}^{+}
at the primes of
K
above
p
are given by:

V_{f,\chi}^{+}

V_{f,\chi}^{-}

HT weight at
\mathfrak{p}

-j-k/2

-j-1-k/2

HT weight at
\bar{\mathfrak{p}}

j-k/2

j-1+k/2

and so we find that
{\rm Fil}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}(V_{f,\chi})=V_{f,\chi}
and
{\rm Fil}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{1}(V_{f,\chi})=0
when
j\geq k/2
, and
{\rm Fil}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}(V_{f,\chi})={\rm Fil}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{1}(V_{f,\chi})=V_{f,\chi}^{+}
when
0\leq j<k/2
, yielding the equalities in the lemma.
∎

For
A_{f,\chi}:={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(T_{f,\chi},\mu_{p^{\infty}})
, and a choice of Galois stable subspaces
\mathscr{F}=\{\mathscr{F}_{v}^{+}(V_{f,\chi})\}_{v|p}
, we define the associated
dual Selmer group

{\rm Sel}_{\mathscr{F}^{*}}(L,A_{f,\chi})
by

{\rm Sel}_{\mathscr{F}^{*}}(L,A_{f,\chi}):={\rm ker}\biggl\{H^{1}(L,A_{f,\chi})\rightarrow\prod_{w}\frac{H^{1}(L_{w},A_{f,\chi})}{H^{1}_{\mathscr{F}^{*}}(L_{w},A_{f,\chi})}\biggr\},

where
H^{1}_{\mathscr{F}^{*}}(L_{w},A_{f,\chi})
is the orthogonal complement of
H^{1}_{\mathscr{F}}(L_{w},T_{f,\chi})
under local Tate duality

H^{1}(L_{w},T_{f,\chi})\times H^{1}(L_{w},A_{f,\chi})\rightarrow\mathbf{Q}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}_{p}.

In particular, we find that:

•

The dual Selmer group of
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(L,T_{f,\chi})
consists of classes that are unramified outside
p
and have the strict (resp. relaxed) condition at the primes
w|\mathfrak{p}
(resp.
w|\bar{\mathfrak{p}})
; we shall denote this by
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(L,A_{f,\chi})
.

•

The dual Selmer group of
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(L,T_{f,\chi})
consists of classes that are unramified outside
p
, and land in the image of the natural map

H^{1}(L_{w},\mathscr{F}_{v}^{+}(A_{f,\chi}))\rightarrow H^{1}(L_{w},A_{f,\chi}),\quad\textrm{$\mathscr{F}_{v}^{+}(A_{f,\chi}):={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(\mathscr{F}_{v}^{-}(T_{f,\chi}),\mu_{p^{\infty}})$,}

for
w|v|p
;
we shall denote this by
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(L,A_{f,\chi})
.

3.2.
Local conditions at
p

Let
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}
be Hecke characters of
K
of infinity type
(1-k_{1},0),(1-k_{2},0)
with
k_{1},k_{2}\geq 1
, and whose central characters satisfy
\chi_{\psi_{1}}\chi_{\psi_{2}}=1
.

By Theorem
2.3.2
we have classes

\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}\in H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}),

where
T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}=T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-1}(1-(k_{1}+k_{2})/2)
.
Replacing the map
\xi
in (
2.5
) by the map
\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2}\mapsto\xi^{\mathbf{c}}(\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2})
with
\xi^{\mathbf{c}}(\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2})(g)=\phi_{1}(g_{1})\otimes\phi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}(g_{2})
for
g=(g_{1},g_{2})\in H_{\mathfrak{m}}^{(p)}\times H_{\overline{\mathfrak{m}}}^{(p)}
, the same construction gives rise to classes

{}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}\in H^{1}_{\rm Iw}(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}}),

where
T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}}:=T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\psi_{1}^{-1}\psi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}(1-(k_{1}+k_{2})/2))
,
satisfying the same norm-compatibility relations as in Theorem
2.3.2
.

Proposition 3.2.1
.

For all ideals
\mathfrak{m}
of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
divisible only by primes split in
K
with
m=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{m})
coprime to
p
, the classes
\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
of Theorem
2.3.2
satisfy

\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}),

and similarly
{}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}})
.

Proof.

We shall adopt some of the notations introduced later in Section
4
. Let
{\bm{f}},{\bm{g}}=\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{1}}(S_{1}),{\bm{h}}=\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{2}}(S_{2})
be the Hida families associated to
f,\theta_{\psi_{1}},\theta_{\psi_{2}}
, respectively. By
[
BSV22
, Cor. 8.2]
, after projection to
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}
the class
\kappa_{m}^{(1)}
in (
2.14
) lands in the balanced Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
(see Definition
4.2.2
). Using that the big Galois representations associated to
{\bm{g}}
and
{\bm{h}}
are both induced from
K
, upon restriction to
G_{K}
the triple tensor product
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}
specialised to
f
decomposes as

(3.2)

{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}|_{G_{K}}=\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr),

and as in the proof of Proposition
5.3.1
, from Shapiro’s lemma we find that the local condition
\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\rm bal}({\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
cutting out the specialised balanced Selmer group at
p
corresponds to

\begin{split}\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm bal}({\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}|_{G_{K}})&=\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-k/2)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\\
\mathscr{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\rm bal}({\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}|_{G_{K}})&=\{0\}\end{split}\begin{split}&\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-k/2)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr),\\
&\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-k/2)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr).\end{split}

Since the diagonal map
\xi_{\Delta}
in (
2.5
) (resp. its twisted variant
\xi_{\Delta}^{\mathbf{c}}
) has the effect of projecting onto the first (resp. second) direct summand in (
3.2
), this shows that the classes
\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
(resp.
{}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
) satisfy the relaxed-strict (resp. ordinary-ordinary) condition at the primes above
p
. On the other hand, at the primes
w\nmid p
, because
V_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}
is conjugate self-dual and pure of weight
-1
, we see that

H^{0}(K[mp^{r}]_{w},V_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})=H^{2}(K[mp^{r}]_{w},V_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})=0

for all
r
, and therefore
H^{1}(K[mp^{r}]_{w},V_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})=0
by Tate’s local Euler characteristic formula. This shows that
H^{1}(K[mp^{r}]_{w},T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})
is torsion, and as a result the inclusion

{\rm res}_{w}(\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}})\in\varprojlim_{r}H^{1}_{f}(K[mp^{r}]_{w},T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})

follows automatically. Similarly, we see that the classes
{}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
are unramifed outside
p
, and since we have shown that they are ordinary at the primes above
p
, the result follows.
∎

3.3.
Applying the general machinery

We give some direct arithmetic applications that follow by applying to our construction the general Euler system machinery of Jetchev–Nekovář–Skinner
[
JNS
]
. Later in the paper, by exploiting the relation between our Euler system classes and special values of complex and
p
-adic
L
-functions, we shall deduce from these results applications to the Bloch–Kato conjecture and the anticyclotomic Iwasawa main conjecture.

For every ideal
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
as in Theorem
2.3.2
, denote by

z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K[m],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})

the image of
\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
under the projection
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})\rightarrow{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K[m],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})
, and put

z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}:={\rm cor}_{K[1]/K}(z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},1})\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}).

Similarly, projecting to
K[m]
the class
{}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}
define

{}^{\mathbf{c}}z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K[m],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}),

and put
{}^{\mathbf{c}}z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}:={\rm cor}_{K[1]/K}({}^{\mathbf{c}}z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},1})
.

3.3.1.
Rank one results

Theorem 3.3.1
.

Assume that
f
is not of CM-type and is non-Eisenstein at
\mathfrak{P}
, and that
p\nmid h_{K}
.

(I)

If
z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}\neq 0
, then
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,V_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})
is one-dimensional.

(II)

If
{}^{\mathbf{c}}z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}\neq 0
, then
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,V_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}})
is one-dimensional.

Proof.

Denote by
\mathcal{N}
the set of squarefree products of primes
\mathfrak{l}
of
\mathcal{O}_{K}
split in
K
and with
\ell=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{l})
coprime to
p
. By Theorem
2.3.2
and Proposition
3.2.1
, the system of classes

(3.3)

\bigl\{z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K[m],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})\;\colon\;\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{N}\bigr\}

forms an anticyclotomic Euler system in the sense of Jetchev–Nekovář–Skinner
[
JNS
]
for the relaxed-strict Greenberg Selmer group.
Hence from their general results the one-dimensionality of
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,V_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})
follows from the nonvanishing of
z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}
provided the
G_{K}
-representation
V=V_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}
satisfies the following hypotheses:

(i)

V
is absolutely irreducible;

(ii)

There is an element
\sigma\in G_{K}
fixing
K[1]K(\mu_{p^{\infty}},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{1/p^{\infty}})
such that
V/(\sigma-1)V
is one-dimensional;

(iii)

There is an element
\gamma\in G_{K}
fixing
K[1]K(\mu_{p^{\infty}},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{1/p^{\infty}})
such that
V^{\gamma=1}=0
.

Since we assume that
f
is not of CM-type, hypotheses (i)–(iii) follow easily from Momose’s big image results
[
Mom81
]
as in
[
LLZ15
, Prop. 7.1.4]
, whence the first part of the theorem; the proof of the second part is the same.
∎

3.3.2.
Iwasawa-theoretic results

Denote by
K_{\infty}^{-}
the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
K[p^{\infty}]/K
, and put
\Lambda_{K}^{-}=\mathbf{Z}_{p}\llbracket{{\rm Gal}(K_{\infty}^{-}/K)}\rrbracket
.
Let
\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},1}
be the
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
-adic class of Theorem
2.3.2
of conductor
\mathfrak{m}=(1)
, and put

\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}:={\rm cor}_{K[1]/K}(\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},1})\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}),

where the inclusion follows from Proposition
3.2.1
. Similarly, put

{}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}:={\rm cor}_{K[1]/K}({}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},1})\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}}).

Notation 3.3.2
.

As in
[
LLZ15
, §7.1]
, we shall say that
f

has big image at
\mathfrak{P}
if the image of
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
in
{\rm Aut}_{\mathcal{O}}(T_{f}^{\vee})
contains a conjugate of
{\rm SL}_{2}(\mathbf{Z}_{p})
.

We also note that, by a theorem of Ribet
[
Rib85
]
, if
f
is not of CM-type then it has big image for all but finitely many primes of
L
.

Put

X_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},A_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\bigl(\varinjlim{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K_{n}^{-},A_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}),\mathbf{Q}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}_{p}\bigr)

and likewise for
X_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},A_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}})
.

The next result can be seen as a divisibility towards an anticyclotomic Iwasawa main conjecture ‘without
L
-functions’.

Theorem 3.3.3
.

Assume that
f
is not of CM-type and it has big image at
\mathfrak{P}
, and that
p\nmid h_{K}
.

(I)

If
\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}
is non-torsion, then
X_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},A_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})
and
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})
both have
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
-rank one, and we have the divisibility

{\rm char}_{\Lambda_{K}^{-}}(X_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},A_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})_{\rm tors})\supset{\rm char}_{\Lambda_{K}^{-}}\biggl(\frac{{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})}{\Lambda_{K}^{-}\cdot\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}}\biggr)^{2}

in
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
.

(II)

If
{}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}
is non-torsion, then
X_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},A_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}})
and
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}})
both have
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
-rank one, and we have the divisibility

{\rm char}_{\Lambda_{K}^{-}}(X_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},A_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}})_{\rm tors})\supset{\rm char}_{\Lambda_{K}^{-}}\biggl(\frac{{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}})}{\Lambda_{K}^{-}\cdot{}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}}\biggr)^{2}

in
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
.

Here, in both
{\rm(I)}
and
{\rm(II)}
, the subscript
{\rm tors}
denotes the
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
-torsion submodule.

Proof.

With notations as in the proof of Theorem
3.3.1
, by Theorem
2.3.2
and Proposition
3.2.1
the system of classes

(3.4)

\bigl\{\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}})\,\colon\;\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{N}\bigr\}

forms a
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
-adic anticyclotomic Euler system in the sense of Jetchev–Nekovář–Skinner for the relaxed-strict Selmer group, and so the non-torsionness of
\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}
implies the conclusions in part (I) of the theorem provided the
G_{K}
-module
T=T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}}
satisfies the following hypotheses:

(i)

\bar{T}:=T/\mathfrak{P}T
is absolutely irreducible;

(ii)

There is an element
\sigma\in G_{K}
fixing
K[1]K(\mu_{p^{\infty}},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{1/p^{\infty}})
such that
T/(\sigma-1)T
is free of rank
1
over
\mathcal{O}
;

(iii)

There is an element
\gamma\in G_{K}
fixing
K[1]K(\mu_{p^{\infty}},(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{1/p^{\infty}})
and acting as multiplication by a scalar
a_{\gamma}\neq 1
on
\bar{T}
;

but these follow easily from the assumption that
f
has big image at
\mathfrak{P}
(see
[
LLZ15
, Prop. 7.1.6]
). This shows part (I) of the theorem, and part (II) follows in the same manner.
∎

Part II
Applications

4.
Preliminaries

In this section we briefly review the unbalanced triple product
p
-adic
L
-function constructed in
[
Hsi21
]
, their associated Selmer groups, and the explicit construction of certain CM Hida families. We also recall from
[
BSV22
]
the explicit reciprocity law for diagonal classes,
and Greenberg’s formulation of the Iwasawa main conjecture for triple products following
[
ACR21
]
.

4.1.
Triple product
p
-adic
L
-function

4.1.1.
Hida families

Let
\mathbb{I}
be a normal domain finite flat over

\Lambda:=\mathcal{O}\llbracket{1+p\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\rrbracket,

where
\mathcal{O}
is the ring of integers of a finite extension of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
. For a positive integer
N
prime
p
and a Dirichlet character
\chi:(\mathbf{Z}/Np\mathbf{Z})^{\times}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\times}
, we denote by
S^{o}(N,\chi,\mathbb{I})\subset\mathbb{I}\llbracket{q}\rrbracket
the space of ordinary
\mathbb{I}
-adic cusp forms of tame level
N
and branch character
\chi
as defined in
[
Hsi21
, §3.1]
.

Denote by
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{+}\subset{\rm Spec}\,\mathbb{I}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
the set of
arithmetic points
of
\mathbb{I}
, consisting of the ring homomorphisms
Q:\mathbb{I}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
such that
Q|_{1+p\mathbf{Z}_{p}}
is given by
z\mapsto z^{k_{Q}-1}\epsilon_{Q}(z)
for some
k_{Q}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 2}
called the
weight of
Q
and
\epsilon_{Q}(z)\in\mu_{p^{\infty}}
. As in
[
Hsi21
, §3.1]
, we say that
\bm{f}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{n}({\bm{f}})q^{n}\in S^{o}(N,\chi,\mathbb{I})
is a
primitive Hida family
if for every
Q\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{+}
the specialization
\bm{f}_{Q}
gives the
q
-expansion of an ordinary
p
-stabilised newform of weight
k_{Q}
and tame conductor
N
. Attached to such
{\bm{f}}
we let
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{\rm cls}
be the set of ring homomorphisms
Q
as above with
k_{Q}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 1}
such that
{\bm{f}}_{Q}
is the
q
-expansion of a classical modular form (thus
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{\rm cls}
contains
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{+}
).

For
{\bm{f}}
a primitive Hida family of tame level
N
, we let

\rho_{{\bm{f}}}:G_{\mathbf{Q}}\rightarrow{\rm Aut}_{\mathbb{I}}(V_{\bm{f}})\simeq{\rm GL}_{2}(\mathbb{I})

denote the associated Galois representation, which here we take to be the
dual
of that in
[
Hsi21
, §3.2]
; in particular, the determinant of
\rho_{\bm{f}}
is
\chi_{\mathbb{I}}\cdot\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}
in the notations of
loc. cit.
, where
\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}
is the
p
-adic cyclotomic character. By
[
Wil88
, Thm. 2.2.2]
, restricted to
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
the Galois representation
V_{\bm{f}}
fits into a short exact sequence

0\rightarrow V_{\bm{f}}^{+}\rightarrow V_{\bm{f}}\rightarrow V_{\bm{f}}^{-}\rightarrow 0,

where the quotient
V_{\bm{f}}^{-}
is free of rank one over
\mathbb{I}
, with the
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
-action given by the unramified character sending an arithmetic Frobenius
{\rm Frob}_{p}^{-1}
to
a_{p}({\bm{f}})
.

Denote by
\mathbb{T}(N,\mathbb{I})
the Hecke algebra acting on
\bigoplus_{\chi}S^{o}(N,\chi,\mathbb{I})
, where
\chi
runs over the characters of
(\mathbf{Z}/Np\mathbf{Z})^{\times}
. Associated with
{\bm{f}}
there is a
\mathbb{I}
-algebra homomorphism

\lambda_{{\bm{f}}}:\mathbb{T}(N,\mathbb{I})\rightarrow\mathbb{I}

factoring through a local component
\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{m}}
. Following
[
Hid88a
]
define the
congruence ideal

C({\bm{f}})
of
{\bm{f}}
by

C({\bm{f}}):=\lambda_{{\bm{f}}}({\rm Ann}_{\mathbb{T}_{\mathfrak{m}}}({\rm ker}\,\lambda_{\bm{f}}))\subset\mathbb{I}.

If the residual representation
\bar{\rho}_{{\bm{f}}}
is absolutely irreducible and
p
-distinguished, it follows from the results of
[
Wil95
]
and
[
Hid88a
]
that
C({\bm{f}})
is generated by a nonzero element
\eta_{{\bm{f}}}\in\mathbb{I}
.

4.1.2.
Triple products of Hida families

Let

({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})\in S^{o}(N_{f},\chi_{f},\mathbb{I}_{f})\times S^{o}(N_{g},\chi_{g},\mathbb{I}_{g})\times S^{o}(N_{h},\chi_{h},\mathbb{I}_{h})

be a triple of primitive Hida families with

(4.1)

\chi_{f}\chi_{g}\chi_{h}=\omega^{2a}
for some
a\in\mathbf{Z}
,

where
\omega
is the Teichmüller character. Put

\mathcal{R}=\mathbb{I}_{f}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{I}_{g}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{I}_{h},

which is a finite extension of the three-variable Iwasawa algebra
\Lambda\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\Lambda\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\Lambda
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{+}\subset{\rm Spec}\,\mathcal{R}(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
be the weight space of
\mathcal{R}
given by

\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{+}:=\left\{\underline{Q}=(Q_{0},Q_{1},Q_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}_{f}}^{+}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}_{g}}^{\rm cls}\times\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}_{h}}^{\rm cls}\;:\;k_{Q_{0}}+k_{Q_{1}}+k_{Q_{2}}\equiv 0\;({\rm mod}\;2)\right\}.

This can be written as the disjoint union
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{+}=\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{\rm bal}\sqcup\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{{\bm{f}}}\sqcup\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{{\bm{g}}}\sqcup\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{{\bm{h}}}
, where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\rm bal}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\underline{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{+}\;:\;\textrm{$k_{Q_{0}}+k_{Q_{1}}+k_{Q_{2}}>2k_{Q_{i}}$ for all $i=0,1,2$}\right\}

is the set of
balanced
weights, where each weight
k_{Q_{i}}
is smaller than the sum of the other two, and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\bm{f}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\underline{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{+}\;:\;\textrm{$k_{Q_{0}}\geq k_{Q_{1}}+k_{Q_{2}}$}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\bm{f}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\underline{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{+}\;:\;\textrm{$k_{Q_{1}}\geq k_{Q_{0}}+k_{Q_{2}}$}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\bm{f}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\underline{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{+}\;:\;\textrm{$k_{Q_{2}}\geq k_{Q_{0}}+k_{Q_{1}}$}\right\},

are the set of
{\bm{f}}
- (resp.
{\bm{g}}
-,
{\bm{h}}
-)
unbalanced
weights.

Let
\mathbf{V}=V_{\bm{f}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{g}}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{h}}}
be the triple tensor product Galois representation attached to
({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
. Write the determinant of
\mathbf{V}
in the form
\det\mathbf{V}=\mathcal{X}^{2}\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}
(note that this is possible by (
4.1
)), and put

(4.2)

{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}:=\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{-1},

which is a self-dual twist of
\mathbf{V}
. Define the rank four
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
-invariant subspace
\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\bm{f}}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})\subset{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}
by

(4.3)

\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\bm{f}}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}):=V_{{\bm{f}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{\bm{g}}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{h}}}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{-1},

and for any
\underline{Q}=(Q_{0},Q_{1},Q_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\bm{f}}
denote by
\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\bm{f}}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}})\subset{\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}
the corresponding specialisations.

For a rational prime
\ell
, let
\varepsilon_{\ell}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}})
be the epsilon factor attached to the local representation
{\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}|_{G_{\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}}}
(see
[
Tat79
, p. 21]
), and assume that

(4.4)

for some
\underline{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\bm{f}}
, we have
\varepsilon_{\ell}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}})=+1
for all prime factors
\ell
of
N_{f}N_{g}N_{h}
.

As explained in
[
Hsi21
, §1.2]
, it is known that condition (
4.4
) is independent of
\underline{Q}
, and it implies that the sign in the functional equation for the triple product
L
-function (with center at
s=0
)

L({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}},s)

is
+1
(resp.
-1
) for all
\underline{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\bm{f}}\cup\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\bm{g}}\cup\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\bm{h}}
(resp.
\underline{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\rm bal}
).

Theorem 4.1.1
.

Let
({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
be a triple of primitive Hida families satisfying conditions (
4.1
) and (
4.4
). Assume in addition that:

•

\gcd(N_{f},N_{g},N_{h})
is square-free,

•

the residual representation
\bar{\rho}_{{\bm{f}}}
is absolutely irreducible and
p
-distinguished,

and fix a generator
\eta_{{\bm{f}}}
of the congruence ideal of
{\bm{f}}
. Then there exists a unique element

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{{\bm{f}}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})\in\mathcal{R}

such that for all
\underline{Q}=(Q_{0},Q_{1},Q_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{{\bm{f}}}
of weight
(k_{0},k_{1},k_{2})
with
\epsilon_{Q_{0}}=1
we have

\bigl(\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{\bm{f}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})(\underline{Q})\bigr)^{2}=\Gamma_{{\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}}(0)\cdot\frac{L({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}},0)}{(\sqrt{-1})^{2k_{0}}\cdot\Omega_{{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}}^{2}}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{p}(\mathscr{F}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}))\cdot\prod_{q\in\Sigma_{\rm exc}}(1+q^{-1})^{2},

where:

•

\Gamma_{{\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}}(0)=\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(c_{\underline{Q}})\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(c_{\underline{Q}}+2-k_{1}-k_{2})\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(c_{\underline{Q}}+1-k_{1})\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(c_{\underline{Q}}+1-k_{2})
, with

c_{\underline{Q}}=(k_{0}+k_{1}+k_{2}-2)/2

and
\Gamma_{\mathbf{C}}(s)=2(2\pi)^{-s}\Gamma(s)
;

•

\Omega_{{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}}
is the canonical period

\Omega_{{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}}:=(-2\sqrt{-1})^{k_{0}+1}\cdot\frac{\|{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}^{\circ}\|_{\Gamma_{0}(N_{f})}^{2}}{\eta_{{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}}}\cdot\Bigl(1-\frac{\chi_{f}^{\prime}(p)p^{k_{0}-1}}{\alpha_{Q_{0}}^{2}}\Bigr)\Bigl(1-\frac{\chi_{f}^{\prime}(p)p^{k_{0}-2}}{\alpha_{Q_{0}}^{2}}\Bigr),

with
{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}^{\circ}\in S_{k_{0}}(\Gamma_{0}(N_{f}))
the newform of conductor
N_{f}
associated with
{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}
,
\chi_{f}^{\prime}
the prime-to-
p
part of
\chi_{f}
, and
\alpha_{Q_{0}}
the specialisation of
a_{p}({\bm{f}})\in\mathbb{I}_{f}^{\times}
at
Q_{0}
;

•

\mathcal{E}_{p}(\mathscr{F}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}))
is the modified
p
-Euler factor

\mathcal{E}_{p}(\mathscr{F}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}})):=\frac{L_{p}(\mathscr{F}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}),0)}{\varepsilon_{p}(\mathscr{F}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}))\cdot L_{p}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}/\mathscr{F}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}),0)}\cdot\frac{1}{L_{p}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}},0)},

and
\Sigma_{\rm exc}
is an explicitly defined subset of the prime factors of
N_{f}N_{g}N_{h}
,
[
Hsi21
, p. 416]
.

Proof.

This is Theorem A in
[
Hsi21
]
, which in fact proves a more general interpolation formula.
∎

Remark 4.1.2
.

The construction of the
p
-adic
L
-function

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{{\bm{f}}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
is based on the
p
-adic Rankin–Selberg method of Hida
[
Hid88b
]
, and the proof of the above exact interpolation formula relies on a suitable choice
(\breve{{\bm{f}}}^{\star},\breve{{\bm{g}}}^{\star},\breve{{\bm{h}}}^{\star})
of level-
N
test vectors for
({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
.
In general, for any choice
(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})
of level-
N
test vectors, Hida’s method produces an element

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})\in{\rm Frac}(\mathbb{I}_{f})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{I}_{g}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{I}_{h},

and by virtue of the proof of Jacquet’s conjecture by Harris–Kudla
[
HK91
]
, for any
\underline{Q}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\bm{f}}

one can find
(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})
such that

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})(\underline{Q})\neq 0\;\Longleftrightarrow\;L(\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger},0)\neq 0.

In particular, if the central
L
-value
L(\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger},0)
is nonzero, then
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})\neq 0
. (In the above notation, we have
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{\bm{f}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})=\eta_{\bm{f}}\cdot\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\bm{f}}(\breve{{\bm{f}}}^{\star},\breve{{\bm{g}}}^{\star},\breve{{\bm{h}}}^{\star})
.)

4.2.
Triple product Selmer groups

Let
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}=\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{-1}
be the self-dual twist of the Galois representation associated to a triple of primitive Hida families
({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
satisfying (
4.1
).

Definition 4.2.1
.

Put

\mathscr{F}^{\rm bal}_{p}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}):=\bigl(V_{{\bm{f}}}\otimes V_{{\bm{g}}}^{+}\otimes V_{{\bm{h}}}^{+}+V_{{\bm{f}}}^{+}\otimes V_{{\bm{g}}}\otimes V_{{\bm{h}}}^{+}+V_{{\bm{f}}}^{+}\otimes V_{{\bm{g}}}^{+}\otimes V_{{\bm{h}}}\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{X}^{-1},

and define the
balanced local condition

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
by

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}):={\rm im}\bigl({\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\rm bal}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}))\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})\bigr).

Similarly, put
\mathscr{F}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}):=\bigl(V_{{\bm{f}}}^{+}\otimes V_{{\bm{g}}}\otimes V_{{\bm{h}}}\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{X}^{-1}
, and define the
{\bm{f}}
-unbalanced local condition

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
by

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}):={\rm im}\bigl({\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathscr{F}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}))\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})\bigr).

It is easy to see that the maps appearing in these definitions are injective, and in the following we shall use this to identify
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{?}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
with
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathscr{F}_{p}^{?}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}))
for
?\in\{{\rm bal},{\bm{f}}\}
.

Definition 4.2.2
.

Let
?\in\{{\rm bal},{\bm{f}}\}
, and define the Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{?}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
by

{\rm Sel}^{?}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}):=\mathop{\rm ker}\nolimits\biggl\{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})\rightarrow\frac{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})}{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{?}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})}\times\prod_{v\neq p}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{v}^{\rm nr},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})\biggr\}.

We call
{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
(resp.
{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
) the
balanced
(resp.
{\bm{f}}
-unbalanced
) Selmer group.

Let
{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}}={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}},\mu_{p^{\infty}})
and for
?\in\{{\rm bal},{\bm{f}}\}
define
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{?}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})\subset{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})
to be the orthogonal complement of
{\mathrm{H}}^{1}_{?}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
under the local Tate duality

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})\times{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})\rightarrow\mathbf{Q}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}_{p}.

Similarly as above, we then define the balanced and
{\bm{f}}
-unbalanced Selmer groups with coefficients in
{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}}
by

{\rm Sel}^{?}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}}):=\mathop{\rm ker}\nolimits\biggl\{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})\rightarrow\frac{{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})}{{\mathrm{H}}_{?}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})}\times\prod_{v\neq p}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{v}^{\rm nr},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})\biggr\},

and let
X^{?}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}({\rm Sel}^{?}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}}),\mathbf{Q}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}_{p})
denote the Pontryagin dual of
{\rm Sel}^{?}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})
.

4.3.
Diagonal classes

We continue to denote by
({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
a triple of primitive Hida families as in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsubsec:hida}
satisfying (
4.1
), and put
N={\rm lcm}(N_{f},N_{g},N_{h})
. Let

(4.5)

\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}(N))

be the big diagonal class constructed in
[
BSV22
, §8.1]
, where
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}(N)
is a free
\mathcal{R}
-module isomorphic to finitely many copies of
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}
. (Note that this is essentially the class
\widetilde{\bm{\kappa}}_{m}^{(1)}
in (
2.13
) with
m=1
.) The definition of the Selmer groups in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:tripleSelmer}
extends immediately to
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}(N)
, and by Corollary 8.2 in
loc. cit.
one knows that
\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})\in{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}(N))
.

Put

\mathscr{F}_{p}^{3}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})=V_{\bm{f}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{g}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{h}}}^{+}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{-1}\subset{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}.

Then clearly
\mathscr{F}_{p}^{3}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})\subset\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\rm bal}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
, with quotient given by

(4.6)

\mathscr{F}^{\rm bal}_{p}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})/\mathscr{F}_{p}^{3}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})\cong\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{f}}}^{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}\oplus\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{g}}}^{{\bm{f}}{\bm{h}}}\oplus\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{h}}}^{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}},

where

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{f}}}^{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}

\displaystyle=V_{\bm{f}}^{-}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{g}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{h}}}^{+}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{g}}}^{{\bm{f}}{\bm{h}}}

\displaystyle=V_{\bm{f}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{g}}}^{-}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{h}}}^{+}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{h}}}^{{\bm{f}}{\bm{g}}}

\displaystyle=V_{\bm{f}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{g}}}^{+}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}V_{{\bm{h}}}^{-}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{-1}.

4.3.1.
Reciprocity law

Assume that the congruence ideal
C({\bm{f}})\subset\mathbb{I}_{f}
is principal, generated by the nonzero
\eta_{\bm{f}}\in\mathbb{I}_{f}
.
As explained in
[
BSV22
, §7.3]
, one can deduce from results in
[
KLZ17
]
the construction of an injective three-variable
p
-adic regulator map with pseudo-null cokernel

(4.8)

{\rm Log}^{\eta_{\bm{f}}}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{f}}}^{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{R}

characterised by the property that for all
\mathfrak{Z}\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{f}}}^{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})
and all points
\underline{Q}=(Q_{0},Q_{1},Q_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{{\bm{f}}}
of weight
(k_{0},k_{1},k_{2})
with
\epsilon_{Q_{i}}=1
(
i=0,1,2
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm Log}^{\eta_{\bm{f}}}(\mathfrak{Z})(\underline{Q})}{\eta_{{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}}}

\displaystyle=(p-1)\alpha_{Q_{0}}\biggl(1-\frac{\beta_{Q_{0}}\alpha_{Q_{1}}\alpha_{Q_{2}}}{p^{c_{\underline{Q}}}}\biggr)\biggl(1-\frac{\alpha_{Q_{0}}\beta_{Q_{1}}\beta_{Q_{2}}}{p^{c_{\underline{Q}}}}\biggr)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\begin{cases}\frac{(-1)^{c_{\underline{Q}}-k_{0}}}{(c_{\underline{Q}}-k_{0})!}\cdot\left\langle{\rm log}_{p}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\underline{Q}}),\eta_{{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}}\otimes\omega_{{\bm{g}}_{Q_{1}}}\otimes\omega_{{\bm{h}}_{Q_{2}}}\right\rangle_{\rm dR},&\textrm{if $\underline{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{\rm bal}$,}\\[5.0pt]
(k_{0}-c_{\underline{Q}}-1)!\cdot\left\langle{\rm exp}_{p}^{*}(\mathfrak{Z}_{\underline{Q}}),\eta_{{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}}\otimes\omega_{{\bm{g}}_{Q_{1}}}\otimes\omega_{{\bm{h}}_{Q_{2}}}\right\rangle_{\rm dR},&\textrm{if $\underline{Q}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{R}}^{{\bm{f}}}$.}\end{cases}

Here,
c_{\underline{Q}}=(k_{0}+k_{1}+k_{2}-2)/2
is as in Theorem
4.1.1
,
\alpha_{Q_{0}}
denotes the specialisation of
a_{p}({\bm{f}})
at
Q_{0}
, we put
\beta_{Q_{0}}=\chi_{f}^{\prime}(p)p^{k_{0}-1}\alpha_{Q_{0}}^{-1}
, and
(\alpha_{Q_{1}},\beta_{Q_{1}})
(resp.
(\alpha_{Q_{2}},\beta_{Q_{2}})
) are defined likewise with
{\bm{g}}
(resp.
{\bm{h}}
) in place of
{\bm{f}}
.

Denote by
{\rm res}_{p}(\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}))_{{\bm{f}}}
the image of
\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
under natural map

(4.9)

{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})\xrightarrow{{\rm res}_{p}}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\rm bal}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}))\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\rm bal}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})/\mathscr{F}_{p}^{3}({\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}))\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{f}}}^{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})

arising from the restriction at
p
and the projection onto the first direct summand in (
4.6
).

Theorem 4.3.1
.

Let
({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
be a triple of primitive Hida families as in Theorem
4.1.1
. Then

{\rm Log}^{\eta_{\bm{f}}}({\rm res}_{p}(\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}))_{\bm{f}})=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{\bm{f}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}).

Proof.

This is Theorem A in
[
BSV22
]
(see also
[
DR22
, Thm. 10]
).
∎

Remark 4.3.2
.

The map
{\rm Log}^{\eta_{{\bm{f}}}}
in (
4.8
) depends on a choice of level-
N
test vectors
(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})
, and implictly in the above statements we took the triple
(\breve{{\bm{f}}}^{\star},\breve{{\bm{g}}}^{\star},\breve{{\bm{h}}}^{\star})
constructed in
[
Hsi21
]
. For any triple
(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})
, one deduces from
[
KLZ17
]
the existence of a
p
-adic regulator map

{\rm Log}_{(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbf{V}_{{\bm{f}}}^{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})\rightarrow{\rm Frac}(\mathbb{I}_{f})\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{I}_{g}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{I}_{h}

characterised by a similar interpolation property, and the explictly reciprocity law of Theorem
4.3.1
applies more generally to give

{\rm Log}_{(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})}({\rm res}_{p}(\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}))_{\bm{f}})=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}}),

where
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})
is as in Remark
4.1.2
.

4.3.2.
Iwasawa–Greenberg main conjectures

Let
({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
be a triple of primitive Hida families as in Theorem
4.1.1
, and assume that the associated ring
\mathcal{R}
is regular. As explained in
[
ACR21
, §7.3]
, the following result can be seen as the equivalence between two different formulation of the Iwasawa main conjecture in the style of Greenberg
[
Gre94
]
for the
p
-adic deformation
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}
.

Proposition 4.3.3
.

The following statements
(I)
and
(II)
are equivalent:

(I)

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{{\bm{f}}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
is nonzero, the modules
{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
and
X^{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})
are both
\mathcal{R}
-torsion, and

{\rm char}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(X^{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})\bigr)=\bigl(\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\bm{f}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})^{2}\bigr)

in
\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathbf{Q}_{p}
.

(II)

\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
is not
\mathcal{R}
-torsion, the modules
{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})
and
X^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})
have both
\mathcal{R}
-rank one, and

{\rm char}_{\mathcal{R}}\bigl(X^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{A}^{\dagger}})_{\rm tors}\bigr)={\rm char}_{\mathcal{R}}\biggl(\frac{{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}})}{\mathcal{R}\cdot\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})}\biggr)^{2}

in
\mathcal{R}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathbf{Q}_{p}
, where the subscript
{\rm tors}
denotes the
\mathcal{R}
-torsion submodule.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
4.3.1
and global duality in the same way as
[
ACR21
, Thm. 7.15]
. See
[
Lai22
]
for the details in the stated level of generality.
∎

4.4.
CM Hida families

We conclude this section with the explicit construction of certain CM Hida families, following the exposition in
[
Hsi21
, §8.1]
.
Let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D_{K}<0
, and suppose that
p=\mathfrak{p}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}
splits in
K
,
with
\mathfrak{p}
the prime of
K
above
p
induced by our fixed embedding
\imath_{p}:\overline{\mathbf{Q}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p}
.

Let
K_{\infty}
be the
\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{2}
-extension of
K
, and denote by
K_{\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}
the maximal subfield of
K_{\infty}
unramified outside
\mathfrak{p}
. Put

\Gamma_{\infty}:={\rm Gal}(K_{\infty}/K)\simeq\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{2},\quad\quad\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}:={\rm Gal}(K_{\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}/K)\simeq\mathbf{Z}_{p}.

For every ideal
\mathfrak{c}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
we denote by
K[\mathfrak{c}]
the ray class field of
K
of conductor
\mathfrak{c}
(so in particular
K_{\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}
is the maximal
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
inside
K[\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}]
). Denote by
{\rm Art}_{\mathfrak{p}}
the restriction of the Artin map to
K_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
, with geometric normalisation. Then
{\rm Art}_{\mathfrak{p}}
induces an embedding
1+p\mathbf{Z}_{p}\rightarrow\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
, where we identified
\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and
\mathcal{O}_{K_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
via
\iota_{p}
. Write
I_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm w}={\rm Art}_{\mathfrak{p}}(1+p\mathbf{Z}_{p})|_{K_{\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}}
and put
[\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}:I_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm w}]=p^{b}
.

Fix a topological generator
\gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\Gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}
with
\gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}^{p^{b}}={\rm Art}_{\mathfrak{p}}(1+p)|_{K_{\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}}
, and for each variable
S
let
\Psi_{S}:\Gamma_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S}\rrbracket^{\times}
be the universal character given by

\Psi_{S}(\sigma)=(1+S)^{l(\sigma)},

where
l(\sigma)\in\mathbf{Z}_{p}
is such that
\sigma|_{K_{\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}}=\gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}^{l(\sigma)}
. Upon possibly enlarging
\mathcal{O}
, assume that it contains an element
\mathbf{v}
with
\mathbf{v}^{p^{b}}=1+p
. Assume that
\mathfrak{c}
is prime-to-
p
, and for any finite order character
\xi:G_{K}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{\times}
of conductor dividing
\mathfrak{c}
put

\bm{\theta}_{\xi}(S)(q)=\sum_{(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{p}\mathfrak{c})=1}\xi(\sigma_{\mathfrak{a}})\Psi^{-1}_{\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S)-1}(\sigma_{\mathfrak{a}})q^{N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{a})}\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S}\rrbracket\llbracket{q}\rrbracket,

where
\sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}\in{\rm Gal}(K[\mathfrak{c}\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}]/K)
is the Artin symbol of
\mathfrak{a}
.
Then
\bm{\theta}_{\xi}(S)
is a Hida family defined over
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S}\rrbracket
of tame level
N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{c})D_{K}
and tame character
(\xi\circ\mathscr{V})\epsilon_{K}\omega^{-1}
, where
\mathscr{V}:G_{\mathbf{Q}}^{\rm ab}\rightarrow G_{K}^{\rm ab}
is the transfer map and
\epsilon_{K}
is the quadratic character corresponding to
K/\mathbf{Q}
.

5.
Definite case

In this section we deduce our applications to the Bloch–Kato conjecture and the Iwasawa main conjecture for anticyclotomic twists of
f/K
in the case where
\epsilon(f/K)=+1
.

5.1.
Anticyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-functions

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Ga_{0}(pN_{f}))
be a
p
-ordinary
p
-stabilised newform of weight

k=2r\geq 2
, and tame level
N_{f}
defined over
\mathcal{O}
, and denote by
\alpha=\alpha_{p}(f)\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
the
U_{p}
-eigenvalue of
f
. Assume that
f
is
p
-old, and let
f^{\circ}\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(N_{f}))
be the newform associated with
f
. Write

N_{f}=N^{+}N^{-}

with
N^{+}
(resp.
N^{-}
) divisible only by primes which are split (resp. inert) in
K
, and fix an ideal
\mathfrak{N}^{+}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
with
\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{N}^{+}\simeq\mathbf{Z}/N^{+}\mathbf{Z}
.

Let
\Gamma^{-}
be the Galois group of the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension
K_{\infty}/K
. By definition, the map
\sigma\mapsto l(\sigma^{1-\mathbf{c}}|_{K_{\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}}})
factor through
\Gamma^{-}
, and we let
\gamma_{-}
be the topological generator of
\Gamma^{-}
mapping to
1
under the resulting isomorphism
\Gamma^{-}\simeq\mathbf{Z}_{p}
. As usual, we identity the anticyclotomic Iwasawa algebra

\Lambda_{K}^{-}:=\mathcal{O}\llbracket{\Gamma^{-}}\rrbracket

with the one-variable power series ring
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W}\rrbracket
via
\gamma_{-}\mapsto 1+W
. For any prime-to-
p
ideal
\mathfrak{a}
of
K
, let
\sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}
be the image of
\mathfrak{a}
in the Galois group of the ray class field
K[p^{\infty}]/K
under the Artin reciprocity map.

Theorem 5.1.1
.

Let
\chi
be a ring class character of conductor
c\mathcal{O}_{K}
with values in
\mathcal{O}
, and suppose:

(i)

(pN_{f},cD_{K})=1
,

(ii)

N^{-}
is the squarefree product of an odd number of primes.

Then there exists a unique element
\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\chi)(W)\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W}\rrbracket
such that for every character
\phi
of
\Gamma^{-}
of infinity type
(j,-j)
with
0\leq j<r
and conductor
p^{n}
, we have

\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\chi)(\phi(\gamma_{-})-1)^{2}=\frac{p^{(2r-1)n}}{\alpha_{p}(f)^{2n}}\cdot\Gamma(r)^{2}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{p}(f,\chi\phi)^{2}\cdot\frac{L(f^{\circ},\chi\phi,r)}{(2\pi)^{2r}\cdot\Omega_{f^{\circ},N^{-}}}\cdot u_{K}^{2}\sqrt{D_{K}}\chi\phi(\sigma_{\mathfrak{N}^{+}})\cdot\varepsilon_{p},

where:

•

\mathcal{E}_{p}(f,\chi\phi)=\begin{cases}(1-\alpha_{p}(f)^{-1}p^{r-1}\chi\phi(\mathfrak{p}))(1-\alpha_{p}(f)p^{r-1}\chi\phi(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}))&\textrm{if $n=0$,}\\[1.99997pt]
1&\textrm{if $n>0$,}\end{cases}

•

\Omega_{f^{\circ},N^{-}}=2^{2r}\cdot\|f^{\circ}\|_{\Gamma_{0}(N_{f})}^{2}\cdot\eta_{f,N^{-}}^{-1}
is the
Gross period
of
f^{\circ}
(see
[
Hsi21
, p. 524]
),

•

u_{K}=|\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}|/2
, and

\varepsilon_{p}\in\{\pm 1\}
is the local root number of
f^{\circ}
at
p
.

Proof.

This is Theorem A in
[
CH18b
]
(see also
[
Hun17
, Thm. A]
), extending and refining a construction in
[
BD96
]
in weight
2
.
∎

5.2.
Factorisation of triple product
p
-adic
L
-functions

Let
f\in S_{2r}(pN_{f})
be a
p
-stabilised newform as in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:Lp-def}
, and suppose the residual representation
\bar{\rho}_{f}
satisfies:

(5.1)

\textrm{$\bar{\rho}_{f}$ is absolutely irreducible and $p$-distinguished}.

By Hida theory,
f
is the specialisation of a unique primitive Hida family
{\bm{f}}\in S^{o}(N_{f},\mathbb{I})

at an arithmetic point
Q_{0}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{+}
of weight
2r
.
Let
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
be ideals coprime to
pN_{f}
, and let
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}
be ray class characters of
K
of conductors dividing
\mathfrak{f}_{1},\mathfrak{f}_{2}
, respectively. Let
\chi_{\xi_{i}}
be the central character of
\xi_{i}
(
i=1,2
). We assume that

(5.2)

\chi_{\xi_{1}}\chi_{\xi_{2}}=1,

and let

(5.3)

{\bm{g}}_{1}=\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{1}}(S_{1})\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket{q}\rrbracket,\quad{\bm{g}}_{2}=\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{2}}(S_{2})\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{2}}\rrbracket\llbracket{q}\rrbracket

be the CM Hida families attached to
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
, respectively.

The triple
({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}}_{1},{\bm{g}}_{2})
satisfies conditions (
4.1
) and (
4.4
) and the associated
{\bm{f}}
-unbalanced triple product
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{{\bm{f}}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}}_{1},{\bm{g}}_{2})
is an element in
\mathcal{R}=\mathbb{I}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{2}}\rrbracket\simeq\mathbb{I}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket
; in the following we let

(5.4)

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{\bm{f}}}(f,{\bm{g}}_{1},{\bm{g}}_{2})\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket

denote its image
under the natural map
\mathbb{I}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket
defined by
Q_{0}
. Denote by
\mathbf{c}
the non-trivial automorphism of
K/\mathbf{Q}
, and for a Hecke character
\psi
put
\psi^{\mathbf{c}}(\sigma):=\psi(\mathbf{c}\sigma\mathbf{c})
.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

Assume (
5.1
), (
5.2
), and that
N^{-}
is the squarefree product of an odd number of primes. Set

W_{1}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{1})^{1/2}(1+S_{2})^{1/2}-1,\quad W_{2}=(1+S_{1})^{1/2}(1+S_{2})^{-1/2}-1.

Then

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{\bm{f}}}(f,{\bm{g}}_{1},{\bm{g}}_{2})(S_{1},S_{2})=\pm\mathbf{w}\cdot\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2})(W_{1})\cdot\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}})(W_{2})\cdot\frac{\eta_{f^{\circ}}}{\eta_{f^{\circ},N^{-}}},

where
\mathbf{w}
is a unit in
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket
.

Proof.

This is an immediate extension of Proposition 8.1 in
[
Hsi21
]
, where the case
\xi_{2}=\xi_{1}^{-1}
is treated.
∎

5.3.
Selmer group decompositions

As in
\lx@sectionsign
5.2
, suppose
{\bm{f}}
is the Hida family passing through a
p
-ordinary
p
-stabilised newform
f\in S_{k}(pN_{f})
of weight
k=2r\geq 2
(so
{\bm{f}}_{Q_{0}}=f
), and

({\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})=({\bm{g}}_{1},{\bm{g}}_{2})=(\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{1}}(S_{1}),\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{2}}(S_{2}))

are CM Hida families as in (
5.3
).

Write
{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}
for the specialisation of
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}
at
Q_{0}
. Let
V_{f}^{\vee}
be the Galois representation associated to
f
, and recall that
\det(V_{f}^{\vee})=\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}^{2r-1}
in our conventions. Setting
T_{i}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{i})-1
(
i=1,2
), we have

\det(V_{{\bm{g}}_{T_{1}}}\otimes V_{{\bm{h}}_{T_{2}}})=\Psi_{T_{1}}\Psi_{T_{2}}\circ\mathscr{V}
, and so

(5.5)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}

\displaystyle\simeq T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes({\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{1}})\otimes({\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{2}})\otimes\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}^{1-r}(\Psi_{T_{1}}^{-1/2}\Psi_{T_{2}}^{-1/2}\circ\mathscr{V})

\displaystyle\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr),

where
T_{f}^{\vee}
is a
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
-stable
\mathcal{O}
-lattice inside
V_{f}^{\vee}
, and we put

W_{1}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{1})^{1/2}(1+S_{2})^{1/2}-1,\quad W_{2}=(1+S_{1})^{1/2}(1+S_{2})^{-1/2}-1

as in Proposition
5.2.1
.
In particular, we get

(5.6)

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})\simeq{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})

by Shapiro’s lemma.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

Under (
5.6
), the balanced Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
decomposes as

\displaystyle{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\oplus{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}),

and the
{\bm{f}}
-unbalanced Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
decomposes as

\displaystyle{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\oplus{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}).

Proof.

From (
5.5
) we see that the balanced local condition is given by

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{\rm bal}_{p}({\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\bigr).

Put
\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}=(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\oplus(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})
, so by (
5.5
) we have

(5.8)

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})\simeq{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K,\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}),

and from (
5.7
) we obtain

\begin{split}\mathscr{F}^{\rm bal}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})&\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\\
\mathscr{F}^{\rm bal}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}&\simeq\{0\}\end{split}\begin{split}&\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr),\\
&\oplus T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}},\end{split}

and this yields the claimed description of
{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
. On the other hand, we similarly find that the
{\bm{f}}
-balanced local condition is given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{{\bm{f}}}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr),
