\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{{\bm{f}}}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr),

from where the claimed description of
{\rm Sel}^{\bm{f}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
follows.
∎

As a consequence we also obtain the following decomposition for the Selmer groups with coefficients in
\mathbf{A}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}({\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}},\mu_{p^{\infty}})
, mirroring in the case of
{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})
the factorisation of
p
-adic
L
-functions in Proposition
5.2.1
.

Corollary 5.3.2
.

The balanced Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})
decomposes as

\displaystyle{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1})\oplus{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}),

where
A_{f}(r)={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r),\mu_{p^{\infty}})
;
and the
{\bm{f}}
-unbalanced Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}_{x_{1}}^{\dagger})
decomposes as

\displaystyle{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1})\oplus{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}).

Proof.

This is immediate from Proposition
5.3.1
and local Tate duality.
∎

5.4.
Explicit reciprocity law

With the same setting as in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:Selmer-decomposition-def}
, put

\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}={\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket}\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket/(S_{1}-S_{2})

(so in the quotient the weights of the CM Hida families
{\bm{g}}
and
{\bm{h}}
move in tandem), and denote by

(5.9)

\kappa(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbb{V}^{\dagger})

the resulting restriction of the three-variable big diagonal class
\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
in (
4.5
). (Here we are implicitly choosing level-
N
test vectors
(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})
,
where
N={\rm lcm}(N_{f},N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{f}_{1}\mathfrak{f}_{2})D_{K})
to project the classes from
\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}(N)
to
\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}
.) Similarly, we denote by
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{\bm{f}}}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})
the image of (
5.4
) in the quotient
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket/(S_{1}-S_{2})
.

Since
\kappa(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})\in{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})
as a consequence of
[
BSV22
, Cor. 8.2]
, we can write

(5.10)

\kappa(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})=(\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}),\kappa_{2}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}))

according to the decomposition from Proposition
5.3.1
; in particular, we have

(5.11)

\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}),

where
W_{1}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{1})-1
.

Similarly as in
\lx@sectionsign
4.1.1
, denote by
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W_{1}}\rrbracket}^{\rm cls}
the set of ring homomorphisms
Q\in{\rm Spec}(\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W_{1}}\rrbracket)(\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{p})
with
Q(W_{1})=\zeta_{Q}(1+p)^{k_{Q}-1}-1
for some
\zeta_{Q}\in\mu_{p^{\infty}}
and
k_{Q}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and for any
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W_{1}}\rrbracket
-module
M
denote by
M_{Q}
the corresponding specialisation. Write
T_{f}^{\vee,-}:=T_{f}^{\vee}/T_{f}^{\vee,+}
, where the
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
-action is given by the unramified character sending an arithmetic Frobenius to
\alpha_{p}(f)
. Then it is easy to see that for any
Q\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W_{1}}\rrbracket}^{\rm cls}
the Bloch–Kato dual exponential and logarithm maps give rise to
L(\zeta_{Q})
-isomorphisms

(5.12)

\displaystyle{\rm exp}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{*}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{f}^{\vee,-}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})_{Q}

\displaystyle\rightarrow L(\zeta_{Q}),\quad\textrm{if $1\leq k_{Q}\leq r$,}

\displaystyle{\rm log}_{\mathfrak{p}}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{f}^{\vee,-}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})_{Q}

\displaystyle\rightarrow L(\zeta_{Q}),\quad\textrm{if $k_{Q}>r$},

where
L
is the field of fractions of
\mathcal{O}
.

Denote by
p_{f}^{-}:T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\rightarrow T_{f}^{\vee,-}(1-r)
the natural projection.

Theorem 5.4.1
.

There is an injective
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W_{1}}\rrbracket
-module homomorphism with pseudo-null cokernel

\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\eta_{f}}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{f}^{\vee,-}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W_{1}}\rrbracket

such that for any
\mathfrak{Z}\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{f}^{\vee,-}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})
and
Q\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W_{1}}\rrbracket}^{\rm cls}
we have

\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\eta_{f}}(\mathfrak{Z})_{Q}=\begin{cases}c_{Q}\cdot{\rm exp}^{*}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathfrak{Z}_{Q})&\textrm{if $1\leq k_{Q}\leq r$,}\\[1.99997pt]
c_{Q}\cdot{\rm log}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathfrak{Z}_{Q})&\textrm{if $k_{Q}>r$,}\\
\end{cases}

where
c_{Q}
is an explicit nonzero constant. Moreover, we have
the
explicit reciprocity law

\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\eta_{f}}\bigl(p_{f}^{-}({\rm res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})))\bigr)(W_{1})=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{\bm{f}}}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})(S_{1}),

where
S_{1}=\mathbf{v}(1+W_{1})-1
.

Proof.

In terms of (
5.5
), we find that
\mathscr{F}_{p}^{3}({\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})=T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}
, which together with (
5.7
) gives the decomposition

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\rm bal}({\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})/\mathscr{F}_{p}^{3}({\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})

\displaystyle\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,-}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)_{W_{2}}\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\bigr)_{W_{2}},

with the terms in the direct sum corresponding to
\mathbb{V}_{f}^{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}
,
\mathbb{V}_{{\bm{h}}}^{f{\bm{g}}}
, and
\mathbb{V}_{{\bm{g}}}^{f{\bm{h}}}
in (
4.6
), respectively. Here the subscript
W_{2}
denotes the quotient by
W_{2}
, noting that
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket/(S_{1}-S_{2})\simeq\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W_{1},W_{2}}\rrbracket/(W_{2})
.

Thus we find that under the first isomorphism of Proposition
5.3.1
, the composite map in (
4.9
) corresponds to the projection onto
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})
composed with the natural map

\displaystyle{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\rm res}_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p_{f}^{-}}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{f}^{\vee,-}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}),

and so under the corresponding isomorphisms we have

{\rm res}_{p}(\kappa(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}))_{f}=p_{f}^{-}({\rm res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})))\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{f}^{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})\simeq{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\mathfrak{p}},T_{f}^{\vee,-}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}).

Finally, the construction of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\eta_{f}}
is deduced from a specialization of the three-variable
p
-adic regulator map
{\rm Log}^{\eta_{\bm{f}}}
in §
4.3
by the same argument as in
[
ACR21
, Prop. 7.3]
, and the associated explicit reciprocity law then follows from Theorem
4.3.1
.
∎

Remark 5.4.2
.

Without the need to assume condition (
5.1
) on
\bar{\rho}_{f}
, for any choice of level-
N
test vectors
(\breve{f},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})
the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
5.4.1
gives an equality

\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p},(\breve{f},\underline{\breve{{\bm{g}}}\breve{{\bm{h}}}})}\bigl(p_{f}^{-}({\rm res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})))\bigr)(W_{1})=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}(\breve{f},\underline{\breve{{\bm{g}}}\breve{{\bm{h}}}})(S_{1}),

where
\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p},(\breve{f},\underline{\breve{{\bm{g}}}\breve{{\bm{h}}}})}
and
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}}}(\breve{f},\underline{\breve{{\bm{g}}}\breve{{\bm{h}}}})
are specilisation of the map
{\rm Log}_{(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})}
and the
p
-adic
L
-function
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\bm{f}}(\breve{{\bm{f}}},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})
in Remark
4.3.2
and Remark
4.1.2
, respectively.

5.5.
On the Bloch–Kato conjecture in rank
0

In this section we deduce our first applications to the Bloch–Kato conjecture in analytic rank zero for the twisted
G_{K}
-representation

V_{f,\chi}:=V_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\chi^{-1}.

Denote by
K_{c}
the ring class field of
K
of conductor
c
. If
\chi
is a Hecke character of conductor
c\mathcal{O}_{K}
, then its
p
-adic avatar is a locally algebraic character of
{\rm Gal}(K_{cp^{\infty}}/K)
. The Galois group
\Gamma^{-}={\rm Gal}(K_{\infty}^{-}/K)
of the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
is the maximal
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-free quotient of
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(K_{cp^{\infty}}/K)
. Fix a (non-canonical) splitting

(5.13)

{\rm Gal}(K_{cp^{\infty}}/K)\simeq\Delta_{c}\times\Gamma^{-},

where
\Delta_{c}
is the torsion subgroup of
{\rm Gal}(K_{cp^{\infty}}/K)
. Note that every character of
\Delta_{c}
can be viewed as the
p
-adic avatar of a ring class character of
K
of conductor dividing
cp^{s}\mathcal{O}_{K}
for sufficiently large
s
. If
\chi
is as above, we then write
\chi=\chi_{t}\cdot\chi_{w}
according to the decomposition (
5.13
).

Theorem 5.5.1
.

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(pN_{f}))
be a
p
-ordinary
p
-stabilised newform of weigh
k=2r\geq 2
which is old at
p
, and let
\chi
be anticyclotomic Hecke character of conductor
c\mathcal{O}_{K}
and infinity type
(-j,j)
for some
j\geq 0
. Assume that:

•

N^{-}
is a square-free product of an odd number of primes;

•

\bar{\rho}_{f}
is absolutely irreducible;

•

(pN_{f},cD_{K})=1
;

•

p>k-2
;

•

p\nmid h_{K}
, the class number of
K
;

•

\chi_{t}
has conductor prime-to-
p
.

Then

L(f/K,\chi,r)\neq 0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})=0,

and hence the Bloch–Kato conjecture for
V_{f,\chi}
holds in analytic rank zero.

Proof.

We begin by noting that under our hypotheses the modular form
f
is not of CM-type (since
N^{-}>1
) and the sign in the functional equation of
L(f/K,\chi,s)
is
-1
for
j\geq r
, so without loss of generality below we assume that
0\leq j<r
.

Write
\chi_{t}=\alpha/\alpha^{\mathbf{c}}
with
\alpha
a ray class character of
K
of conductor
\mathfrak{f}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
prime-to-
p
(note that this is possible by e.g.
[
DR17
, Lem. 6.9]
or
[
Hid06b
, Lem. 5.31]

and our assumption on
\chi_{t}
). Now, we fix a prime
\ell\neq p
split in
K
, and for an auxiliary ring class character
\beta
(to be further specified below) of
\ell
-power conductor we consider the setting of
\lx@sectionsign
5.2
with the CM Hida families

{\bm{g}}=\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{1}}(S_{1}),\quad{\bm{h}}=\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{2}}(S_{2}),

where

\xi_{1}:=\beta\alpha,\quad\xi_{2}:=\beta^{-1}\alpha^{-\mathbf{c}}.

Then the decomposition (
5.5
) of the associated
{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}
specialised to
S_{1}=S_{2}
yields

(5.14)

\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\chi_{t}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\beta^{-2},

where
W_{1}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{1})-1
. Denoting by
Q
the specialization
W\mapsto\zeta(1+p)^{j}-1
(
\zeta\in\mu_{p^{\infty}}
) corresponding to
\chi_{w}
, it follows that

L(\mathbb{V}_{Q}^{\dagger},0)=L(f/K,\chi,r)\cdot L(f/K,\beta^{2},r).

By
[
CH18b
, Thm. D]
, the character
\beta
may be chose so that
\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\beta^{2})(0)
is nonzero, and for such choice of
\beta

by Theorem
5.4.1
(see also Remark
5.4.2
) we then have

L(f/K,\chi,r)\neq 0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\rm res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})_{Q})\neq 0.

By construction, the class
\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})_{Q}\in{\rm Sel}_{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}(K,V_{f,\chi})
is the base class of the anticyclotomic Euler system

\{z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K[m],T_{f,\chi})\,\colon\,\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{N}\}

of (
3.3
), with
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
the Hecke characters associated with
({\bm{g}}_{S_{1}},{\bm{h}}_{S_{2}})
. Hence by Theorem
3.3.1
we deduce that the Selmer group
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,V_{f,\chi})
is one-dimensional,
spanned by

z_{f,\chi}={\rm cor}_{K[1]/K}(z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},(1)})=\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})_{Q}.

Since also
{\rm res}_{\mathfrak{p}}(z_{f,\chi})\neq 0
, the vanishing of
{\rm Sel}_{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}(K,V_{f,\chi})
then follows by a standard argument using Poitou–Tate duality (see e.g.
[
CH18a
, Thm. 7.9]
). Since by Lemma
3.1.2
for
0\leq j<r
the latter group is the same as
{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})
, this yields the result.
∎

5.6.
On the Iwasawa main conjecture

Our next application is a divisibility in the anticyclotomic Iwasawa main conjecture for modular forms in the definite setting. For an eigenform
f
of weight
k=2r\geq 2
and trivial nebentypus and
\chi
an anticyclotomic character, put

A_{f,\chi}={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\chi^{-1},\mu_{p^{\infty}}).

Theorem 5.6.1
.

Let the hypotheses be as in Theorem
5.5.1
, and assume in addition that:

•

\bar{\rho}_{f}
is
p
-distinguished;

•

f
has big image (see
3.3.2
).

Then
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},A_{f,\chi})
is cotorsion over
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
, and we have the divisibility

{\rm char}_{\Lambda_{K}^{-}}\bigl({\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},A_{f,\chi})^{\vee}\bigr)\supset\bigl(\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\chi)\bigr)

in
\Lambda_{K}^{-}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathbf{Q}_{p}
.

Proof.

Proceeding as in the proof of Theorem
5.5.1
we obtain the decomposition (
5.14
), which by Proposition
5.2.1
translates into the factorisation

(5.15)

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{f}},\eta_{f}}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})(S_{1})=\pm\mathbf{w}\cdot\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\chi_{t})(W_{1})\cdot\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\beta^{2})(0)\cdot\frac{\eta_{f^{\circ}}}{\eta_{f^{\circ},N^{-}}},

where
W_{1}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{1})=1
, and
from Proposition
5.3.1
we have

(5.16)

{\rm Sel}^{\bm{f}}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbb{A}^{\dagger})\simeq{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},A_{f}(r)\otimes\chi)\oplus{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\beta^{2}),

where
\mathbb{A}^{\dagger}={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(\mathbb{V}^{\dagger},\mu_{p^{\infty}})
.

By Theorem
5.1.1
and
[
CH18b
, Thm. D]
, the auxiliary ring class character
\beta
may be chosen so that
\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\beta^{2})(0)\neq 0
. Moreover, by Vatsal’s nonvanishing results
[
Vat03
]
and their extension to higher weights by Chida–Hsieh
[
CH18b
]
, the
p
-adic
L
-function
\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\chi_{t})(W_{1})
is nonzero. Hence from (
5.15
) and Theorem
5.4.1
it follows that the class

\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})

is non-torsion over
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W_{1}}\rrbracket
. Since by construction
\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}})
is the base class of the
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
-adic anticyclotomic Euler system

\bigl\{\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f,\chi})\,\colon\;\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{N}\bigr\}

in (
3.4
), with
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
the Hecke characters corresponding to
({\bm{g}}_{S_{1}},{\bm{h}}_{S_{2}})
, the result follows immediately from Theorem
3.3.3
applied to

(5.17)

\mathbf{z}_{f,\chi}:={\rm cor}_{K[1]/K}(\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},(1)})=\kappa_{1}(f,\underline{{\bm{g}}{\bm{h}}}),

the equivalence in Proposition
4.3.3
, and the Selmer group decomposition (
5.16
), using that by the rank
0
cases of the Bloch–Kato conjecture established in Theorem
5.5.1
, the nonvanishing of
L(f/K,\beta^{2},r)
implies that
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\beta^{2})
is finite.
∎

Remark 5.6.2
.

A divisibility in the anticyclotomic Iwsawa Main Conjecture for
V_{f,\chi}
was first obtained by Bertolini–Darmon
[
BD05
]
in weight
k=2
and by Chida–Hsieh
[
CH15
]
in higher weights using Heegner points and
level-raising congruences. Our proof of Theorem
5.6.1
is completely different from theirs, and in particular it dispenses with any “level-raising” hypothesis.

5.7.
On the Bloch–Kato conjecture in rank
1

The arguments in the proof of Theorem
5.6.1
give the following result towards the Bloch–Kato conjecture in rank
1
.

Theorem 5.7.1
.

Let the hypotheses be as in Theorem
5.5.1
, and assume in addition that:

•

\bar{\rho}_{f}
is
p
-distinguished;

•

f
has big image.

If
j\geq r
(which implies
L(f/K,\chi,r)=0
), then

{\rm dim}_{L_{\mathfrak{P}}}\,{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})\geq 1.

Moreover, there exists a class
z_{f,\chi}\in{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})
such that

z_{f,\chi}\neq 0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\rm dim}_{L_{\mathfrak{P}}}\,{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})=1.

Proof.

The proof of Theorem
5.6.1
shows the non-torsionness of the class
\mathbf{z}_{f,\chi}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\chi})
, which is the base of a
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
-adic anticyclotomic Euler systems as in (
5.17
) for the relaxed-strict Selmer group.
By Theorem
3.3.3
, it follows that
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\chi})
has
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
-rank
1
. Since by (a straighforward variation of) Mazur’s control theorem the natural map

(5.18)

{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\chi})/(\gamma_{-}-1){\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K_{\infty}^{-},T_{f,\chi})\rightarrow{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f,\chi})

is injective with finite cokernel, we conclude that
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f,\chi})
has positive
\mathcal{O}
-rank, and by Lemma
3.1.2
the first part of the theorem follows.

On the other hand, letting
z_{f,\chi}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f,\chi})
be the image of
\mathbf{z}_{f,\chi}
under the projection (
5.18
), the last claim in the result follows from Theorem
3.3.1
.
∎

Remark 5.7.2
.

From the Euler system of Beilinson–Flach elements constructed by Lei–Loeffler–Zerbes and Kings–Loeffler–Zerbes
[
LLZ14
,
KLZ17
]
attached to the Rankin–Selberg convolution of
f
and a suitable
\theta
-series, one can produce a class
{\rm BF}_{f,\chi}\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K,V_{f,\chi})
. As shown in
[
LLZ15
]
and
[
BL18
]
, this class forms the basis of an anticyclotomic Euler system for
V_{f,\chi}
, but not for the correct local conditions at
p
. Indeed, with notations as in the proof of Theorem
5.5.1
, it follows from the explicit reciprocity law of
[
KLZ17
]
that, for
j\geq r
, the class
{\rm BK}_{f,\chi}
lands in
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,V_{f,\chi})={\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})
precisely when the
p
-adic
L
-value
\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\chi_{t})(\zeta(1+p)^{j}-1)
vanishes (see
[
Cas17
, Thm. 2.4]
,
[
BL18
, Thm. 3.11]
). However, since
j\geq r
is
outside
the range of interpolation of
\Theta_{p}^{\rm BD}(f/K,\chi_{t})
, such vanishing is not a consequence of the forced vanishing of
L(f/K,\chi,r)
, and Theorem
5.7.1
seems to fall outside the scope of these classes.
(On the other hand, it also seems to fall outside the scope of Heegner cycles, since the squarefree integer
N^{-}
is assumed to have an odd number of prime factors, so Heegner cycles are not directly available, and the level-raising techniques of Bertolini–Darmon
[
BD05
]
are only known to yield results towards the Bloch–Kato conjecture in rank
0
, see e.g.
[
LV10
]
.)

6.
Indefinite case

In this section we deduce our applications to the Bloch–Kato conjecture and the Iwasawa main conjecture for anticyclotomic twists of
f/K
when
\epsilon(f/K)=-1
.

Since the nonvanishing results we shall need
from
[
Hsi14
]
are currently only available in the literature under the classical Heegner hypothesis, in the following we shall restrict to this case, but note that with the required extension of
[
Hsi14
]
at hand (see
[
Bur17
,
Mag
]
for progress in this direction), our results directly extend to the general indefinite case.

6.1.
Anticyclotomic
p
-adic
L
-functions

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Ga_{0}(pN_{f}))
be a
p
-ordinary
p
-stabilised newform as in
\lx@sectionsign
5.1
(in particular,
f
is old at
p
) where
k=2r\geq 2
, and let
K
be an imaginary quadratic field of discriminant
-D_{K}<0
in which
p=\mathfrak{p}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}
splits. Assume that
K
satisfies the classical
Heegner hypothesis
:

(6.1)

every prime
\ell|N_{f}
splits in
K
,

and fix an ideal
\mathfrak{N}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
with
\mathcal{O}_{K}/\mathfrak{N}\simeq\mathbf{Z}/N_{f}\mathbf{Z}
.

Recall that
\Gamma^{-}
denotes the Galois group of the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
, and
\Lambda_{K}^{-}=\mathcal{O}\llbracket{\Gamma^{-}}\rrbracket
is the associated Iwasawa algebra. Let
\Omega_{p}
and
\Omega_{K}
be CM periods attached to
K
as in
[
CH18a
, §2.5]
, and put

\Lambda_{K}^{-,\rm ur}=\Lambda_{K}^{-}\hat{\otimes}\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\rm ur},

where
\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\rm ur}
is the completion of the ring of integers of the maximal unramified extension of
\mathbf{Q}_{p}
.
Similarly as before, we shall often identify
\Lambda_{K}^{-,\rm ur}
with the one-variable power series ring
\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\rm ur}\llbracket{W}\rrbracket
via
\gamma_{-}\mapsto 1+W
for a fixed topological generator
\gamma_{-}\in\Gamma^{-}
.

Theorem 6.1.1
.

Let
\chi
be an
\mathcal{O}
-valued ring class character of conductor
c\mathcal{O}_{K}
with
(pN_{f},cD_{K})=1
. Then there exists a unique element

\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\chi)\in\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\rm ur}\llbracket{W}\rrbracket
such that every character
\phi
of
\Gamma^{-}
of infinity type
(j,-j)
with
j\geq r
and conductor
p^{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\chi)(\phi(\gamma_{-})-1)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Omega_{p}^{4j}}{\Omega_{K}^{4j}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma(r+j)\Gamma(j+1-r)\phi(\mathfrak{N}^{-1})}{4(2\pi)^{2j+1}\sqrt{D_{K}}^{2j-1}}\cdot e_{\mathfrak{p}}(f,\chi\phi)\cdot L(f/K,\chi\phi,r),

where

e_{\mathfrak{p}}(f,\chi\phi)=\begin{cases}\bigl(1-a_{p}\chi\phi(\bar{\mathfrak{p}})p^{-r}+\chi\phi(\bar{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}p^{-1}\bigr)^{2}&\textrm{if $n=0$,}\\[3.00003pt]
\varepsilon(\frac{1}{2},\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}\phi_{\mathfrak{p}})^{-2}&\textrm{else},\end{cases}

with
\varepsilon(\frac{1}{2},\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}\phi_{\mathfrak{p}})
the local
\varepsilon
-factor in
[
CH18a
, p. 570]
attached to the component at
\mathfrak{p}
of
\chi\phi
.
Moreover,
\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\chi)
is a nonzero element of
\Lambda_{K}^{-,{\rm ur}}
.

Proof.

This is a reformulation of results contained in
[
CH18a
, §3]
. In particular, since
(N_{f},D_{K})=1
by hypothesis (
6.1
), the nonvanishing of
\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\chi)
follows from
[
CH18a
, Thm. 3.9]
.
∎

Remark 6.1.2
.

The CM period
\Omega_{K}\in\mathbf{C}^{\times}
in Theorem
6.1.1
agrees with that in
[
BDP13
, (5.1.16)]
, but is
different
from the period
\Omega_{\infty}
defined in
[
dS87
, p. 66]
and
[
HT93
, (4.4b)]
. In fact, one has

\Omega_{\infty}=2\pi i\cdot\Omega_{K}.

In terms of
\Omega_{\infty}
, the interpolation formula in Theorem
6.1.1
reads

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\chi)(\phi(\gamma_{-})-1)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Omega_{p}^{4j}}{\Omega_{\infty}^{4j}}\cdot\frac{\Gamma(r+j)\Gamma(j+1-r)\phi(\mathfrak{N}^{-1})}{4(2\pi)^{1-2j}\sqrt{D_{K}}^{2j-1}}\cdot e_{\mathfrak{p}}(f,\chi\phi)\cdot L(f/K,\chi\phi,r).

This is the form of the interpolation that we shall use later.

6.2.
Factorisation of triple product
p
-adic
L
-function

As in
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:factor-L-def}
, we now let
{\bm{f}}\in S^{o}(N_{f},\mathbb{I})
be the primitive Hida family associated to
f
; so
{\bm{f}}
specialises to
f
at an arithmetic point
Q_{0}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{+}
of weight
2r
, and consider a pair of CM Hida families

(6.2)

({\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})=({\bm{g}}_{1},{\bm{g}}_{2})=(\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{1}}(S_{1}),\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{2}}(S_{2}))\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1}}\rrbracket\llbracket{q}\rrbracket\times\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{2}}\rrbracket\llbracket{q}\rrbracket

similar to (
5.3
).

The triple product
p
-adic
L
-function of relevance in this section is the
{\bm{g}}
-unbalanced
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}},\eta_{\bm{g}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
,
which is an element in
\mathcal{R}=\mathbb{I}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1}}\rrbracket\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{2}}\rrbracket\simeq\mathbb{I}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket
. In the following we let

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}},\eta_{\bm{g}}}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket

be the image of
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}},\eta_{\bm{g}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
under the map
\mathbb{I}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket
given by

Q_{0}:\mathbb{I}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
.

6.2.1.
Anticyclotomic Katz
p
-adic
L
-function

Before we can state and prove the main result of this section, we need to recall the interpolation property of the Katz
p
-adic
L
-functions
[
Kat78
]
, following the exposition in
[
dS87
]
.

Fix an ideal
\mathfrak{c}\subset\mathcal{O}_{K}
prime-to-
p
stable under the action of complex conjugation,
and denote by
Z(\mathfrak{c})
the ray class group of
K
of conductor
\mathfrak{c}p^{\infty}
(so
Z(\mathfrak{c})\simeq{\rm Gal}(K[\mathfrak{c}p^{\infty}]/K)
).

Theorem 6.2.1
.

There exists an element
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{c}}^{\rm Katz}\in\mathcal{O}\llbracket{Z(\mathfrak{c})}\rrbracket\hat{\otimes}\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\rm ur}

such that for every character
\xi
of
Z(\mathfrak{c})
of infinity type
(k,j)
with
k>-j\geq 0
satisfies

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{c}}^{\rm Katz}(\xi)=\frac{\Omega_{p}^{k-j}}{\Omega_{\infty}^{k-j}}\cdot\Gamma(k)\cdot\biggl(\frac{\sqrt{D_{K}}}{2\pi}\biggr)^{j}\cdot(1-\xi^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})p^{-1})(1-\xi(\bar{\mathfrak{p}}))\cdot L(\xi,0).

Moreover, we have the functional equation

\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{c}}^{\rm Katz}(\xi)=\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{c}}^{\rm Katz}(\xi^{-\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{N}^{-1}),

where the equality is up to a
p
-adic unit.

Proof.

Our
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{c}}^{\rm Katz}
corresponds to the measure denoted
\mu(\mathfrak{c}\bar{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})
in
[
dS87
, Thm. II.4.14]
. The stated functional equation is given in
[
dS87
, Thm. II.6.4]
.
∎

Let
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{c}}
be the maximal torsion-free subgroup of
Z(\mathfrak{c})
, and fix a (non-canonical) splitting

Z(\mathfrak{c})\simeq\Delta_{\mathfrak{c}}\times\Gamma_{\mathfrak{c}}

with
\Delta_{\mathfrak{c}}
a finite group and
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{c}}\simeq\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{2}
. For
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{c}
the natural projection
Z(\mathfrak{c})\twoheadrightarrow Z(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
takes
\Delta_{\mathfrak{c}}
to
\Delta_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}
, inducing an isomorphism
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{c}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}}
. Thus in the following we shall identify
\Gamma_{\mathfrak{c}}
with
\Gamma_{K}:=\Gamma_{(1)}
.
Since
p>2
, the action of complex conjugation
\mathbf{c}
splits

\Gamma_{K}\simeq\Gamma^{+}\times\Gamma^{-}

with
\Gamma^{\pm}\simeq\mathbf{Z}_{p}
, and where
\mathbf{c}
sends
\gamma\in\Gamma^{\pm}
to
\gamma^{\pm{1}}
. Then of course
\Gamma^{-}
is identified with the Galois group
{\rm Gal}(K_{\infty}^{-}/K)
of the anticyclotomic
\mathbf{Z}_{p}
-extension of
K
.

Suppose
\eta
is a Hecke character of
K
of conductor dividing
\mathfrak{c}p^{\infty}
. Viewing
\eta
as a character on
Z(\mathfrak{c})\simeq\Delta_{\mathfrak{c}}\times\Gamma_{K}
, we put
\bar{\eta}:=\eta|_{\Delta_{\mathfrak{c}}}
, and denote by
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\bar{\eta}}^{{\rm Katz},-}
the image of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{c}}^{\rm Katz}
under the composite map

\mathcal{O}\llbracket{Z(\mathfrak{c})}\rrbracket\hat{\otimes}\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\rm ur}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\llbracket{\Gamma_{K}}\rrbracket\hat{\otimes}\mathbf{Z}_{p}^{\rm ur}\rightarrow\Lambda_{K}^{-,{\rm ur}},

where the first arrow is the natural projection defined by
\bar{\eta}
, and the second is given by
\gamma\mapsto\gamma^{\mathbf{c}-1}
for
\gamma\in\Gamma_{K}
. Put also
\bar{\eta}^{-}:=\bar{\eta}^{\mathbf{c}-1}
.

Lemma 6.2.2
.

Let
\xi
be a ray class character of
K
such that
\bar{\xi}^{-}
has conductor
\mathfrak{c}
prime-to-
p
. Assume that:

(i)

\mathfrak{c}
is only divisible by primes that are split in
K
;

(ii)

\Delta_{\mathfrak{c}}
has order prime-to-
p
;

(iii)

\bar{\xi}^{-}
has order at least
3
.

Then the congruence ideal of the CM Hida family
\bm{\theta}_{\xi}(S)
is generated by

\frac{h_{K}}{w_{K}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\bar{\xi}^{-}}^{{\rm Katz},-},

where
h_{K}=|{\rm Pic}(\mathcal{O}_{K})|
and
w_{K}=|\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times}|
.

Proof.

As explained in
[
ACR22
, Prop. 4.6]
, this is a consequence of the proof of the anticyclotomic Iwasawa main conjecture for Hecke characters by Hida–Tilouine
[
HT93
,
HT94
]
and Hida
[
Hid06a
]
.
∎

6.2.2.
The factorisation result

We shall work with the following integral normalisation of the triple product
p
-adic
L
-function of Theorem
4.1.1
.

Definition 6.2.3
.

Put

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}):=\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}},\eta_{{\bm{g}}}^{\star}}({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}),

where
\eta_{{\bm{g}}}^{\star}=\frac{h_{K}}{w_{K}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\bar{\xi}^{-}}^{{\rm Katz},-}
is the generator of
C({\bm{g}})
given by Lemma
6.2.2
.

Note that
\xi_{1}
can be replaced by a twist
\xi_{1}\cdot\phi\circ\mathbf{N}
for a Dirichlet character
\phi
without changing
\bar{\xi}_{1}^{-}
, and thus in the following we may assume that
\xi_{1}
satisfies the following minimality hypotheses:

(6.3)

the conductor of
\xi_{1}
is minimal among Dirichlet twists.

The following is an analogue of Proposition
5.2.1
in the indefinite setting. Note that a variant of this result first appeared in the work of Darmon–Lauder–Rotger (see
[
DLR15
, Thm. 3.9]
), but unfortunately the formulation of their result is not well-suited for our Iwasawa-theoretic purposes in this paper (see also the more recent
[
BCS23
, §8]
for a factorisation result closer to ours).

Proposition 6.2.4
.

Assume that
\xi_{1}
satisfies the conditions in Lemma
6.2.2
.
Put

W_{1}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{1})^{1/2}(1+S_{2})^{1/2}-1,\quad W_{2}=(1+S_{1})^{1/2}(1+S_{2})^{-1/2}-1.

Then

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}}}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})(S_{1},S_{2})

\displaystyle=\pm\mathbf{w}\cdot\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2})(W_{1})\cdot\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}})(W_{2}),

where
\mathbf{w}
is a unit.

Proof.

Let
k_{1},k_{2}
be integers with
k_{1}\equiv k_{2}\pmod{2}
and
k_{1}\geq k_{2}+2r
. Set
S_{i}=\mathbf{v}(1+p)^{k_{i}-1}-1
(
i=1,2
), so the corresponding specialisations of
W_{i}
are given by

W_{1}=(1+p)^{(k_{1}+k_{2}-2)/2}-1,\quad W_{2}=(1+p)^{(k_{1}-k_{2})/2},

and denote by
{\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}
the specialisation of
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}
at
\underline{Q}=(Q_{0},S_{1},S_{2})
.
Putting
T_{i}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{i})-1
for the ease of notation, we have

\det(T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes V_{{\bm{g}}_{T_{1}}}\otimes V_{{\bm{g}}_{T_{2}}})=\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}^{2r-1}\cdot(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{T_{1}}\Psi_{T_{2}}\circ\mathscr{V})=\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}^{2r-1}\cdot(\Psi_{T_{1}}\Psi_{T_{2}}\circ\mathscr{V}),

using that the central characters of
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
are inverses of each other for the second equality, and so

(6.4)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}

\displaystyle=T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes({\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{1}})\otimes({\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{2}})\otimes\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}^{1-r}(\Psi_{T_{1}}^{-1/2}\Psi_{T_{2}}^{-1/2}\circ\mathscr{V})

\displaystyle\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr).

Thus we find that the completed
L
-value appearing in the interpolation formula of Theorem
4.1.1
is given by

(6.5)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{{\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}}(0)\cdot L({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}},0)

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma\bigl(\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{2}+r-1\bigr)\Gamma\bigl(\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{2}-r+1\bigr)\Gamma\bigl(\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{2}-r\bigr)\Gamma\bigl(\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{2}+r\bigr)}{2^{4}\cdot(2\pi)^{2k_{1}}},

\displaystyle\times L(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1},r)\cdot L(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1},r),

and similarly the modified Euler factor decomposes as

(6.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p}(\mathscr{F}_{p}^{{\bm{g}}}({\mathbf{V}_{\underline{Q}}^{\dagger}}))

\displaystyle=\,\bigr(1-a_{p}(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1})(\bar{\mathfrak{p}})p^{-r}+(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1})(\bar{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}p^{-1}\bigl)^{2}

\displaystyle\times\bigr(1-a_{p}(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1})(\bar{\mathfrak{p}})p^{-r}+(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1})(\bar{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}p^{-1}\bigl)^{2}.

Moreover, letting
\chi_{g}^{\prime}
be the prime-to-
p
part of the nebentypus character of
{\bm{g}}_{T_{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigl(1-\frac{\chi_{g}^{\prime}(p)p^{k_{1}-1}}{\xi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{1}}(\bar{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}}\Bigr)\Bigl(1-\frac{\chi_{g}^{\prime}(p)p^{k_{1}-2}}{\xi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{1}}(\bar{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(1-\xi_{1}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\Psi_{T_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr)\bigl(1-\xi_{1}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\Psi^{1-\mathbf{c}}_{T_{1}}(\mathfrak{p})p^{-1}\bigr),

and therefore the canonical period
\Omega_{{\bm{g}}_{T_{1}}}
in Theorem
4.1.1
associated with
\eta_{{\bm{g}}}^{\star}
is given by

(6.7)

\Omega_{{\bm{g}}_{T_{1}}}=(-2\sqrt{-1})^{k_{1}+1}\cdot\frac{\|{\bm{g}}_{T_{1}}^{\circ}\|_{\Gamma_{0}(C)}^{2}}{\eta_{{\bm{g}}_{T_{1}}}^{\star}}\cdot\bigl(1-\xi_{1}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\Psi_{T_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr)\bigl(1-\xi_{1}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\Psi^{1-\mathbf{c}}_{T_{1}}(\mathfrak{p})p^{-1}\bigr),

where
C=N_{K/\mathbf{Q}}(\mathfrak{f}_{1})D_{K}
and we note that here
\Sigma_{\rm exc}
consists of the primes
q\mid C
inert in
K
.

On the other hand, since
g_{T_{1}}
has weight
k_{1}
, from Hida’s formula for the adjoint
L
-value
[
HT93
, Thm. 7.1]
(using that
\xi_{1}
satisfies the minimality condition (
6.3
)) and Dirichlet’s class number formula we obtain

\|g_{T_{1}}^{\circ}\|^{2}_{\Gamma_{0}(C)}=\Gamma(k_{1})\cdot\frac{D_{K}^{2}}{2^{2k_{1}}\pi^{k_{1}+1}}\cdot\frac{2\pi h_{K}}{w_{K}\sqrt{D_{K}}}\cdot L(\xi_{1}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{T_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1},1)\cdot\prod_{q\in\Sigma_{\rm exc}}(1+q^{-1}).

The character
\xi_{1}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{T_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\mathbf{N}^{-1}
has infinity type
(k_{1},2-k_{1})
, and so for
k_{1}\geq 2
it lies in the range of interpolation of
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}
. Noting that
L(\xi_{1}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{T_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\mathbf{N}^{-1},0)=L(\xi_{1}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{T_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1},1)
, from the above formula for this value and in Theorem
6.2.1
we thus obtain

(6.8)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\xi_{1}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{T_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\mathbf{N}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggr)^{2k_{1}-2}\cdot\frac{\pi^{2k_{1}-2}\cdot 2^{3k_{1}-3}}{\sqrt{D_{K}}^{k_{1}+1}}

\displaystyle\times\bigl(1-\xi^{\mathbf{c}-1}\Psi^{1-\mathbf{c}}_{T_{1}}(\mathfrak{p})\bigr)\bigl(1-\xi_{1}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\Psi^{1-\mathbf{c}}_{T_{1}}(\mathfrak{p})p^{-1}\bigr)\cdot\|{\bm{g}}^{\circ}_{T_{1}}\|_{\Gamma_{0}(C)}^{2}\cdot\frac{w_{K}}{h_{K}}.

Moreover, by the functional equation for Katz’s
p
-adic
L
-function
and the definition of
\eta_{{\bm{g}}}^{\star}
we have the relation

\frac{h_{K}}{w_{K}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{f}}^{\rm Katz}(\xi_{1}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{T_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\mathbf{N}^{-1})\sim_{p}\eta_{{\bm{g}}_{T_{1}}}^{\star},

where
\sim_{p}
denotes equality up to a
p
-adic unit, and so from (
6.7
) and (
6.8
) we arrive at

(6.9)

\frac{1}{\Omega_{{\bm{g}}_{T_{1}}}}\sim_{p}\biggl(\frac{\Omega_{p}}{\Omega_{\infty}}\biggr)^{2k_{1}-2}\cdot\frac{(2\pi)^{2k_{1}-2}}{\sqrt{-D_{K}}^{k_{1}+1}}.

Finally, note that the characters
\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}
and
\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}
in the right-hand side of (
6.5
) are both anticyclotomic, and of infinity type
((k_{1}+k_{2})/2-1,-(k_{1}+k_{2})/2+1)
and
((k_{1}-k_{2})/2,(k_{2}-k_{1})/2)
, respectively, and so for
k_{1}\geq k_{2}+2r
they are in the range of interpolation for
\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2})
and
\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}})
, respectively. Thus substituting (
6.5
), (
6.6
), and (
6.9
) into the interpolation formula for
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{\bm{g}}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
in Theorem
4.1.1
and comparing with Theorem
6.1.1
we finally arrive at

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}}}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})(S_{1},S_{2})^{2}\sim_{p}\frac{-1}{D_{K}^{k_{1}+1}}\cdot\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2})(W_{1})^{2}\cdot\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}})(W_{2})^{2},

and this yiels the proof of the result.
∎

6.3.
Selmer group decomposition

We keep the setting in
\lx@sectionsign
6.2
, so in particular
f\in S_{2r}(pN_{f})
is the specialisation of
{\bm{f}}
at
Q_{0}\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathbb{I}}^{+}
, and write
{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}
for the corresponding specialisation of
{\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}}
.

As in the proof of Proposition
6.2.4
, setting
T_{i}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{i})-1
(
i=1,2
) we have

(6.10)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}

\displaystyle=T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes({\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{1}})\otimes({\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{2}})\otimes\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}^{1-r}(\Psi_{T_{1}}^{-1/2}\Psi_{T_{2}}^{-1/2}\circ\mathscr{V})

\displaystyle\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes{\rm Ind}_{K}^{\mathbf{Q}}\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr),

where
T_{f}^{\vee}
is a
G_{\mathbf{Q}}
-stable
\mathcal{O}
-lattice inside
V_{f}^{\vee}
, and we put

W_{1}=\mathbf{v}^{-1}(1+S_{1})^{1/2}(1+S_{2})^{1/2}-1,\quad W_{2}=(1+S_{1})^{1/2}(1+S_{2})^{-1/2}-1.

In particular, we get

(6.11)

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})\simeq{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})

by Shapiro’s lemma.

Proposition 6.3.1
.

Under (
6.11
), the balanced Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
decomposes as

\displaystyle{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\oplus{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}),

and the
{\bm{g}}
-unbalanced Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{g}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
decomposes as

\displaystyle{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{g}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\oplus{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}).

Proof.

The proof of the decomposition for
{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
is the same as in Proposition
5.3.1
,
so we focus on
{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{g}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
. Put

\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}=\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr),

so by Shapiro’s lemma we have

{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})\simeq{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K,\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}).

A direct computation shows that the
{\bm{g}}
-unbalanced local condition is given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\bm{g}}({\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})

\displaystyle=T_{f}^{\vee}\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{1}}\otimes\bigl(\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{T_{2}}\oplus\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{T_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\otimes\varepsilon_{\rm cyc}^{1-r}(\Psi_{T_{1}}^{-1/2}\Psi_{T_{2}}^{-1/2}\circ\mathscr{V})

\displaystyle=\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr).

Therefore, we have

\mathscr{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})=\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger},\quad\mathscr{F}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})=0,

and this yields the stated decomposition for
{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{g}}}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}})
.
∎

Corollary 6.3.2
.

The balanced Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})
decomposes as

\displaystyle{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1})\oplus{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}),

where
A_{f}(r)={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r),\mu_{p^{\infty}})
; and the
{\bm{g}}
-unbalanced Selmer group
{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{g}}}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})
decomposes as

\displaystyle{\rm Sel}^{{\bm{g}}}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{A}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\Psi_{W_{1}}^{\mathbf{c}-1})\oplus{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K,A_{f}(r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W_{2}}^{\mathbf{c}-1}).

Proof.

As in Corollary
5.3.2
, this is immediate from Proposition
6.3.1
and local Tate duality.
∎

6.4.
Explicit reciprocity law

In the setting of
\lx@sectionsign\ref{subsec:Selmer-decomposition-indef}
, we now put

\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}={\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket}\mathcal{O}\llbracket{S_{1},S_{2}}\rrbracket/(\mathbf{v}^{2}(1+S_{2})^{-1}-1),

and let

(6.12)

\kappa(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1})\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q},\mathbb{V}^{\dagger})

be the resulting restriction of the three-variable big diagonal class
\kappa({\bm{f}},{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
in (
4.5
), where
{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1}
is the weight one theta series obtained by specialising
{\bm{h}}=\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{2}}(S_{2})
at
S_{2}=\mathbf{v}^{2}-1
.

Since
\kappa(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1})\in{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})
, we can write

(6.13)

\kappa(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1})=(\kappa_{1}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1}),\kappa_{2}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1}))

according to the decomposition from Proposition
6.3.1
; in particular, we have

\kappa_{2}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1})\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}}),

where
W=(1+S_{1})^{1/2}-1
. Similarly as in
\lx@sectionsign
5.4
, for any
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W}\rrbracket
-module
M
we denote by
M_{Q}
its specialisation at
Q\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W}\rrbracket}^{\rm cls}
. Then, if
Q
has weight
k_{Q}\geq 1
, it is easy to see that the Bloch–Kato dual exponential and logarithm maps give rise to
L(\zeta_{Q})
-isomorphisms

(6.14)

\displaystyle{\rm exp}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{*}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}},T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}})_{Q}

\displaystyle\rightarrow L(\zeta_{Q}),

\displaystyle{\rm loc}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}},T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}})_{Q}

\displaystyle\rightarrow L(\zeta_{Q}),

according to whether
k_{Q}>2r
or
1\leq k_{Q}\leq 2r
, respectively.

Theorem 6.4.1
.

Let
\eta_{\bm{g}}
be a generator of the congruence ideal of
{\bm{g}}
. There is an injective
\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W}\rrbracket
-module homomorphism with pseudo-null cokernel

\mathscr{L}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\eta_{\bm{g}}}:{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}},T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W}\rrbracket

such that for any
\mathfrak{Z}\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}},T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}})
and
Q\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{O}\llbracket{W}\rrbracket}^{\rm cls}
we have

\mathscr{L}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\eta_{\bm{g}}}(\mathfrak{Z})_{Q}=\begin{cases}c_{Q}\cdot{\rm exp}^{*}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathfrak{Z}_{Q})&\textrm{if $k_{Q}>2r$,}\\[1.99997pt]
c_{Q}\cdot{\rm log}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(\mathfrak{Z}_{Q})&\textrm{if $1\leq k_{Q}\leq 2r$,}\end{cases}

where
c_{Q}
is an explicit nonzero constant. Moreover, we have the
explicit reciprocity law

\mathscr{L}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\eta_{{\bm{g}}}^{\star}}\bigl({\rm res}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(\kappa_{2}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1}))\bigr)=\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{{\bm{g}}}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})((1+W)^{2}-1,\mathbf{v}^{2}-1).

Proof.

In terms of the direct sum decomposition in (
6.10
) we have

\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\rm bal}({\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\big)\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes(\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}}+\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{\mathbf{c}-1})\bigr)

as
G_{\mathbf{Q}_{p}}
-representations, while a direct computation shows that
\mathscr{F}_{p}^{3}({\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})=T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}}
. From these we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{p}^{\rm bal}({\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})/\mathscr{F}_{p}^{3}({\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})\simeq\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,-}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\oplus\bigl(T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{\mathbf{c}-1}\bigr),

with the terms in the direct sum corresponding to
\mathbb{V}_{f}^{{\bm{g}}h}
,
\mathbb{V}_{h}^{f{\bm{g}}}
, and
\mathbb{V}_{{\bm{g}}}^{fh}
in (
4.6
), respectively, where
h={\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1}
. Thus we find that the composite map
in (
4.9
)

{\rm Sel}^{\rm bal}(\mathbf{Q},{\mathbb{V}^{\dagger}})\rightarrow{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},\mathbb{V}_{{\bm{g}}}^{fh})

corresponds under the isomorphism of Proposition
6.3.1
to the projection onto
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}})
composed with the restriction map

{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}})\xrightarrow{{\rm res}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}}{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}},T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}}).

In particular, under the corresponding identifications it follows that

\displaystyle{\rm res}_{p}(\kappa(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1}))_{\bm{g}}

\displaystyle={\rm res}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(\kappa_{2}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1}))

\displaystyle\in{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(\mathbf{Q}_{p},V_{\bm{g}}^{fh})\simeq{\mathrm{H}}^{1}(K_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}},T_{f}^{\vee,+}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}}).

Finally, the construction of
\mathscr{L}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\eta_{\bm{g}}}
is deduced from a specialisation of the three-variable
p
-adic regulator map
{\rm Log}^{\eta_{\bm{g}}}
in §
4.3
(see
[
ACR21
, Prop. 7.3]
), and the associated explicit reciprocity law then follows from Theorem
4.3.1
.
∎

6.5.
On the Bloch–Kato conjecture in rank
0

As another application of the Euler system construction in this paper, we can now deduce a result towards the Bloch–Kato conjecture for

V_{f,\chi}=V_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\chi^{-1}

analogous to Theorem
5.5.1
but in the indefinite setting. Note that a similar result was obtained in
[
CH18a
, Thm. B]
using the generalised Heegner cycles of Bertolini–Darmon–Prasanna.

Theorem 6.5.1
.

Let
f\in S_{k}(\Gamma_{0}(pN_{f}))
be a
p
-ordinary
p
-stabilised newform of weigh
k=2r\geq 2
which is old at
p
, and let
\chi
be anticyclotomic Hecke character of conductor
c\mathcal{O}_{K}
and infinity type
(-j,j)
for some
j\geq 0
. Assume that:

•

every prime
\ell\mid N_{f}
splits in
K
;

•

(pN_{f},cD_{K})=1
;

•

p\nmid h_{K}
, the class number of
K
;

•

\bar{\rho}_{f}
is absolutely irreducible and
p
-distinguished;

•

\chi_{t}
has conductor prime-to-
p
.

Assume in addition that
f
is not of CM type. Then

L(f/K,\chi,r)\neq 0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})=0,

and hence the Bloch–Kato conjecture for
V_{f,\chi}
holds in analytic rank zero.

Proof.

This follows by an argument similar to the proof of Theorem
5.5.1
after some modifications. Write
\chi_{t}=\alpha/\alpha^{\mathbf{c}}
for a ray class character
\alpha
as in the proof of that result, but now put

(6.15)

\xi_{1}:=\beta\alpha,\quad\xi_{2}:=\beta\alpha^{-1},

with
\beta
an auxiliary ring class character of
K
(to be further specified below) of
\ell
-power conductor for a prime
\ell\neq p
split in
K
. Consider the setting of
\lx@sectionsign
6.2
with the CM Hida families

{\bm{g}}=\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{1}}(S_{1}),\quad{\bm{h}}=\bm{\theta}_{\xi_{2}}(S_{2}).

If
(-j,j)
denotes the infinity type of
\chi
, then
\chi
is the specialisation of
\xi_{1}\xi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}\Psi_{W}^{\mathbf{c}-1}
at
W=\zeta(1+p)^{j}
for some
\zeta\in\mu_{p^{\infty}}
, and letting
\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0},\chi}^{\dagger}
be the corresponding specialisation of
{\mathbf{V}_{Q_{0}}^{\dagger}}
at
(S_{1},S_{2})=(\zeta^{2}(1+p)^{2j}-1,\mathbf{v}^{2}-1)
from the decomposition (
6.10
) we obtain the factorisation

(6.16)

L(\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}_{Q_{0},\chi},0)=L(f/K,\beta^{2}\chi,r)\cdot L(f/K,\chi,r).

By our assumption (
6.1
) on
N_{f}
, the sign in the functional equation of
L(f/K,\chi,s)
is
-1
for
0\leq j<r
, so without loss of generality we assume that
j\geq r
. Then the above
(Q_{0},S_{1},S_{2})
is in the range of interpolation of
\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}}}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})
, and by
[
Hsi14
, Thm. C]
we may choose
\beta
as above so that
L(f/K,\beta^{2}\chi,r)\neq 0
.
Thus from (
6.16
) and Theorem
4.1.1
(see also Remark
4.1.2
), with such choice of
\beta
we conclude that

\displaystyle L(f/K,\chi,r)\neq 0\quad

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}}}(\breve{f},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})(\zeta^{2}(1+p)^{2j}-1,\mathbf{v}^{2}-1)\neq 0

for a suitable choice of test vectors
(\breve{f},\breve{{\bm{g}}},\breve{{\bm{h}}})
. Denoting by
Q
the specialisation
W\mapsto\zeta^{2}(1+p)^{2j}-1
, from Theorem
6.4.1
and Remark
4.3.2
we conclude that

L(f/K,\chi,r)\neq 0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\rm res}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}(\kappa_{2}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1})_{Q})\neq 0.

Since by construction
\kappa_{2}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1})_{Q}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,V_{f,\chi})
is the base class of the anticyclotomic Euler system

\bigl\{{}^{\mathbf{c}}z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K[m],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}})\;\colon\;\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{N}\bigr\}

as in the proof of Theorem
3.3.1
, with
(\psi_{1},\psi_{2})
the Hecke characters corresponding to
({\bm{g}}_{S_{1}},{\bm{h}}_{S_{2}})
, by Theorem
3.3.1
we deduce that
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,V_{f,\chi})
is one-dimensional, spanned by

{}^{\mathbf{c}}z_{f,\chi}:={\rm cor}_{K[1]/K}({}^{\mathbf{c}}z_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},(1)})=\kappa_{2}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}-1})_{Q}.

The vanishing of the Bloch–Kato Selmer group
{\rm Sel}_{\rm BK}(K,V_{f,\chi})={\rm Sel}_{{{\rm rel},{\rm str}}}(K,V_{f,\chi})
(see Lemma
3.1.2
) now follows from this and the nonvanishing of
{\rm res}_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}}({}^{\mathbf{c}}z_{f,\chi})
using global duality (cf.
[
CH18a
, Thm. 7.9]
).
∎

6.6.
On the Iwasawa main conjecture

We conclude by giving an application to the Iwasawa–Greenberg main conjecture for
\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\chi)
. As before, we put

A_{f,\chi}={\rm Hom}_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}(T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\chi^{-1},\mu_{p^{\infty}}).

Theorem 6.6.1
.

Let the hypotheses be an in Theorem
6.5.1
, and assume in addition that
f
has big image. Then
{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K,A_{f,\chi})
is cotorsion over
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
, and we have the divisibility

{\rm char}_{\Lambda_{K}^{-}}\bigl({\rm Sel}_{{{\rm str},{\rm rel}}}(K,A_{f,\chi})^{\vee}\bigr)\supset\bigl(\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\chi)\bigr)

in
\Lambda_{K}^{-,\rm ur}\otimes_{\mathbf{Z}_{p}}\mathbf{Q}_{p}
.

Proof.

Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
6.5.1
we get the factorisation

\mathscr{L}_{p}^{{\bm{g}}}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}})((1+W)^{2}-1,\mathbf{v}^{2}-1)=\pm\mathbf{w}\cdot\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\beta^{2})(W)\cdot\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\chi)(W)

where the auxiliary ring class character
\beta
can be chosen, by virtue of
[
Hsi14
, Thm. C]
, so that the factor
\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\beta^{2})
is a unit in
\Lambda_{K}^{-,{\rm ur}}
and conditions (i)–(iii) in Lemma
6.2.2
hold. By Theorem
6.1.1
the second factor
\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm BDP}(f/K,\chi)
is nonzero, from Theorem
6.4.1
we conclude that the resulting class

\kappa_{2}(f,{\bm{g}},{\bm{h}}_{\mathbf{v}^{2}}-1)\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K,T_{f}^{\vee}(1-r)\otimes\xi_{1}^{-1}\xi_{2}^{-1}\Psi_{W}^{1-\mathbf{c}})

is non-torsion. Since by construction this class is the base of a
\Lambda_{K}^{-}
-adic anticyclotomic Euler system

\bigl\{{}^{\mathbf{c}}\mathbf{z}_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2},\mathfrak{m}}\in{\rm Sel}_{{{\rm ord},{\rm ord}}}(K[mp^{\infty}],T_{f,\psi_{1},\psi_{2}^{\mathbf{c}}})\;\colon\;\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{N}\bigr\}

as in the proof of Theorem
3.3.3
, the result follows from Theorem
3.3.3
, Proposition
4.3.3
, Proposition
6.3.1
, and Theorem
6.5.1
.
∎

Remark 6.6.2
.

Note that Theorem
6.6.1
also yields a proof of a divisibility towards the Perrin-Riou main conjecture for generalised Heegner cycles formulated in
[
LV19
]
(see
[
BCK21
, Thm. 5.2]
for the argument), removing some of the hypotheses in the main result of
[
LV19
]
.
